# Backend design v9 review

Status: reviewed 2026-09-17 in the worktree `/Users/zliu/Workspace/G5-Lean-v9`, branch `docs/backend-design-v9`, commit `33154ac`. Documents covered: [lean-backend-design-v9.md](lean-backend-design-v9.md), [workspace-vm-api-v9.md](workspace-vm-api-v9.md), [worker-host-api-v9.md](worker-host-api-v9.md), [agent-api-surface-v9.md](agent-api-surface-v9.md), [workspace-sharing-v9.md](workspace-sharing-v9.md), [v9-meeting-decisions.md](v9-meeting-decisions.md), [lean-implementation-plan-v9.md](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) and [lean-implementation-status-v9.md](lean-implementation-status-v9.md), with targeted inspection of the retained code the documents cite. This is a design review: nothing from design §5.4 onward is implemented yet, so every finding is about the text, its internal consistency, its grounding in retained code and its sufficiency as a specification for AI-agent implementation, not about runtime behavior. Findings N1 to N108 were produced by twelve independent reviewers and N109 to N125 by a second round on host registration and identity, the repository-selected image and runtime directory, inbound provider mirroring, and the nudge and its notices; each finding below survived an independent evidence check against the cited lines and a materiality check. Nothing in the worktree was edited except creating this file.

## Recommendation

Revise before acceptance. Of the 125 findings (10 P1, 77 P2, 38 P3), the ten P1 findings are not wording defects; each leaves a boundary that an AI agent implementing the named PRs would have to invent, and four of them (N3, N5, N6, N8) are the v8 security findings S1, S2, S3 and the passt/metadata item reappearing without a mechanism or an owning test, which N9 records as a whole; the tenth (N109) is a new v9 boundary, the self-hosted and macOS host join, whose only retained mechanism is a reusable token that a later MVP PR deletes. Before acceptance the design and plan must: give `workspace_requests.task_id` a single writer, a UNIQUE/FK constraint and a designation rule (N1); make the flush capture `refs/heads/<branch>` and not only `HEAD`, with a restore rule for detached or foreign-branch HEADs (N2); define how the API authenticates the sending execution instead of trusting `G5D_EXEC_ID` from a shared proxy secret (N3); make `lease_ttl_secs` a platform value bounded below by the control-plane deploy budget and defer lease-expiry settlement across a reconnect grace (N4); decide preview origin isolation fail-closed or record it as an accepted risk with a P5.1 gate (N5); state an upload-pack policy per ref namespace and either accept or filter cross-workspace ref exposure (N6); retarget the g5-ci-e2e lane steps in the PRs that delete the per-task workers rather than in P8.6 (N7); specify a host-enforced guest egress policy (N8); give self-hosted and macOS host registration a single-use, expiring token exchanged once for the host credential, in a table P4.9b does not drop (N109); and record in the plan that the N6, N5 and N8 regressions are owned by MVP PRs and that P8.8 verifies rather than proposes them (N9). The 77 P2 findings can be handled as amendments to the affected PR prompts and the sections they cite (data-model homes for the Start/Resume authorization, `vm.event` ingestion, mirror freshness, the nudge window and its notices and the continuation input; status and enumeration vocabularies; size, retention and rate bounds; image selection, allowlist and runtime-directory provenance; the MVP-window inbound mirroring path; the P4.5 and P4.1 splits; the CI-lane and QA-observation policies), provided each amendment lands before the owning PR starts and names its regression; the 38 P3 findings are mostly vocabulary, cross-reference and schedule corrections that the editing PR can carry, plus five mechanism gaps (N82, N84, N99, N100, N101) that need a design edit before their owning PR starts and one coverage item (N125) that lands with N109 and N123. Verdict: the v9 design is structurally sound and unusually well grounded, but it is not yet ready for unattended AI-agent implementation, because the P1 gaps sit on the security and durability boundaries that the plan's acceptance matrix claims to test.

### N1 — [P1] Designated producer workspace_requests.task_id has no writer or designation rule

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 218–219; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 448, 452, 454; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 684; [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 706; [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 63; [agent-api §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 205, 230; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 509; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 711; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 930.

**Evidence**: design line 219: "task_id NULL, selected_submission_id NULL, status pending|running|completed|failed|cancelled,". Design line 452: "`workspace_requests.task_id` names the designated final result-producing task." Design line 448: "require the designated producer’s scoped current execution acting for the child owner". Agent-api line 230: "the service resolves and validates the child/request/source binding before dispatch". Plan line 930: "Present explicit Start review/explainer or Start workspace and chat intent before attachment".

**Problem**: Observation: every mention of the producer presupposes it is already designated. The schema has a nullable `task_id` with no UNIQUE or FK; §5.9, P4.6 1a and the submit route attach the publication barrier and authorization to it; agent-api §5 binds a `workspace_request_id` to a turn but never says that binding writes `task_id`, whether a bound task is the producer or a prerequisite, or what happens when several tasks carry one request id. P4.14 item 2 offers two entry paths (templated Start review/explainer, typed Start workspace and chat) and §7.2 line 706 ("Distinct sessions may run concurrently") permits concurrent sessions in the child. Line 454 calls a producer that ends without a valid candidate a "recoverable held/publication failure", but the only recovery in the text (line 684) is a follow-up that "creates a new `agent` task", which is not the designated task. Consequence: P4.1, P4.6, P4.8 and P4.14 must each guess which write sets `task_id`, when, whether a typed chat task can be the producer, and whether designation can move. Divergent guesses make `g5d pr result submit` from the templated task fail with an authority error, or leave a request nonterminal with no eligible producer so completion-driven cleanup never fires and the P4.14 acceptance row cannot be demonstrated.

**Change**: Add to design §5.9 "Result authority", mirrored in [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 49, agent-api §2.4 line 63, P4.1 item 12, P4.6 1a, P4.8 item 7 and P4.14 items 2/3/5: `workspace_requests.task_id` is written exactly once by the Workspace service in the transaction that creates the templated producer `agent` task from the child owner's explicit Start review/explainer (profile kind chosen by `request.kind`); the column is UNIQUE and FK `tasks(id)`. Any other child task (typed chat, job prerequisites, follow-ups) may carry `workspace_request_id` as provenance only; submit from it fails with `RESULT_NOT_DESIGNATED_PRODUCER`. A producer that settles finally without a valid selected candidate sets the request `failed`; retry is a new request/child. A never-started request stays `pending` and is cancellable by requester or reviewer; a second Start review/explainer returns the existing task. Add UNIQUE/FK to the §5.1 DDL.

**Validation to add**: P4.14 tests at the request/TaskService boundary: (1) Start review creates the producer task and sets `task_id` atomically, a second Start returns the same id; (2) a typed chat task in the same child with `workspace_request_id` gets `RESULT_NOT_DESIGNATED_PRODUCER`; (3) producer final C3 without a valid candidate sets `failed`, leaves `selected_submission_id` NULL, runs no cleanup, allows a new request; (4) never-started request cancelled by requester and by reviewer; (5) two concurrent child sessions, only the designated task's current attempt can capture/select. P4.1 migration test: two requests with one `task_id` violate UNIQUE.

### N2 — [P1] Flush pins only HEAD; unpushed commits on the workspace branch are lost when HEAD is on another branch or detached

**Location**: [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 317, 328, 343; [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 351; [design §5.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 374; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 394; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 526; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 578; [workspace-vm-api §5](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 155.

**Evidence**: design line 317: "Snapshot a commit `S` whose tree is the working tree and whose parent is the local `HEAD`". Line 328: "Because `S` descends from the local `HEAD`, the push carries every unpushed local commit." Line 351: "set the local branch to the receipt's `local_head_oid`: `git checkout -B <branch> <local_head_oid>`". Line 343: "Work committed locally but not pushed, and uncommitted edits, survive every graceful teardown whose flush completed". Line 374: "{ repo: \"backend\", expected: \"fix-login\", actual: \"main\", detached: false }". Plan line 578: "run exactly the recipe in design §5.4".

**Problem**: Observation: the recipe records one local OID per repo (`local_head_oid` = current `HEAD`), makes S a child of HEAD, and restore runs `checkout -B <branch> <local_head_oid>`. §5.7 anticipates HEAD on another branch or detached (the mismatch payload has `detached`), yet the flush has no rule for `HEAD != refs/heads/<branch>`, and the stop sequence never runs the §5.7 association check. Probe in isolated repos: branch `ws` with one unpushed commit, `git checkout -b scratch HEAD~1`, run the recipe and push S; the unpushed `ws` commit is not an ancestor of S, and after restore step 3 `ws` points at the scratch tip and the commit object is absent from the cold clone. Consequence: a graceful stop with a complete, verified receipt silently discards unpushed commits on the mapped branch and rewrites that branch to whatever HEAD was (a scratch branch, `main`, a mid-rebase detached commit). Mid-rebase is a routine agent state at quiescence. This contradicts the §5.4 Guarantee and §5.5. Fork (line 394) pins the same `local_head_oid`, so children inherit the wrong tip. An implementer told to "run exactly the recipe" ships this.

**Change**: Amend design §5.4 steps 2–4, §5.5 step 3, §5.1 `workspace_flushes.repos`, workspace-vm-api §5 FlushReceipt, VmSpec.restore (line 526), plan P4.3a steps 1/3/6 and P4.3b: per-repo receipt becomes `{repo_id, branch_oid, local_branch_oid, local_head_oid, head_ref ("refs/heads/<name>" | "detached"), sequencer (none|rebase|merge|cherry_pick|revert), snapshot_oid, dirty}` with `local_branch_oid = git rev-parse refs/heads/<branch>`. When `local_branch_oid` is not an ancestor of HEAD, push S with `-p HEAD -p <local_branch_oid>` or push `local_branch_oid` to `refs/g5/flush/<ws>/<flush>/branch`; the API verifies both. Restore: `git update-ref refs/heads/<branch> <local_branch_oid>` (never `checkout -B` onto HEAD's OID), then reproduce `head_ref` (checkout the branch, else `git checkout --detach <local_head_oid>`), then materialize the snapshot; with a sequencer, restore leaves HEAD detached with no `.git/rebase-merge`/`MERGE_HEAD` and `workspace.changes get` reports a `sequencer` field. Fork pins `local_branch_oid` as the child branch OID.

**Validation to add**: P4.3a dispatcher integration tests against a local bare origin: (1) unpushed commit on `<branch>`, `git checkout -b scratch HEAD~1` plus a dirty edit at flush; after cold restore `refs/heads/<branch>` equals its pre-flush OID, the scratch commit and dirty edit are present, HEAD is on scratch; (2) rebase stopped on a conflict at flush; restore keeps `<branch>` at the pre-rebase tip, no `.git/rebase-merge`, `workspace.changes get` reports detached; (3) HEAD on `main` at flush; restore keeps `<branch>` unchanged and reports `WORKSPACE_BRANCH_MISMATCH`. P4.3b: a durable fork of receipt (1) creates the child branch at the pre-flush `<branch>` tip. All fail against the current recipe.

### N3 — [P1] Guest processes spoof G5D_EXEC_ID through the workspace-token proxy (S3)

**Location**: [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 448; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 561, 567; [design §6.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 614; [design §6.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 637; [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 707; [design §10.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 918; [design §11.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 956; [worker-host §6.5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 243; [agent-api §2.5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 82; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 888.

**Evidence**: design line 561: "a per-VM proxy secret minted at boot and passed only to the dispatcher (`G5D_LLM_PROXY_SECRET`), which includes it in the proxy configuration it gives every process it launches". Line 567: "`G5D_EXEC_ID` and `G5D_SESSION_ID` are plain values, not secrets | injects the workspace token (`g5_api` grant) and forwards; the API still applies the incarnation fence and the execution identity carried in the request". Line 637: "the shell runs as uid 1000 in `/work` with the same environment the dispatcher gives modules". Line 956: "with `G5D_EXEC_ID` naming the execution; none holds a token". Line 614: "uplift's bootstrap submits child tasks through the API with its execution identity".

**Problem**: Observation: one per-VM proxy secret reaches every launched process (provider CLIs, previews, the owner's terminal shell, and by inheritance anything they spawn such as a postinstall); the proxy injects the workspace token into every `/api/…` call; the only execution identity is a plain environment value. §5.9, §10.3, agent-api line 82 and worker-host line 243 build result submission, replies, bootstrap task creation and (P6.1) approval decisions on "the designated producer's scoped current execution". No section says how the API authenticates which execution sent a request, which routes accept a workspace-token principal, or how the dispatcher's token and secret stay out of reach of uid-1000 processes. Retained code has no `G5D_EXEC_ID` handling; today's per-task worker JWT is replaced by one workspace token plus a plain exec id. Consequence: P4.14 item 3 can only implement exec_id/attempt equality, which any process in the child VM satisfies by setting `G5D_EXEC_ID`. In a review child the reviewed PR's untrusted tests, builds or preview run during the reviewer's turn and can publish a forged "no findings" result, create or cancel tasks and previews on the reviewer's account, consume the bootstrap task-creation path and later record approval decisions. The P4.14/P8.8 spoofing tests cannot discriminate because no credential distinguishes executions; the acceptance criterion is unfalsifiable. The incarnation fence proves which VM sent the request, not which execution.

**Change**: Amend design §6.5 (and worker-host §6.5, plan P4.12 item 4, P4.11, P4.14 item 3) to define two guest principals. (1) Trusted-runtime principal: the dispatcher and the agent.turn supervisor, identified by a per-execution capability minted at `modules.invoke` (random 256-bit, bound to workspace_id, vm_id, exec_id, attempt, registered through the dispatcher's token) delivered only to the module supervisor, or equivalently `g5d` commands connect to a dispatcher-owned local socket where the dispatcher stamps exec_id server-side; the API derives exec_id from this and ignores header/env values. Enumerate its routes: result submit/get/list and replies, bootstrap child-task creation, receipts/harvest/lifecycle (dispatcher only), PR creation. (2) Repository-process principal (provider child and descendants, preview, terminal): model traffic and git egress only; `/api/…` without a valid capability returns 403 before any row is written. State that `G5D_WORKSPACE_TOKEN` and `G5D_LLM_PROXY_SECRET` live only in the dispatcher's environment (distinct uid, env file mode 0600, `/proc` hidepid=2). Reword §7.2 line 707: "Only the current owner, or the trusted-runtime principal acting for the current owner on a current execution, creates/cancels agent/command work"; bootstrap child tasks record actor=execution, owner=current owner.

**Validation to add**: P4.12/P4.14 regression in a booted workspace VM (docker-ci isolation lane): from the terminal shell and a fake provider child, `curl` the proxy for result submit, reply, task create, preview start/cancel, receipt/harvest with (a) a sibling exec id copied from `/proc` and no capability, (b) a guessed exec id; assert 403 and zero rows in `workspace_result_submissions`/`tasks`. The legitimate agent.turn path with the minted capability submits and is selected. From the same processes read `/proc/<dispatcher pid>/environ` and the env file; assert EACCES and that neither secret appears in any uid-1000 environment. Mutation run: make the API accept exec_id from the body and assert the spoof test fails.

### N4 — [P1] Every control-plane deploy is a fleet-wide lease-expiry event unless the lease TTL exceeds the deploy downtime; the TTL is unspecified and the deploy is Recreate

**Location**: [design §6.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 477; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 521; [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 553, 557; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 647; [design §14](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1054; [worker-host §10](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 312, 314, 322; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1197, 1204; [deployment-api.yaml](../infra/helm/g5/templates/deployment-api.yaml) lines 52–56; [values.yaml](../infra/helm/g5/values.yaml) line 46.

**Evidence**: design line 647: "the control plane deploys first and accepts the current and the previous host protocol version". Line 521: "incarnation:   { workspace_id, vm_id, epoch, org_id, owner_user_id, lease_ttl_secs, idle_timeout_secs },". Line 557: "The reconciler marks an incarnation `failed` and clears `vm_id` and `host_id` only after the lease has expired or the host reported the VM gone". Worker-host line 322: "incarnation state is in Postgres, so nothing is lost". Design line 1054: "| Single API replica | The tunnel registry and event bus are process-local". deployment-api.yaml line 56: "type: Recreate"; values.yaml line 46: "replicaCount: 1". Plan line 1204: "Acceptance: the QA rehearsal in the runbook passes end to end once."

**Problem**: Observation: the single API replica is an accepted MVP condition and the control plane deploys first. The retained chart runs one `g5-api` pod with `strategy: Recreate`, so a deploy stops the only pod before starting the new one. Lease renewal travels over `rpc` and `ctl`, the tunnel the deploy drops (line 553). `lease_ttl_secs` is a VmSpec field with no default, bound or config owner in any document (grep finds only mechanics). The host keeps lease timers running through a tunnel drop and kills the VM on expiry (worker-host lines 312, 314); the reconciler settles active executions `vm_lost` once `lease_expires_at` passes (line 477); on reconnect a VM absent from `current_incarnations` is `stale_killed`. Worker-host line 322 claims a control-plane restart loses nothing without the condition that the outage is shorter than the remaining lease. P8.7's rehearsal tests protocol skew, not VM survival. Consequence (hypothesis, mechanics confirmed): if Recreate downtime exceeds any VM's remaining lease, every host kills every VM (`lease_expired_killed`), unflushed work is lost back to the last receipt, live native questions are invalidated and every owner must Resume. P4.4b/P4.5 agents choose the TTL with no guidance while the same value is the replacement-admission wait (line 557), which pulls toward a short value; the wrong choice passes P8.7 acceptance.

**Change**: Design §6.4 (Lease bound): make `lease_ttl_secs` a platform config (not an org setting) with the default of N99's timing table (300 s) and the invariant `lease_ttl_secs >= 2 x control_plane_deploy_budget_secs`, the budget a named platform value (120 s) measured by P8.7; state that heartbeat loss never shortens a lease. Worker-host §10 row "Control plane restarts" and design §14: "nothing is lost while the restart is shorter than the remaining lease; a longer outage is the documented ungraceful-loss case". Design §6.1 and plan P4.5 item 3: on process start the reconciler defers lease-expiry settlement for one reconnect grace (3 x heartbeat_secs) so a host that reconnects with a live VM in `host.hello` wins over a `lease_expires_at` that passed during the control plane's own downtime; a reported `lease_expired_killed` settles immediately. Plan P4.4b item 3 and P4.5 item 1: read `lease_ttl_secs` from platform config. Plan P8.7 item 4: the runbook measures Recreate downtime in QA and refuses rollout above the budget; §13 post-MVP list: multi-replica API with a trigger.

**Validation to add**: P8.7 QA rehearsal: restart `g5-api` while N fake-provider turns run, one workspace is mid stop-sequence after flush start, one has a live native question and one start is `queued_for_capacity`; assert zero `lease_expired_killed`/`stale_killed` events, turns continue, the receipt completes, the question stays answerable and the queued start is placed. Rust integration test (P4.5, fake host on `ctl`): stop the control plane with `lease_expires_at` in the past by less than the grace, reconnect the host listing the VM, assert the row stays `running` and the lease is re-extended; negative: outage longer than `lease_ttl_secs`, assert the host kills the VM, `unflushed_loss_at` is set and the loss notification is sent. Record deploy downtime as a P8.7 runbook number.

### N5 — [P1] Preview relay still serves untrusted code on the platform origin (S2)

**Location**: [design §6.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 623, 631; [design §17.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 1130–1138; [v8 security review S2](lean-backend-design-v8-security-review.md) line 34; [decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 45; [plan P5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 957; [PreviewBrowserView.tsx](../frontend/src/components/preview/PreviewBrowserView.tsx) line 608; [preview.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/preview.rs) line 340; [main.rs](../backend/g5-api/src/main.rs) lines 591–593.

**Evidence**: design line 631: "with relay code carried forward from `routes/preview.rs`, target resolution keyed by workspace, current preview execution and port, a workspace-scoped ticket cookie, and optional subdomain mode" and, same line, "Browser-origin and untrusted-content isolation still require the historical security review gates; owner-only access alone does not close those findings." Security review line 34: "Make an isolated preview origin mandatory for shared deployments, including self-hosted installations, and fail closed when it is unavailable." Plan line 957: "Retain HTTP/WebSocket relay at /api/v1/workspaces/:id/preview/:port/*". PreviewBrowserView.tsx line 608: "LEGACY SAME-ORIGIN (item 3): deliberately NO sandbox attribute and". preview.rs line 340: "The legacy cookie is set in BOTH modes so the legacy path". main.rs line 593: "if state_for_preview_host.preview_host_suffix.is_some() {" (preceded by "A pure pass-through when the suffix is not configured").

**Problem**: Observation: §6.8 keeps subdomain mode optional and carries forward code whose same-origin legacy mode omits the iframe sandbox and sets a same-origin ticket cookie in both modes; the suffix is optional with a pass-through fallback. The design admits the isolation gates are "still required", but §17.2 lists only preview write isolation, §14 has no risk row, §15 and the plan acceptance matrix test owner-only access only, the P5.1 prompt never mentions an isolated origin or fail-closed behaviour, and P8.8 audits after the MVP. Consequence: an agent implementing P5.1 as written ships previews that run repository-controlled JavaScript (agent-written code, third-party dependencies) on the platform origin whenever `G5_PREVIEW_HOST_SUFFIX` is unset, which is the default and the self-hosted default. Owner-only viewing removed the reader crossing but not the primary one: the viewing owner (or an admin who just took ownership) holds a platform session with authority over other workspaces and org settings, and same-origin code can call token refresh and any API with it. The v8 P1 finding remains open with no owner, acceptance row or deferral record while the MVP passes its matrix.

**Change**: Decide in v9 text, either (a) amend design §6.8 to require an isolated per-(workspace, port) origin (`https://<workspace>-<port>.<G5_PREVIEW_HOST_SUFFIX>/`), make the relay return `503 PREVIEW_ORIGIN_UNCONFIGURED` when the suffix is unset except behind `G5_PREVIEW_ALLOW_SAME_ORIGIN_DEV=1`, set the ticket cookie only on the preview host, keep platform auth cookies off preview origins, and delete the same-origin legacy branch of PreviewBrowserView and the relay; add this to P5.1 item 5, a "Preview origin isolation" row to plan §7.1 and design §15, a §14 risk row, and require the suffix in the self-hosted bundle (P9.1) and QA Terraform; or (b) add a §17.2 entry "Preview browser-origin isolation: MVP accepts owner-only same-origin previews; must land in <PR> before the first shared or self-hosted deployment" with a §14 risk row naming the platform-session exposure. In both cases replace "optional subdomain mode" in §6.8 with the chosen rule.

**Validation to add**: P5.1 (or the owning PR) Playwright/API regression: a fixture preview page attempts `window.parent` DOM/localStorage access, `fetch` of `/api/v1/auth/refresh` and `/api/v1/workspaces` with credentials, `document.cookie` writes and `navigator.serviceWorker.register`; run embedded and via Open in new tab as an owner of a second workspace; assert each attempt fails with a cross-origin SecurityError and the API access log shows no request carrying the owner session from the preview origin. Deployment test: start `g5-api` without `G5_PREVIEW_HOST_SUFFIX` and assert `GET /api/v1/workspaces/:id/preview/:port/` returns 503 `PREVIEW_ORIGIN_UNCONFIGURED`. Mutation run: re-enable the same-origin fallback and assert the refresh probe succeeds, failing the test.

### N6 — [P1] Repo-level upload-pack exposes other workspaces' branches and flush snapshots (S1)

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 258; [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 272, 285, 297; [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 852; [design §10.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 922; [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 15; [decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 45; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 585, 589; [git.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/git.rs) lines 421–470, 548, 592.

**Evidence**: design line 852: "| non-owner, any VM state | selected complete flush snapshot in internal Git; otherwise branch head |". Line 297: "`git-upload-pack` requires workspace read access (§5.1)". Sharing line 15: "| Clone/fetch authorized repository refs | yes | yes |". git.rs line 421: "pub async fn require_read(&self, state: &Arc<AppState>, repo_id: Uuid)"; line 445: "for target in targets {"; line 548: "// Spawn `git <subcommand> --stateless-rpc --advertise-refs <repo>`."

**Problem**: Observation: internal Git is the only read source for non-owners and flush refs (which carry dirty edits and unpushed commits) are the default reader snapshot. Read authorization for upload-pack is stated at workspace granularity. The retained handler grants read when the caller can access any workspace that uses the repository (git.rs lines 439–455), then `info_refs` and `upload_pack` advertise and serve the whole bare repository, including every other workspace's `refs/heads/<branch>` and `refs/g5/flush/<other workspace>/…`, with no hideRefs, namespace or allowAnySHA1 configuration. The v8 review filed this as S1; the decision record says Git object confidentiality is not established; P4.3a item 7 says internal git reads remain unchanged; §17.2/§17.3 do not defer it. Consequence: a repository is shared by its org-visible base workspace and every fork. Any org member who reads one of them, or the incarnation token of any VM on that repo, can `git fetch origin 'refs/heads/*' 'refs/g5/flush/*'` and obtain an invite_only workspace's branch history and flushed uncommitted tree while the file/tree APIs deny the same content. `visibility=invite_only` and `workspace_members` (kept at §10.4) are unenforceable at the boundary the design makes authoritative for reader content, and the §7.1 "Reads" row passes because it tests HTTP file routes only.

**Change**: Amend design §5.3 with an "Upload-pack policy" table beside the receive-pack table (ref namespace x user reader / workspace incarnation / API identity) and choose: (a) repository-wide readability, stated in §5.1 and §10.4 as exposing every branch and flush ref of every workspace on the repository to any reader of any of them, with `visibility=invite_only` governing metadata/UI only, plus a §14 risk row; or (b) per-caller ref filtering: `info/refs` and `upload-pack` run with `-c transfer.hideRefs=refs/` and `!refs/heads/<protected>`, `!refs/heads/<branch of each readable workspace>`, `!refs/g5/flush/<those workspace ids>/` (incarnation tokens see only their own workspace's refs plus protected refs), with `uploadpack.allowAnySHA1InWant=false` and `uploadpack.allowReachableSHA1InWant=false`; state that hideRefs is advertisement-level and object confidentiality across workspaces sharing a bare repo is a documented residual risk or needs a separate object store. Assign to P4.3a item 0 with the receive-pack owner, add "authorized ref set" to the §7.1 "Reads" row and a §14 risk row.

**Validation to add**: API integration test (routes/git.rs, real bare repo): repo R with an org-visible base workspace, org-visible workspace A and invite_only workspace B excluding user U; commit `secret.txt` on B's branch and flush a dirty `secret2.txt` into `refs/g5/flush/<B>/<id>`. As U's JWT and as A's incarnation token: `git ls-remote` lists neither B's branch nor its flush refs; fetches of `refs/heads/<B>`, `refs/g5/flush/<B>/*` and B's known OID fail; A's branch and protected refs still fetch. Mutation run: remove the hideRefs config and assert failure at ls-remote. Under (a) the test asserts the documented exposure and the metadata rule instead.

### N7 — [P1] g5-ci-e2e lane depends on per-task workers that P4.9a/P4.9b delete while lane edits are reserved to P8.6

**Location**: [cloudbuild-ci-e2e.yaml](../infra/ci/cloudbuild-ci-e2e.yaml) lines 1750, 1781, 1818 (siblings 2143, 2177, 2259, 2378); [plan §2.11](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 31; [plan P4.4a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 633; [plan P4.9a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 793–794, 800; [plan P4.9b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 815; [plan P8.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1176; [design §16.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1091.

**Evidence**: cloudbuild-ci-e2e.yaml line 1750: "host-up"; line 1818: "\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM workers WHERE org_id = '$$ORG_ID' AND status != 'terminated';\"". Plan line 815: "1. API: a migration dropping `workers`, `worker_tokens`, `worker_session_generation`". Plan line 800: "docker-ci passes with the worker lanes replaced by dispatcher lanes." Plan line 31: "changes to the CI lanes are P8.6 and P8.7 only, except where a prompt names a lane it must adjust". Design line 1091: "Both `g5-ci-rust` and `g5-ci-e2e` must pass on the current PR head before merging". Plan line 1176: "prune infra/ci/cloudbuild-*.yaml of retired lanes (worker per-task lanes, golden canaries, customer images, CRDT lanes)".

**Problem**: Observation: the e2e lane starts `g5d host-up` per-task hosts, then blocks in `wait-workers` polling the `workers` table (exit 1 after 30 attempts) before seeding fixtures and running rust-e2e and Playwright, which `waitFor` that step. P4.9a deletes the per-task assignment loop, `g5d worker` and the listener; P4.9b drops `workers` and the registration, heartbeat, poll, claim and ack routes. P4.9a's acceptance names only docker-ci; the only prompt allowed to prune "worker per-task lanes" is P8.6 in wave 13; "host-up" appears nowhere in the plan or design. P4.4a's acceptance ("the old host JWT path is gone", line 633) plausibly breaks the same step earlier. §2.5 (update retained fixtures in the retiring PR) is a partial counter-clause but does not override the explicit §2.11 reservation. Consequence: after P4.9b the lane cannot start its stack, so it is red on that head and on every later MVP PR (P4.10, P4.14, P2.4). §2.9 and §16.1 forbid merging on a red lane and §2.11 forbids the PR from fixing it. Either the gate is silently waived, removing all end-to-end evidence for the new value loop, or the MVP stalls. The same deadlock threatens P4.7 and P4.8 if an e2e stage depends on the paths they remove.

**Change**: Plan §2.11: add "A PR that retires a component the Cloud Build lanes start, wait on or seed (for example the per-task host, `g5d worker`, the `workers` table) must retarget those lane steps in the same PR; that edit is in scope and is not a P8.6 change." Plan P4.4a: add a Do item "retarget the g5-ci-e2e start-workers/wait-workers steps from host JWT registration onto the new `ctl` hello/heartbeat readiness (poll the host registry, not `workers`)". Plan P4.9a/P4.9b: add a Do item "rewrite cloudbuild-ci-e2e.yaml start-workers, start-value-loop-worker, start-rust-e2e-worker, start-pa-worker and their wait-* steps onto `g5d host` plus workspace-VM readiness; remove every `workers`-table query" and extend the acceptance to "both Cloud Build lanes green on the PR head".

### N8 — [P1] No host-enforced guest egress policy; passt exposes metadata and host listeners

**Location**: [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 561; [design §6.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 605; [design §17.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1127; [worker-host §6](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 175, 177; [worker-host §13](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 363; [v8 security review](lean-backend-design-v8-security-review.md) lines 82, 84; [passt.rs](../backend/g5d-isolation/src/isolation/passt.rs) lines 84–85, 201, 289; [main.tf](../infra/terraform/modules/worker-host/main.tf) line 40; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 637–660; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 876–896.

**Evidence**: worker-host line 177: "Because every VM's passt maps the door to the same host `127.0.0.1`, the host cannot tell VMs apart by source address." passt.rs line 201: "\"--map-host-loopback\".into(),"; line 84: "the per-VM policy socket, zero rules"; line 289: "D51 attribution structure (zero rules at parity)". Security review line 84: "Assign the validation to P4.4b/P4.12 before VM admission is enabled. Specify a fail-closed policy". main.tf line 40: "# Read ONLY the worker registration token secret."

**Problem**: Observation: v9 keeps `--map-host-loopback 169.254.1.2`, which maps the whole host loopback (every port) into the guest, and the retained launch installs zero network rules; the only host-side guard named is the per-VM proxy secret on the G5 listener. "Network egress policy" appears only as a module capability (design line 605) that the dispatcher intersects, and the dispatcher runs inside the guest next to the uid-1000 code it constrains. grep of the design, companions and plan for egress, metadata, 169.254.169, nftables, no-map-host, link-local or RFC1918 finds no host-enforced policy; the P4.4b and P4.12 prompts contain no egress or metadata requirement; §17 lists only the per-VM door address question. The v8 review asked P4.4b/P4.12 for a fail-closed policy. Consequence: an implementer of P4.4b/P4.12 ships a guest that can reach the GCE metadata server via NAT (host service-account token, which can read the worker registration secret per main.tf line 40), every co-located host loopback listener (sibling VMs' proxies protected only by their secret, the tunnel client's local sockets, self-hosted services on 127.0.0.1), the VPC and arbitrary internet hosts. Actual metadata reachability remains an unverified hypothesis, as the v8 review states, but nothing in the design or code blocks it. This is the classic cloud-escape path for a multi-tenant SaaS.

**Change**: Add to design §6.3 (VmSpec/host boot), §6.5 and worker-host §6: "Guest egress policy (host-enforced, fail-closed)". Before the VM boots the host installs an nftables ruleset keyed on the passt uid/cgroup already reserved by D51 (`G5D_PASST_UID`/`G5D_PASST_CGROUP_ROOT`), or launches passt with `--no-map-host-loopback` plus explicit `-t <proxy port>` forwards, so guest traffic may reach only the per-VM proxy port on the door 169.254.1.2, DNS through the passt forwarder and an org-configured destination allowlist (default empty for SaaS). Deny 169.254.0.0/16, 127.0.0.0/8 except the proxy port, RFC1918/VPC ranges and all other host loopback ports; IPv6 stays disabled. Boot fails with `boot_failed(egress_policy_unavailable)` if the ruleset cannot be installed; the host reports `egress_policy: enforced` in `runtime_identity` and placement refuses hosts without it. State that the module egress capability (§6.7) narrows inside this policy and is never the boundary. Assign to P4.4b (ruleset install/teardown per VM) and P4.12 (proxy port allowlist); add a §14 risk row and a §7.1 acceptance row "Guest egress".

**Validation to add**: docker-ci isolation lane test in `backend/g5d/tests`: boot a workspace VM; from inside run `curl -m 3 http://169.254.169.254/computeMetadata/v1/ -H 'Metadata-Flavor: Google'`, connect to a TCP listener the test opens on host 127.0.0.1:<random>, to a second VM's proxy port, to 1.1.1.1:443 and to a hostname resolving there; all fail closed while `curl http://g5-llm:<port>/_g5/status` with the VM's secret and DNS resolution succeed. Repeat after `vm.grants.update`. Force the ruleset install to fail and assert `boot_failed(egress_policy_unavailable)`. Mutation run: remove the nftables install and assert the metadata and loopback probes succeed, failing the test.

### N9 — [P1] Open v8 security findings have no owning MVP PR or test

**Location**: [design §0](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 28; [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 297; [design §6.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 631; [v8 security review](lean-backend-design-v8-security-review.md) line 84; [decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 45; [plan §4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 200; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 589; [plan P8.8](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1217; [plan §7.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1339; [git.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/git.rs) lines 443–467, 579–601.

**Evidence**: plan line 1339 (MVP exit row): "no reader write/credential escape". Design line 631: "Browser-origin and untrusted-content isolation still require the historical security review gates". Security review line 84: "Assign the validation to P4.4b/P4.12 before VM admission is enabled." Decisions line 45: "The historical v8 security review remains open evidence." Plan line 200: "After the MVP, in priority order. Governance and publish (P1.5, P1.6, P6.1 to P6.4); the pre-launch audits (P8.8)".

**Problem**: Observation: the v8 review defines regressions for S1 (a reader of workspace A fetching workspace B's objects from a shared repository), S2 (hostile preview JavaScript on the platform origin) and the passt/metadata egress probe, assigning the last to P4.4b/P4.12. In v9, upload-pack still requires only workspace read access (line 297) and git.rs serves the whole bare repository; P4.3a's tests cover "invite removal revokes read/clone/fetch" per workspace (line 589), not per ref; the P5.1 prompt (lines 948–975) contains no origin, sandbox or isolation Do or test; P4.4b (640–663) and P4.12 (880–905) mention no passt, egress, metadata or loopback requirement. The only plan reference to the v8 review is P8.8, post-MVP, whose validation is "none beyond the probes the documents record". Consequence: the MVP exit row asserts "no reader write/credential escape" while the three documented escapes have no test in any MVP prompt, and §15's Sharing bullet enumerates API/UI/git routes but not cross-workspace object fetches, preview-origin attacks or guest-to-metadata traffic. The QA environment runs multi-user sharing with real provider accounts on the host while known P1 findings stay unverified until an audit that only writes documents. N6, N5 and N8 give the per-finding detail; this finding records that the plan converted P1 corrections into a later audit with no owner.

**Change**: The three mechanisms are specified once each: the per-workspace upload-pack serving and the S1 regression in N6 (P4.3a item 0), the fail-closed isolated preview origin and the S2 regression in N5 (P5.1 item 5), and the host-enforced guest egress policy and the passt/metadata probe in N8 (P4.4b item 2). This finding adds only the plan-level owner change: (d) Design §15 Sharing bullet: enumerate "cross-workspace Git object fetch from a shared repository, preview-origin attacks, guest-to-metadata traffic" as tested boundaries; §17.2 no longer treats them as historical; and add to plan §4 (line 200) that no v8 P1 finding is deferred to P8.8. (e) P8.8 item 0: "verify the three regressions (N6, N5, N8) exist and pass on the exact head" instead of proposing them.

**Validation to add**: The regressions are N6's, N5's and N8's, run in the docker-ci isolation lane by their owning PRs. This finding adds the plan check (process/manual): `grep -n 'S1\|S2\|passt' docs/lean-implementation-plan-v9.md` shows each regression named in the P4.3a, P5.1 and P4.4b prompts and P8.8 item 0 reads "verify", and the §15 Sharing bullet lists the three boundaries.

### N10 — [P2] S4 still contradictory: the host companion and P4.12 build a host-keyed git credential injector that permits external pushes, while the design reserves publication for the API and defers the external grant

**Location**: [design header](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 11 and [design §0](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 28; [design §5.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 360, 364; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 533; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 566; [design §17.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1135; [worker-host §6.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 137; [worker-host §6.4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 230; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 887.

**Evidence**: design line 11: "Where this document and a note differ, this document says so in place and wins; the notes are then to be amended." Worker-host line 230: "`git push` to the internal Git service and to a connected GitHub or GitLab repository needs no token in the VM." Design line 364: "Private git dependencies on GitHub or GitLab are reached through the credential proxy with an org-level read-only grant (§6.5) or are unsupported." Design line 533: "{ kind: git_https,    target_host, source: workspace_token | secret_ref },". Plan line 887: "`/git/<host>/…` paths re-originated to the real host with the `git_https` credential".

**Problem**: Observation: the design says external publication happens only through explicit API actions with the acting user's stored token (line 360), that external dependency grants are read-only (line 364) and defers them (line 1135). The companion §6.4 still says pushing to a connected GitHub or GitLab repository needs no token in the VM; the grant shape is scoped by `target_host` only; P4.12 step 3 implements host-keyed re-origination with no repository or operation restriction; design line 566 itself allows "GitHub or GitLab for private dependencies when granted" without an operation restriction. `diff` of worker-host-api-v8.md and -v9.md §6.4 is empty, so the sentence the v8 review flagged as S4 was carried unchanged although line 11 requires differing notes to be amended. Consequence: P4.12's agent reads worker-host §6 in full and has two normative texts, one calling write-capable external injection a feature and one forbidding it; a permissive injector is the natural outcome and P1.5/P6.1/P6.2 inherit it. Following the companion, guest code (including untrusted PR code in a review child) could push to org GitHub repositories with an injected org credential, bypassing create-if-absent publication, recorded publication ownership, lease policy and any provider branch protection that G5 approvals gate; a host-scoped read grant also exposes every repository that credential can read. MVP exposure is bounded because the external grant is deferred, but the grant shape and proxy contract are built now.

**Change**: Worker-host §6.4 line 230: replace with "`git push` to the internal Git service needs no token in the VM; external SCM hosts are clone/fetch only through this proxy, and publication to a provider happens only on the control plane (design §5.6)." Worker-host §6.3 line 137 and design §6.3 line 533 grant shape: `{ kind: git_https, target_host, repositories: [full_name], operations: [upload_pack] | [upload_pack, receive_pack], source }`, with `receive_pack` valid only when `source: workspace_token`; the proxy matches canonical scheme/host/port/path prefix per repository, rejects `git-receive-pack` and non-git paths for external grants, follows no redirect to another host and forwards no credential on refusal (`403 no_grant`). Mirror in design §6.5 row "git over HTTPS" (line 566) and P4.12 steps 3 and 7. Note in P4.5 step 1 that the MVP control plane issues only the internal `git_https` grant.

**Validation to add**: P4.12 proxy tests against a fake upstream: internal push with `source: workspace_token` succeeds; with an external `secret_ref` grant for `github.com/org/dep`, `git fetch` succeeds, `git push` (`/info/refs?service=git-receive-pack` and `/git-receive-pack`) returns `403 no_grant`, a fetch of `github.com/org/other` returns `403 no_grant`, a percent-encoded path (`/git/github.com/org%2Fother/...`) is refused, and a 302 to another host is not followed; the fake upstream records zero `Authorization` headers on every refused case. P1.5/P6.2: publication still enforces create-if-absent and `--force-with-lease` against the recorded publication.

### N11 — [P2] Storage cost drivers are unbounded: flush snapshots have no size cap and no object garbage collection story, session files and task_events have no retention, host disk sizing is undefined

**Location**: [design §4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 132; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 327, 337, 341; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 546; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 671; [design §8.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 823; [design §16.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1113; [worker-host §7.1](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 276, 289–291; [repo_gc.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/repo_gc.rs) lines 1–21; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 579.

**Evidence**: design line 327: "Ignore rules and the platform excludes file suppress only untracked files. Ignored build outputs, `node_modules`, `target/`, the docker scratch disk and provider state are caches and are not flushed." Line 341: "The API prunes, per workspace, every flush ref older than the two newest complete receipts". Line 823: "128 MiB per file" (mid-line). Line 671: "task_events(id bigserial, task_id, attempt, vm_id, seq bigint, ts, kind, payload jsonb,". Line 546: "the host keeps the workspace root and scratch disk for `retain_hours` (org setting, default 24 h)". repo_gc.rs line 14: "periodically repacks repos (`git gc`) so loose objects are consolidated into". Line 132: "Filestore for the SaaS".

**Problem**: Observation: a shutdown flush pushes what `git add -A` captures; in a repository without a `.gitignore`, every untracked build output, dataset or vendored dependency becomes a snapshot commit on Filestore at every graceful stop, with no byte cap or refusal path. Pruning deletes refs only; no document mentions repack or pruning of the unreachable objects, no document says repo_gc is retained for the flush store, and repo_gc triggers on loose-object count while receive-pack keeps large pushes as packfiles, so the sweep may never run on the repos that grow most. Session files have a per-file cap but no per-session, per-org or age cap; `task_events` and `usage_records` have no retention or partitioning; retained roots plus scratch disks are kept 24 h per stopped workspace with no host disk formula, only eviction under pressure (worker-host line 276). Consequence: Filestore and Cloud SQL grow with the number of graceful stops rather than useful work; a single misconfigured repository can push gigabytes per stop; superseded snapshots persist after their refs are gone; `slot_unavailable(disk)` becomes the common capacity failure and forces drains; P4.3a's agent prunes refs and reasonably believes space is reclaimed.

**Change**: Design §5.4 Retention: after ref pruning a maintenance sweep runs `git repack -Ad` plus `git prune --expire=<grace>` on repos whose flush refs were pruned, under the repo_gc one-at-a-time IOPS bound and suspended by the §16.4 barrier; state that repo_gc is retained for the flush store and triggers on packed size, not only loose count. §5.4 recipe: the oversize policy is N32's (per-file exclusion recorded in `excluded_paths`, the total cap `flush_max_snapshot_bytes`, and the definite `FLUSH_TOO_LARGE`), stated once there; the platform excludes template ships common build-output patterns. §8.5: `chat_session_files` per-session total cap (1 GiB) and org retention with the existing new-session fallback. §7.1/§12: monthly partitions for `task_events` and `usage_records`, raw retention N days, aggregates kept. §6.3: host disk rule `slots x (root + scratch) x retained_multiple` and an eviction threshold as host config. Plan: P4.3a items 1–2 (cap and object sweep), P3.4/P4.11 (session cap and retention), P4.4b item 5 (disk rule), P7.2 (partitions).

**Validation to add**: P4.3a: the oversize cases are N32's (a snapshot over the total cap completes with `excluded_paths` naming what was left out); after three flushes the sweep reclaims space (`git count-objects -v` size-pack drops below the pre-prune value). P3.4/P4.11: a session exceeding the total cap is refused at harvest and an idle session past retention is pruned while resume works via the new-session fallback. P7.2: partition and retention migration validated on a copy of real rows under the schema recipe. P4.4b: fill retained roots to the threshold and assert eviction precedes `slot_unavailable(disk)`.

### N12 — [P2] Receipt completion verifies the dispatcher-reported vector, not the workspace's required member set or local_head reachability

**Location**: [design §4.3 invariant 7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 175; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 305, 323, 329; [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 351; [workspace-vm-api §5](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 155; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 579.

**Evidence**: design line 329: "The dispatcher reports the vector `[{repo_id, branch_oid, local_head_oid, snapshot_oid, dirty}]`; the API verifies with `git for-each-ref` on each mirror that the flush ref exists at `snapshot_oid`, then sets `complete_at`". Plan line 579: "`complete` verifies with `git for-each-ref` that every reported flush ref exists at the reported `snapshot_oid`, then sets `complete_at`". Design line 175: "a receipt is complete only when every repository's snapshot is retained by an immutable ref that the API has verified". Workspace-vm-api line 155: "completes the receipt only after the API verifies every required ref. Consumers never use a partial cut."

**Problem**: Observation: the design step and the P4.3a prompt verify "every reported" ref; invariant 7 and the companion require "every repository" / "every required ref". Nothing says `complete` compares the reported `repo_id` set against `workspace_repos`, that `local_head_oid` exists in the mirror, that it is `snapshot_oid` or an ancestor of it, or that `dirty=false` implies `snapshot_oid == local_head_oid` (line 323 says only that S is skipped when clean). The vector originates in the VM, which invariant 1 treats as a cache that never writes the database; the only completeness guard is the dispatcher's own obligation at line 305. Consequence: an implementer following the plan literally completes a receipt for N-1 repos when the dispatcher fails or skips a member but reports, and a buggy or compromised incarnation can report a `local_head_oid` not contained in the pushed ref. That receipt becomes `last_flush_id` and a fork source; restore step 3 then fails attach for the repo (§5.5 "A missing mapping/ref fails attach") on every later resume with no recovery path in the text, or a tolerant implementation restores at branch head and silently loses work; §9.1 readers and forks consume the incomplete cut. The dispatcher is platform code in the same PR and a failed push already fails the flush, so this is defense in depth at the stated verifier, not P1.

**Change**: Amend design §5.4 step 4 and plan P4.3a step 2: `complete` loads the workspace's current `workspace_repos` set and rejects with `FLUSH_INCOMPLETE_VECTOR` (row `error` set, `last_flush_id` unchanged) unless the reported `repo_id` set equals it exactly; for each entry it verifies on that repository's mirror that `refs/g5/flush/<workspace id>/<flush id>` resolves to `snapshot_oid` (`git for-each-ref`), that `local_head_oid` exists (`git cat-file -e`) and is `snapshot_oid` or an ancestor of it (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`), and that `dirty=false` implies `snapshot_oid == local_head_oid`; `branch_oid` is recorded as observed only, since laptop pushes may have moved it. Replace "every reported" with "every member repository's" in the plan and add "each member repository's mirror" in the design step.

**Validation to add**: API tests in P4.3a with a real receive-pack and mirror: (1) a two-repo workspace whose completion omits one member is rejected with `FLUSH_INCOMPLETE_VECTOR`, the row has `error`, `workspace_vms.last_flush_id` is unchanged; (2) a completion whose `local_head_oid` is not reachable from the pushed snapshot (push S with a fabricated local head) is rejected; (3) `dirty=false` with `snapshot_oid != local_head_oid` is rejected; (4) a genuine two-repo flush completes and a cold restore resolves both flush refs and both local heads. Each fails without the fix because the current spec accepts the vector as reported.

### N13 — [P2] Harvest failure inside the stop sequence has no policy and no fault-injection test

**Location**: [design §4.3 invariant 8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 176; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 335–339; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 409–418 (teardown table), 430; [design §8.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 823; [design §14](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1048; [agent-api §6.3](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 253; [workspace-vm-api §4](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 120; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 670, 676; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 709; [plan P4.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 744.

**Evidence**: design line 430: "5. **Harvest.** Session files changed since the last harvest are uploaded (§8.5) and acknowledged; the event journal is drained and acknowledged; Agents commits its stop receipt (`agents.workspace_stops.commit`)". Line 176: "A VM is released only after the flush receipt and the session-file harvest are durable, or after a deadline-bound teardown has recorded that the flush failed". Line 335: "**Failure policy.** A flush that fails (a push refused, the API unreachable, the verification failed) is retried with backoff." Plan line 676: "aborts on a failed flush for a user stop with admission reopened, proceeds at the deadline for a drain and records the loss". Plan line 744: "the durability test of design §8.7 (VM killed after transport acceptance and before harvest acknowledgment".

**Problem**: Observation: invariant 8 makes harvest durability a release precondition and step 5 adds journal drain and the Agents stop-receipt commit, but the failure policy and the teardown-reason table define outcomes only for a failed flush; invariant 8's escape clause covers only a recorded flush failure. §8.5 defines deterministic harvest rejections (128 MiB per-file cap, stale incarnation) without saying what the stop does; §14 covers only a failed restore. Agent-api §6.3 says `prepare` persists session bundles before the flush while step 5 harvests after it, and offers `workspace_stops.abort` with no rule on when the lifecycle calls it. P4.5 tests inject flush failure only; P4.6 step 0 places `commit` after harvest with no failure case; P4.7 test 4 kills the VM before harvest acknowledgment, an ungraceful loss, not a failed harvest in a graceful stop. Consequence: P4.5's implementer must choose between abort (a deterministic rejection then makes a workspace unstoppable and untransferable by its owner), proceed-and-record (needs a column and notice that do not exist) and release regardless (violates invariant 8 and silently degrades the next turn to the §8.5 new-session fallback with no recorded loss). The choices diverge for transfer: the new owner either never gets the workspace or gets stale provider sessions without a notice. A drain whose harvest fails past the deadline has no permitted outcome. No listed test catches any of this.

**Change**: Amend design §5.8: (a) add a "harvest or stop-receipt failure" rule to the failure policy: harvest and journal drain retry with backoff up to `harvest_deadline_secs` (default 120 s, §17.1); a transient failure (API unreachable, Postgres error) follows the flush policy of the teardown reason (user/transfer/idle abort with admission reopened and `last_error`; deadline-bound reasons proceed at the deadline); a deterministic rejection (per-file cap, invalid bundle, stale incarnation) never blocks release: the stop proceeds, sets `workspace_vms.session_harvest_loss_at`, marks the affected `chat_sessions` so the next turn uses the §8.5 fallback and shows a notice; (b) add a "Harvest/stop-receipt failure" column to the teardown table; (c) state that `agents.workspace_stops.commit` and the release update (step 7) run in one transaction, so a commit failure leaves the row `stopping` for the reconciler, and reconcile agent-api §6.3 `prepare` with step 5 by naming which call persists bundles and when `abort` is called; (d) reword invariant 8 to "or after a deadline-bound teardown or a deterministic harvest rejection has recorded the loss". Plan: P4.5 step 1 and test 7 gain harvest fault injection; P4.6 step 0 gains the commit-failure case; `session_harvest_loss_at` joins P4.1's migration list.

**Validation to add**: `api_workspace_vms.rs` (P4.5): inject a Postgres error on the harvest upsert during a `user` stop; row stays `running` after retries, admission reopened, `last_error` set, no `vm.stop` sent. Same during a `drain` past `stop_deadline_at`; released, `session_harvest_loss_at` set, notification emitted, next attach runs and the first turn creates a new provider session with delivered history. A 129 MiB session file during a `user` stop; stop completes, loss recorded, UI notice present. `transfer` with a failed harvest; owner unchanged, VM up, operation `aborted`. P4.6 stop-coordination test: fail `agents.workspace_stops.commit` after harvest; row remains `stopping`, reconciler tick retries and releases once. Each fails at HEAD because no policy or column exists.

### N14 — [P2] No rule for who may add or remove read invites or change visibility; on main this authority is org-admin OR member-admin role OR creator

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 198, 251, 261; [design §10.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 922; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1080; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 669; [workspace_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/workspace_service.rs) lines 4659–4690, 4713, 4732; [workspaces.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/workspaces.rs) lines 6150, 6166.

**Evidence**: design line 198: "workspace_members(workspace_id, user_id, PRIMARY KEY (workspace_id, user_id)) -- read invite list". Line 261: "| Stop/resume/restart/delete; change settings | no | yes, subject to lifecycle admission |". Line 922: "`workspace_members` grants read access only; `workspaces.owner_user_id` is the sole human writer." Plan line 669: "Membership/visibility administration must not mint a second writer." workspace_service.rs line 4713: "pub async fn can_manage_workspace_members("; line 4732: "Ok(created_by == Some(user_id))". workspaces.rs line 6166: ".can_manage_workspace_members(&mut db, params.id, inviter_user_id)".

**Problem**: Observation: the design defines what an invite grants but never who may create, remove or change one, nor who may flip `visibility`. "Change settings" is an owner capability, but settings are enumerated elsewhere (§12, §5.7) as idle timeout, deadlines, retain hours and policy defaults, not visibility or invites. The retained code authorizes membership management for org owner/admin, `workspace_members.role='admin'` or the creator. P4.1 removes the admin membership role and line 251 says `created_by` is not permission, so a literal implementation leaves only org owner/admin able to invite and a non-admin owner unable to share their own workspace. grep of all documents for invite/visibility finds only the read model, the plan sentence at line 669 and the §15 enumeration. Invitee same-org membership is already enforced (`ensure_org_user_id`, workspaces.rs line 6150) and by P4.1 same-org integrity. Consequence: P4.5 (item 0 names membership/visibility administration) and P4.10 (sharing controls) must guess whether a non-owning org admin may invite readers (an admin "reads any workspace" but must "transfer before writing"; is inviting a write?) and whether the creator keeps invite authority after transfer (the code says yes). Each guess changes the confidentiality boundary of invite_only workspaces, and the §15 Sharing validation ("owner, org admin, org-visible reader, invited reader, removed reader and outsider") cannot be written without the rule.

**Change**: Add to design §5.1 after "An invite grants read access only.": "Invite add/remove and visibility changes are owner capabilities; an org admin may also perform them without transfer because they grant or revoke read access only and never write to the workspace. The creator, invited readers and former owners have no administration authority." Add two rows to the §5.1 matrix and the sharing companion table: "Invite or remove readers" and "Change visibility" (non-owner: no; org admin: yes; owner: yes). In plan P4.5 item 0 replace the line 669 sentence with "Replace `can_manage_workspace_members`, add/remove member and the visibility update path with: actor is `workspaces.owner_user_id` or an org owner/admin; drop the `created_by` and `workspace_members.role` disjuncts; membership rows carry no role." Add to P4.10 item 0 the sharing panel for owner and org admin only.

**Validation to add**: Route tests in `api_workspaces` over real HTTP: owner and org admin can add/remove an invite and change visibility; creator-not-owner (after transfer), invited reader and outsider get 403 with no `workspace_members` change; a cross-org `user_id` is rejected; switching org to invite_only closes an org-visible reader's open event stream and their next read is 403 (ties to the P2.1 revocation test). The creator-not-owner case fails against the retained `can_manage_workspace_members`.

### N15 — [P2] RLS unspecified for new tables lacking org_id

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 200, 204, 214, 223, 227, 231, 233; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 664; [design §10.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 922; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 498–510; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) lines 5338, 8867, 8936, 10158–10163.

**Evidence**: design line 233: "workspace_vms(workspace_id PK, host_id NULL, vm_id NULL, epoch bigint,". Line 200: "workspace_ownership_transfers(id, workspace_id, from_user_id, to_user_id,". Line 223: "workspace_result_submissions(id, request_id, task_id, attempt, exec_id, owner_user_id, idempotency_key,". Line 664: "task_attempts(task_id, attempt, vm_id, epoch, policy_snapshot_ref NULL, started_at, ended_at, outcome,". Line 922: "RLS tenant isolation stays." Plan line 498: "unflushed_loss_at NULL, last_error)` with org RLS." Baseline line 8936: "CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation ON public.workspaces USING ((org_id = ANY (authz.current_org_ids())));".

**Problem**: Observation: the baseline pattern is `org_id NOT NULL` plus `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` plus a `tenant_isolation` policy on `authz.current_org_ids()` for every tenant table (111 tables, 75 with RLS, 69 forced, 83 policies; `workspace_members` carries `org_id` at line 5338; the one join-based policy, `task_completion_constraints` at line 8867, is dropped by P4.9b). The design DDL omits `org_id` on `workspace_ownership_transfers`, `workspace_repos`, `workspace_fork_reservations`, `workspace_result_submissions`, `workspace_results`, `workspace_result_replies`, `workspace_vms`, `workspace_flushes`, `task_attempts` and `provider_requests`. P4.1 says "with org RLS" only for steps 1–2; steps 3–13 and P4.14 item 3 never mention RLS, policies or grants. No generic schema test enumerates tables lacking RLS (`tenant_scope_regressions.rs` tests routes). Consequence: "RLS tenant isolation stays" is not executable for a table without the keyed column. Three PR agents (P4.1, P4.6/P4.7 task tables, P4.14 result tables) must each choose between denormalizing `org_id`, writing join-based policies the pattern does not use, or leaving the tables unprotected behind application checks, and will choose differently. The result, submission and reply tables hold org-confidential agent output and `provider_requests` holds redacted prompt payloads, so an omission regresses the baseline tenancy contract exactly on the MVP tables and would surface only at P8.8.

**Change**: Add to design §5.1 (before the DDL) and §12 a schema rule: "Every new tenant table carries `org_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES orgs(id)`, ENABLE and FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY, policy `tenant_isolation` USING/WITH CHECK `(org_id = ANY (authz.current_org_ids()))`, and the `g5_tenant`/`g5_app` grants of the baseline template (001_lean_baseline.sql lines 10158–10163)"; add `org_id` to the listed DDL of the ten tables above. `configs(scope, scope_id, key, value)` (design §12 line 984) serves org, user and workspace scopes, so state how it satisfies the rule rather than exempting it: it carries `org_id uuid NOT NULL`, derived as `scope_id` when `scope = 'org'`, the workspace's `org_id` when `scope = 'workspace'`, and the org the user setting belongs to when `scope = 'user'` (user settings are per org membership, as the retained `user_configs.org_id` and `workspace_configs.org_id` already are, baseline lines 4959 and 5306, with the same `tenant_isolation` policies at 8886 and 8910), enforced by a CHECK for `scope = 'org'` and by the writing service for the other two; the standard policy then applies unchanged. In P4.1 replace "with org RLS" on steps 1–2 with a step-0 sentence applying the rule to every table the PR creates, and add a same-org CHECK/trigger tying `workspace_requests.org_id` to parent and child workspaces and `workspace_results`/submissions/replies to their request's `org_id`; repeat the rule in P4.14 item 3 and in P4.6/P4.7 where they add tables.

**Validation to add**: `backend/g5-api/tests/schema_rls_contract.rs`: query `pg_class`/`pg_policies` for every public table and fail on any table without `relforcerowsecurity`, without a `tenant_isolation` policy or without an `org_id` column, except an explicit allowlist of the baseline's non-tenant tables; an integration test connecting as `g5_tenant` with org A context inserts and selects rows in each new table for org B and asserts zero visible rows and rejected writes. Both fail if any P4.1/P4.14 table omits the pattern.

### N16 — [P2] Pruning ignores receipts pinned by pending forks, request manifests and reader selection; row-retention semantics are ambiguous

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 210, 249; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 341; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 400–401; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 442; [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 852; [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 33; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 579.

**Evidence**: design line 341: "every flush ref older than the two newest complete receipts" and, same line, "Retained receipts keep their rows." Line 400: "Under deterministic repository lock ordering, reserve `(repo_id, name)` across all members". Line 401: "use a create-if-absent Git ref transaction to create both `refs/heads/<name>` at the pinned source OID and `refs/g5/forks/<fork_id>` at that OID." Line 852: "| non-owner, any VM state | selected complete flush snapshot in internal Git; otherwise branch head |". Line 210: "repo_manifest jsonb, source_flush_id NULL,".

**Problem**: Observation: a fork pins `source_flush_id` at step 3 and copies refs at step 4, a pending fork "survives API restart" (line 249) so the pin-to-copy window is unbounded, and a review request pins its source manifest at acceptance before its child fork runs (line 442). The pruner keeps only the two newest complete receipts per workspace with no exemption for pinned receipts and no lock against a fork in progress. "Retained receipts keep their rows" does not say whether rows of pruned receipts are deleted, so whether `source_flush_id` and `last_flush_id` are foreign keys is unspecified (§5.1 and P4.1 step 2 mark none). Reader selection is always the last complete receipt (§9.1, sharing line 33), so a reader cannot select the third-newest; only a pinned stale view could meet a pruned OID, a transient the design tolerates. Consequence: two source flushes (stop, resume, stop) between pin and copy make the pinned refs disappear; step 4's verification fails while the durable operation "resumes its durable phase" with inputs that no longer resolve, holding reservations and the name, or an implementer that resolves OIDs directly copies objects that are then garbage-collected. P4.3a's agent implements the pruner without knowledge of forks; P4.5/P4.3b's agent must guess the FK behaviour.

**Change**: Amend §5.4 Retention and P4.3a step 2: a receipt is pinned while it is `workspace_vms.last_flush_id`, is referenced by `workspace_forks.source_flush_id` of a pending|aborting fork, or by `workspace_requests.source_manifest` of a request whose child fork has not completed; pinned receipts and their refs are exempt from the two-newest rule; the pruner takes the per-workspace flush lock and fork step 3 records `source_flush_id` under that same lock before releasing it. State that rows of pruned receipts are retained with a new `pruned_at NULL` column (P4.1 step 2) and that `source_flush_id`/`last_flush_id` reference rows, never refs, so nothing dangles; a restore or fork that meets `pruned_at` fails with `FLUSH_PRUNED` rather than a Git miss. Reference this from P4.5 step 11 and P4.3b step 2.

**Validation to add**: API test at the fork service boundary: create a pending fork pinned to receipt R, complete two newer shutdown flushes of the source, run the pruner, then resume the fork; R's refs still resolve, the child receipt verifies and its refs exist; after publication the next prune removes R. Repeat with a review request accepted against R across an API restart. Migration test: deleting a workspace with pruned rows and a fork referencing them keeps FK integrity. The first test fails without the exemption.

### N17 — [P2] workspace_requests.pr_id has no MVP referent before P6.1

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 218; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 444; [design §10.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 898; [plan §4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 180, 200, 326; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 510; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 929, 931; [plan P6.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1001, 1004; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 5413.

**Evidence**: design line 218: "source_manifest jsonb NULL, pr_id NULL, reviewed_head_sha NULL,". Line 444: "`workspace_requests` owns parent/child linkage, requested purpose, source manifest, optional PR identity and reviewed SHA, and lifecycle." Plan line 1001: "Migration: `pull_requests` (design §10.2 shape including the merge-operation columns), `approval_rules`, `approval_decisions` (design §10.3); drop the old `workspace_pull_requests`, `_events`, `_provider_repos` tables". Plan line 180: "Formal approval rules/provider PR publication/merge remain P6 work; result delivery must not wait for them." Plan line 931: "a client-supplied parent/PR/SHA cannot retarget publication". Baseline line 5413: "CREATE TABLE public.workspace_pull_requests (".

**Problem**: Observation: the request schema and §5.9 treat PR review as a request kind pinning `pr_id` and `reviewed_head_sha`, and P4.14 requires server-side validation that a client cannot retarget the PR. The `pull_requests` table of §10.2 is created by P6.1, which is post-MVP (plan lines 200, 326) and also drops the legacy `workspace_pull_requests` tables that exist on main today, the only PR table present when P4.1 lands. P4.1 item 12 says only "optional PR identity/reviewed_head_sha"; P6.1 item 4 binds reviews to "the PR/reviewed SHA" but never says how `pr_id` is remapped when the legacy table is dropped. Consequence: the P4.1 implementer must guess the FK target (the legacy table P6.1 drops, blocking that DROP, or a bare uuid whose meaning flips between releases with no discriminator) and the P4.14 implementer must guess whether an MVP PR-review entry point exists and what it validates the head against (legacy table, provider API, nothing). Either an FK to a table P6.1 deletes, a `pr_id` with no integrity, or PR review silently absent from the MVP although §5.9 and the acceptance row describe it. The retargeting guard rests on server-side pinning of `reviewed_head_sha`/`source_manifest`, not on `pr_id`, so this is schema churn across two PRs rather than a security defect.

**Change**: Amend design §5.1 line 218 and §5.9 "Source and navigation" (line 444): "In the MVP `pr_id` is a nullable, un-referenced uuid reserved for P6.1; the MVP PR-review entry point is `review branch <name> at OID <sha>` for the request's member repos, pinned as `reviewed_head_sha` plus the full `source_manifest` at acceptance, with no provider PR object. P6.1 adds the FK to `pull_requests` and links existing results by (repo, source_branch, head_sha) without altering them." Copy into P4.1 item 12, P4.14 item 1 and P6.1 item 1 (add: migrate `workspace_requests.pr_id` before dropping `workspace_pull_requests`).

**Validation to add**: P4.1 migration test asserts `workspace_requests.pr_id` is nullable with no FK; P4.14 API test rejects a request whose `reviewed_head_sha` is not reachable from the pinned branch OID and rejects a client-supplied parent/PR/SHA that differs from the accepted pin; P6.1 upgrade test links an existing MVP result to a new `pull_requests` row and drops the legacy table without touching `workspace_results`.

### N18 — [P2] Request status transitions are unspecified: nothing sets running or failed, reviewer decline and requester cancel are undefined, and a request can be running before a child exists

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 219; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 440–458 (line 442); [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 47; [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 61; [workspace-vm-api §5.1](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 175; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 509; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 929, 934.

**Evidence**: design line 219: "status pending|running|completed|failed|cancelled,". Line 442: "Until acceptance, a request may exist without a usable child (`child_workspace_id` is nullable during preparation)". Sharing line 47: "`workspace_requests` owns the parent-child relationship, requester, optional `pr_id`/`reviewed_head_sha` and `source". Workspace-vm-api line 175: "atomically persists final output of the designated result-producing `workspace_requests.task_id`, immutable result, request completion and pending cleanup". Plan line 929: "A failed/pending fork admits no execution."

**Problem**: Observation: the five states are listed identically in four documents, but only one transition is defined: `completed` in the publication transaction (and, implicitly, non-completion on idle stop). grep of all documents for decline, withdraw, reject or cancel near "request" finds no reviewer decline, requester withdrawal or parent-owner cancellation of a `workspace_requests` row; nothing sets `running`, `failed` or `cancelled`; nothing says whether `running` begins at acceptance, fork completion, producer dispatch or first confirmed attempt; nothing says what `failed` means or whether a failed fork after acceptance returns the request to `pending`; nothing says what cancellation does to an existing child. P4.1 item 12 lists only the enum; P4.14 item 6 requires the UI to show "request status". Consequence: P4.1 must persist the enum and P4.14/P4.10 must drive and render it, so the implementers invent the state machine. Because `child_workspace_id` is nullable during preparation, an implementation may mark a request `running` at acceptance before the fork completes, so the parent UI shows a review in progress for a request whose child fork failed on a name collision. A reviewer who does not want the request has no defined action, so requests accumulate as `pending` with no exit or notification. The cancellation actor matrix is also an authorization surface, and the acceptance criterion at plan line 934 cannot be falsified without a defined machine.
