**Change**: Add a "Request lifecycle" paragraph to design §5.9 after "Scope and authority", in the style of §7.2: `pending` (row exists, `child_workspace_id` NULL) to `cancelled` by the requester (withdraw), the named reviewer (decline, optional note) or an org admin, or when the parent is deleted or revoked; `pending` to `running` only in the fork-completion transaction that writes `child_workspace_id` and pins `source_manifest` (fork failure leaves `pending` with the fork error surfaced, retryable with a new name); `running` to `completed` only in the publication transaction; `running` to `failed` when the designated producer settles terminally without a publishable candidate or publication is permanently impossible (terminal; follow-up is a new request); `running` to `cancelled` by the child owner or requester, cancelling the producer task under the task lock and leaving the child an ordinary stopped workspace with purpose unchanged until Keep/Delete. Each transition names its permitted principals and is idempotent. Mirror one sentence each in sharing "Review and explainer children", agent-api §2.4 and workspace-vm-api §5.1; add the transitions to plan P4.1 item 12, P4.14 items 1/6 and P4.10 status rendering.

**Validation to add**: P4.14 table-driven test over the real request service and TaskService: every listed transition succeeds for its permitted principals and is refused (409) for every other principal (requester, reviewer, parent owner, reader, org admin); illegal edges (`pending` to `completed`, `running` before a child exists, any transition from `completed`/`cancelled`) are rejected; a fork collision after acceptance leaves `status=pending`, `child_workspace_id` NULL and the fork error visible; reviewer decline is idempotent and the requester sees `cancelled` in the parent UI.

### N19 — [P2] Result payload schema, version field, size bound and digest canonicalization are unspecified, and the workspace_results column set differs across design, agent-api and P4.1

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 227–229; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 446, 450; [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 65, 74–82; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 12 line 509; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 line 931.

**Evidence**: design line 227: "workspace_results(id, request_id UNIQUE, submission_id UNIQUE, producer_task_id, producer_attempt,"; line 446: "a versioned bounded payload containing summary, structured findings or selected explainer output"; line 450: "an identical retry returns the same candidate and a different payload under the same key is a conflict"; agent-api line 65 adds "child and producing task/attempt identity ... publication identity"; plan line 931: "immutable versioned, size-bounded Postgres payloads with request/child/task/attempt/owner/source/optional-PR attribution and digest".

**Problem**: Observation: the payload is called versioned and bounded, but no document gives a schema, a version field, a numeric bound or the digest algorithm and input. The three column lists disagree: §5.1 has request/submission/producer task and attempt/manifest digest/reviewed SHA; agent-api adds child and publication identity; P4.1 item 12 adds owner, "result kind/schema", a full "reviewed source manifest" and "request-scoped ACL links". Consequence: P4.1 cuts a migration from three lists and may re-cut it for P4.14; the skill, CLI, route validator and UI renderer are written against an unstated shape; the idempotency rule at line 450 depends on `payload_digest`, and a digest over raw bytes makes a key-reordered retry a 409 while a digest over `jsonb` text makes different inputs identical.

**Change**: Add to [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) after line 79 a normative block `type ResultPayloadV1 = { schema_version: "g5.result/v1"; kind: "review" | "explainer"; summary_md: string; findings?: { id; severity: info|low|medium|high; title; body_md; locations?: { repo_id; path; line_start?; line_end?; oid }[] }[]; explainer?: { body_md } }`, every `*_md` field untrusted Markdown; validation at capture; `payload_digest = sha256(RFC 8785 canonical JSON of the validated payload)` computed server-side and returned by submit; retries compare digests. Settle one column list in design §5.1 line 227 (`id, request_id UNIQUE, submission_id UNIQUE, child_workspace_id, owner_user_id, producer_task_id, producer_attempt, schema_version, kind, source_manifest_digest, pr_id NULL, reviewed_head_sha NULL, payload jsonb, payload_digest, created_at`), copy it verbatim into agent-api line 65 and P4.1 item 12, drop "request-scoped ACL links" (visibility follows the parent per §5.9) and replace "reviewed source manifest" with `source_manifest_digest`. Reference the schema from P4.14 item 3 and P4.6 item 1a. The size bound is N20.

**Validation to add**: P4.1 migration test asserting the exact column set and constraints. P4.14 API tests: two submits under one idempotency key differing only in key order return the same candidate id and digest; a one-character difference returns 409 with one row; a payload missing `schema_version` or with an unknown `kind` is rejected at capture; a CLI → API → Postgres → `get` → UI round trip on the docker-ci fake-provider fixture asserts sanitized Markdown. Each fails without the schema and digest rules.

### N20 — [P2] Result payloads, candidate rows and replies are 'bounded' without any bound, and candidates per attempt are uncapped

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 231; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 450, 458; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 510; [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 82; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 line 931.

**Evidence**: design line 231: "workspace_result_replies(id, result_id, author_user_id, body, created_at) -- append-only feedback"; line 450: "Candidate rows are immutable and bounded"; agent-api line 82: "The server durably captures the bounded validated payload"; plan line 931: "size-bounded Postgres payloads"; design line 510: "`proxy_max_body_bytes` (32 MiB)".

**Problem**: Observation: three documents call the payload bounded but none states a byte size, a maximum finding count, a reply body limit or a cap on candidates per (request, task, attempt); the only concrete limit on the path is the proxy's 32 MiB body cap, and §17.1 lists session-file caps but not result caps. Replies are restricted to the linked parent or child owner (line 448) and retention is a stated choice (line 458), so the risk is intra-tenant growth, not a cross-tenant leak. Consequence: "bounded" cannot fail a test; a producer loop can write near-32 MiB immutable rows under fresh idempotency keys for the whole attempt, bloating Postgres and backups and degrading the reader UI; P4.1 and P4.14 each pick their own number.

**Change**: In [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) "Durability and completion" (line 450) and [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 82, state defaults and list them in §17.1 as `configs` keys: payload ≤ 1 MiB canonical JSON (`result_payload_max_bytes`), ≤ 500 findings (`result_max_findings`), reply body ≤ 64 KiB (`result_reply_max_bytes`), ≤ 20 captured candidates per (request, task, attempt) (`result_max_candidates_per_attempt`); each refusal is definite (`413 payload_too_large`, `422 too_many_findings`, `429 candidate_limit`) with no row written; the proxy body cap is not the result bound. Put the numbers in the `g5d pr result` skill text. In [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 12 add CHECK constraints or service validation for payload and reply sizes; in P4.14 item 3 replace "size-bounded" with the named limits. Do not add candidate pruning; §5.9 line 458 keeps results under ordinary retention.

**Validation to add**: P4.14 HTTP-boundary tests: 1 MiB + 1 byte returns 413 and `workspace_result_submissions` has no new row; 501 findings returns 422; the 21st distinct key in one attempt returns 429 with `selected_submission_id` unchanged; a 64 KiB + 1 reply returns 413 with no `workspace_result_replies` row; a payload at each exact limit succeeds. All fail before the limits exist because the rows are written.

### N21 — [P2] Capacity queue has no data model or persisted representation

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 438; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 643; [design §7.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 719; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 234; [design §6.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 474; [worker-host-api §2.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 50; [workspace-vm-api §3.1](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 92; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 1 line 670; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 2 line 910; [plan P4.10](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 1 line 835.

**Evidence**: design line 643: "then `no_capacity`, surfaced to the user as `queued_for_capacity` with a position and retried by the dispatch loop. Org quota is `max_running_vms`, enforced before `vm.start`."; line 438: "With no free slot, an explicit start shows `queued_for_capacity`; do not take another user's slot."; line 234: "status stopped|starting|running|stopping|failed,"; worker-host-api line 50: "`count(workspace_vms where host_id = ? and status in (starting, running, stopping))`"; plan line 835: "`queued_for_capacity` with position".

**Problem**: Observation: a queued start has a position and is retried by the dispatch loop, but that loop (§7.3) handles tasks with recorded intent, so a plain owner Start with no task has no retrier; `queued_for_capacity` is not a `workspace_vms.status`; §12 has no queue table; nothing defines the ordering behind "position", what "do not take another user's slot" means when a slot frees, how long a queued Start stays valid, or whether `starting|stopping` rows count toward `max_running_vms` (they count for host occupancy at companion line 50). Consequence: P4.5 must choose between `starting` with null `host_id` (which breaks the occupancy count) and `stopped` with a side record; P4.13 invents ordering and retry; P4.10 displays a position nothing computes; a queued intent with no expiry can boot a VM hours later with nobody present, the hidden wake line 438 forbids.

**Change**: The queue is the N25 record (`workspace_start_authorizations`, status `queued_for_capacity`; no separate table) plus the following rules, added to [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) and §12: the lifecycle service moves the row from `pending` to `queued_for_capacity` when `place` returns `no_capacity`; FIFO by `created_at` within an org; position = rank among that org's `queued_for_capacity` rows; a placement reconciler in the lifecycle service (every 2 s and on `vm.event exited`, a heartbeat with free slots, or host `ready`) offers a freed slot only to the head; a row past `expires_at` (`created_at` + `configs` key `start_request_ttl_secs`, default 900) becomes `expired` and surfaces as `start_expired` requiring a fresh Start; the owner can cancel (`cancelled`); `max_running_vms` counts `starting|running|stopping`. P4.13 owns the reconciler, P4.1 the table (N25), P4.5 writes rows, P4.10 shows position, cancel and expiry. Amend P4.5 item 1, P4.13 item 2 and P4.10 item 1.

**Validation to add**: P4.13 api_worker_hosts.rs: two owners queue behind one slot on a one-slot host; the slot frees and only the earlier request is placed while the later stays at position 1; a request older than the TTL is `expired` and a later free slot does not boot it; owner cancel removes the row; quota refuses when `starting+running+stopping` equals `max_running_vms`. P4.10: the status strip renders position and the expiry notice from the API.

### N22 — [P2] Compound 'Start workspace and …' has no API surface, persisted record or lifetime; capability tables are looser than the prose

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 234, 259–260, 266; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 438; [design §6.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 469; [design §7.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 719; [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) lines 17–18; [agent-api §4.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 129; [workspace-vm-api §3.1](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 92; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 13 line 510; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 6 line 675; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 2 line 713.

**Evidence**: design line 266: "A compound Start workspace and run action records that intent explicitly; task existence or a read is not intent."; line 438: "may record that intent, wait for `runtime.ready`, then launch the requested work."; line 260: "| ... use terminal; save/upload/delete/rename files; Push | no | attached VM and open admission required |"; agent-api line 129: "Start/Resume admission is recorded and resolved by the server, not inferred from the `reason` string or a caller-supplied claim of consent"; plan line 675: "`POST /workspaces/:id/{resume,stop,restart,delete,transfer}`"; line 469: "failed ──next resume──► starting".

**Problem**: Observation: four documents rely on a server-recorded intent that clears `owner_resume_required` after `runtime.ready`, survives `queued_for_capacity` and is read by the dispatch loop, and P4.1 item 13 mandates persisting it, but no table, column or route stores it; the P4.5 route list has no compound-start shape; nothing says what happens on `boot_failed`, on cancel during `starting`, or on stop or transfer before ready; the capability rows at design 259–260 and sharing 17–18 say "attached VM" without naming the compound route. Consequence: five PRs across three owners each encode their own intent; boot failure has two readings, indefinite 2 s re-`ensure` against a broken image or a consumed intent with the task stranded; P4.6 test 8 "launches exactly once after ready" has no contract to assert against.

**Change**: The compound Start is the N25 record (`workspace_start_authorizations` with `kind = compound_start` and `launch_task_id`; no separate table) plus the following rules in [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) "Explicit start": the only creator is `POST /workspaces/:id/resume` with optional body `{launch: {task_id}}`; chat, preview, terminal and review UIs call this route, never a flag on their own routes; the row is `consumed` on `runtime.ready` in the transaction that clears `owner_resume_required`; `failed` after 3 `boot_failed`/`spec_rejected` results (`attempts`) with the launch task held `waiting`; `cancelled` by owner cancel, stop, transfer, purpose completion or owner change (`superseded_reason`); the dispatch loop never creates one. Reword design 259–260 and sharing 17–18 to "attached VM, or via the compound Start route while stopped"; "clearly labelled" is a UI acceptance item. Reference the table from P4.1 item 13, P4.5 item 6 and P4.6 item 2.

**Validation to add**: P4.5/P4.6 tests against the real dispatch loop: a stopped workspace with a queued task and no intent row gives zero `vm.start` over 10 ticks; POST resume with a launch task gives exactly one `vm.start` and one `modules.invoke` after ready; three `boot_failed` results from the fake host produce no fourth `vm.start` and a waiting task with reason; cancel during `starting` cancels the intent and invokes nothing; a transfer committed while pending cancels the intent and the new owner gets no `vm.start` until they POST resume.

### N23 — [P2] The child receipt created by fork-copy has no valid shape under the workspace_flushes schema

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 240–241; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 394, 401; [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 350–351; [workspace-vm-api §5](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) lines 135–168; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 2 and 11 lines 499, 508; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 11 line 682; [plan P4.3b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 595.

**Evidence**: design line 240: "workspace_flushes(flush_id PK, workspace_id, vm_id, epoch,"; line 241: "reason shutdown|fork, materialization snapshot,"; line 394: "copy the selected snapshot refs under the child workspace ID, and complete its receipt"; line 401: "Copy any source flush refs under the destination workspace ID"; plan line 508: "Constrain new `workspace_flushes.reason` to shutdown/fork".

**Problem**: Observation: the fork copies refs to `refs/g5/flush/<child>/<flush id>` and completes a receipt for a child that has never had an incarnation. `flush_id` is the sole PK, so the copied receipt cannot reuse the source id; `vm_id` and `epoch` have no NULL marker (workspace_vms marks nullable columns explicitly) and no meaningful value; `reason shutdown|fork` describes the source flush, not a copy; nothing sets `repos.branch_oid` for the new child branch or says whether `concurrent_writers` is copied. Lines 394 and 401 read consistently ("any" means "if a receipt exists"). Consequence: P4.1 (constraints), P4.5 (writer) and P4.3a (restore) share the row with no contract; NOT NULL `vm_id`/`epoch` would reject the natural copied row, and independent agents will fabricate `vm_id`, mismatch `branch_oid` or copy all receipts instead of the selected one; the child's first attach depends on the result.

**Change**: Amend [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 240 and [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 2: add `origin flush|fork_copy`, `source_flush_id NULL`, and allow null `vm_id`/`epoch` only when `origin=fork_copy`; `reason` stays `shutdown|fork` and is copied from the source. Amend §5.8 step 4 (line 394), P4.5 item 11 and P4.3b item 2: the child receipt gets a fresh UUIDv7 `flush_id`; exactly the selected source receipt's refs are copied to `refs/g5/flush/<child id>/<new flush id>` at the source `snapshot_oid` per member; `repos` is the source vector with `branch_oid := local_head_oid`; `concurrent_writers` copied; `complete_at` is set in the step 5 publication transaction together with `workspace_vms.last_flush_id`. State in §5.5 that restore reads only `repos` and `flush_id`.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 fork boundary test: fork a stopped source whose receipt holds a dirty file and an unpushed commit; assert the child row has `origin=fork_copy`, null `vm_id`, `source_flush_id` equal to the source receipt, refs under the child id at the source snapshot OIDs, and that P4.3a restore on first attach materializes the dirty file with the branch ahead of origin. P4.1 migration test: constraints accept that row and reject a `fork_copy` row with non-null `vm_id` or a reason outside `shutdown|fork`. Both fail without the schema change.

### N24 — [P2] Plan's code grounding omits the actual read-access authority, which still grants the creator invite_only read access after transfer

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 251; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1080; [plan §3.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 132; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 line 500; [plan §7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1351; [scoped.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/db/scoped.rs) lines 1083–1086; [workspace_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/workspace_service.rs) lines 3147, 3207; [git.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/git.rs) line 1545.

**Evidence**: design line 251: "`created_by` is immutable provenance, not permission. ... other org members read when visibility is `org` or their invite is present in `workspace_members`."; plan line 500: "the former owner gains no special access."; plan line 132: "has `is_workspace_admin`, `can_manage_workspace_members` and `add_workspace_member`"; scoped.rs 1083–1085: `"invite_only" => { if workspace.1 == Some(user_id) { return Ok(WorkspaceAccessResult::Allowed);`; workspace_service.rs 3147: `w.created_by = $3`; git.rs 1545: `user_can_access_workspace(system_pool, db, user_id, org_id, workspace_id)`.

**Problem**: Observation: the v9 rule is explicit, but the retained read authority `check_user_workspace_access` (used by git.rs `require_read` and `scoped_db_for_workspace`) and the `list_workspaces` SQL grant invite_only read to `created_by`, and plan §3.5 names only three membership helpers in workspace_service.rs; every named test covers writes (plan 1351, design 1080) or a generic removed reader. Consequence: an agent replacing the named helpers plausibly leaves scoped.rs and `list_workspaces` untouched, so after a transfer the creator of an invite_only workspace keeps listing, file tree, chat metadata and `git clone`/`fetch`, a confidentiality leak the design says does not exist.

**Change**: Extend [plan §3.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 132: "Read authority lives in backend/g5-api-core/src/db/scoped.rs `check_user_workspace_access`/`user_can_access_workspace` (used by git.rs `require_read` and `scoped_db_for_workspace`) and in `WorkspaceService::list_workspaces`; both still grant invite_only access to `created_by`; v9 replaces that disjunct with `owner_user_id`." Add to [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 (or P4.5 item 0): "Rewrite `check_user_workspace_access` and the `list_workspaces` visibility predicate so read = org owner/admin OR `owner_user_id = actor` OR `visibility = org` OR invite row; remove every `created_by` disjunct." Add "not-invited former owner of an invite_only workspace" to the §15 Sharing actor list at design line 1080 and to the plan §7 Workspace authority row.

**Validation to add**: API test at real routes: A creates an invite_only workspace, transfers it to B through the real transfer service, A has no invite; `GET /workspaces` omits it, the files tree route returns 403/404, `git ls-remote` and upload-pack as A are denied, chat listing denied; after B invites A, reads succeed and no live-VM route is reachable. The listing and tree assertions fail against the retained `created_by` disjuncts.

### N25 — [P2] 'Recorded continuation authorization' for drain/rollout has no data-model home, and the `owner_resume_required` set-by list omits deadline-bound teardowns

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 193–245, 266; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 438; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 661; [design §7.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 719; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 13 line 510; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 2 line 713; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 0 and 3 lines 1193, 1196.

**Evidence**: design line 266: "It is initially true, and set by user stop, idle stop, completed purpose cleanup, transfer or ungraceful loss."; line 719: "require recorded, current-owner Start/Resume intent (or a still-valid drain/rollout continuation authorization) before `workspace.runtime.ensure`"; line 661: "chat_session_id NULL, continuation_pending bool, continuation_reason NULL,"; plan line 1196: "recorded continuation authorization still valid against stop/transfer/purpose-completion generation"; plan line 510: "Persist lifecycle/start authorization bound to current owner/generation and operation intent".

**Problem**: Observation: design, plan and sharing note all require a persisted authorization with a validity generation, but the §5.1 data model has no such record, `tasks` carries only `continuation_pending/continuation_reason`, `workspaces` has no generation column (`workspace_vms.epoch` changes per incarnation, not per owner decision), and line 266 never states the flag's value after a `drain|rollout|credential|capacity` teardown when no valid authorization exists. Consequence: P4.1, P4.5, P4.6 and P8.7 must each define "still-valid"; a mismatch either strands every rollout as an owner-Resume hold or lets a delayed rollout resume boot a VM after the owner stopped or transferred the workspace.

**Change**: Amend [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md): add `workspaces.lifecycle_generation bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, incremented in the same transaction as every stop release, transfer completion, purpose-complete cleanup and ungraceful-loss record; add the one Start/Resume record of this review (N21, N22 and P1-2 all use it and define no table of their own): `workspace_start_authorizations(id, workspace_id, org_id, owner_user_id, kind resume|restart|compound_start|rollout_continuation, launch_task_id NULL, operation_ref NULL, lifecycle_generation bigint NOT NULL, status pending|queued_for_capacity|starting|consumed|expired|cancelled|failed, attempts int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at, expires_at, resolved_at NULL, consumed_vm_id NULL, superseded_reason stop|transfer|purpose_complete|loss NULL, error NULL)` with a partial unique index on `workspace_id` where `status IN (pending, queued_for_capacity, starting)`, written by `resume`/`restart`/compound Start in P4.5 and by the rollout orchestration in P8.7. Define "still-valid" once: `status IN (pending, queued_for_capacity, starting) AND expires_at > now() AND lifecycle_generation = workspaces.lifecycle_generation AND owner_user_id = workspaces.owner_user_id`. Teardown rule at line 266: `drain|rollout|credential|capacity` leave `owner_resume_required` unchanged when a still-valid authorization exists (the rollout resume consumes it) and set it true otherwise. Reference from §5.8, §7.2 Interruption, §7.3 step 2, P4.1 item 13 (name the table), P4.5 item 1, P4.6 item 2, P8.7 item 3.

**Validation to add**: P4.1: fresh and upgrade fixtures create the table; a running upgraded workspace gets a backfilled authorization at generation 0. P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: `resume` inserts one authorization; a later user stop supersedes it and increments the generation. P8.7 api_rollouts.rs: a rollout of a running fake-provider turn resumes under the recorded authorization with a new attempt on the same logical task; the same rollout with a user stop or transfer injected between nudge and resume issues no `vm.start`, leaves `owner_resume_required=true` and shows held work (fails without the generation check). P4.6: a queued task on a stopped workspace with no valid authorization never calls `workspace.runtime.ensure`.

### N26 — [P2] Renewed workspace token is never installed into the host proxy's grant table

**Location**: [design §5.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 272; [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 553; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 533–534, 566; [worker-host-api §3.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 101; [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 112; [worker-host-api §5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 165; [worker-host-api §6.4](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 224–234; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 line 648; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 5 line 889.

**Evidence**: design line 272: "renewed through `lease.renew` while the incarnation is current, held by the dispatcher and by the host credential proxy"; line 566: "the internal service's grant is the workspace token itself (`source: workspace_token`)"; worker-host-api line 112: "passes the token through as an opaque blob to the dispatcher, which checks that its `vm_id` claim matches before installing it"; line 165: "the credential proxy holds the opaque token in memory solely to inject it on authorized egress".

**Problem**: Observation: the `git_https` (internal) and `g5_api` grants are sourced from the workspace token in the spec; `lease.renew` returns a new token that the host forwards as an opaque blob; no clause says the host replaces the token in its grant table, `vm.grants.update` carries grant references only, P4.12 item 5 replaces values only on `vm.grants.update` or an upstream 401, and the token's expiry is stated nowhere. Consequence: if the token expires (the natural reading of "renewed"), every proxied `git push` to the internal service and every `g5d`/MCP platform call from launched processes fails with 401/403 after the first lifetime while the VM is healthy; the tempting fix is a long-lived token, which weakens the incarnation fence.

**Change**: In [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) "Credential" (line 553) and [worker-host-api §5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 165: the token's `exp` equals its lease grant (control-plane handling time plus `lease_ttl_secs`; at boot plus `start_deadline_secs`); the API and Git service refuse an expired token in addition to the `vm_id`/`epoch` fence; on every successful `lease.renew` reply the host atomically installs the returned token as the value of that VM's `source: workspace_token` grants before passing the blob to the dispatcher, in-flight proxied requests finish on the old token, and obsolete renewal sequence numbers are ignored (same swap rule as `vm.grants.update`). Mirror in worker-host-api §6.4 and add to P4.4b item 3, P4.12 item 5 and P4.5 item 1 (mint with `exp`).

**Validation to add**: P4.12 host integration test with a fake control plane: `lease_ttl_secs=4`, renewal n returns token n; after two renewals a dispatcher-launched process runs `git push` to `/git/<internal host>/` and a `g5d` call to `/api`; the fake upstream asserts `Authorization` carries token 2 and rejects token 0 as expired; both succeed. P4.5 API test: receive-pack with an expired token is refused even though `vm_id` is current. Both fail without the swap and the `exp` rule.

### N27 — [P2] Workspace token has no audience or type claim, and the platform API route set it may reach is undefined

**Location**: [design §5.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 272; [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 553; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 567; [design §11.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 953, 956; [worker-host-api §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 155; [worker-host-api §5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 165; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 line 672; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 4 line 888; [git.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/git.rs) line 175.

**Evidence**: design line 272: "the workspace token, a JWT with claims `workspace_id`, `vm_id`, `epoch`, minted at each attach"; line 567: "injects the workspace token (`g5_api` grant) and forwards; the API still appli[es the incarnation fence]"; line 956: "Agents cannot create workspaces from inside a workspace"; git.rs 175: `let is_worker = claims.scopes.contains(&Scope::Internal);`; plan line 672: "reject a token whose `vm_id` is not the row's current one at receive-pack and upload-pack (git.rs), session-file routes ... flush routes and facade replies".

**Problem**: Observation: the claim set has no `aud`, type discriminator, `org_id` or actor; today's git handler tells workers from users by a `scopes` claim through one shared `jwt_validator`; the token is injected into every `/api/…` call from the VM, yet no section says how it maps to an actor, which routes admit it or how org-level and other-workspace routes reject it. The laptop half (JWT as today, device flow retained) is answered and dropped here. Consequence: P4.5 and P4.12 must guess; reusing the user validator without a discriminator lets any guest process, including a review child running untrusted PR code whose `g5d pr create` must work, call org-level or other-workspace routes with the injected token; omitting `org_id` breaks RLS scoping; a user JWT accepted at incarnation routes could forge receipts.

**Change**: In [design §5.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 272, §6.4 line 553 and [worker-host-api §5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 165: the workspace token carries `aud: g5-workspace-incarnation`, `token_use: workspace_incarnation`, `org_id`, `workspace_id`, `owner_user_id`, `vm_id`, `epoch`, `iat`, `exp = iat + lease_ttl_secs` (N99's default, 300 s); the user validator rejects any token carrying `token_use`; the incarnation validator rejects user JWTs. Add a table to §6.5 or §9.1 listing the routes that admit the incarnation principal (internal git for the mapped branch and flush refs, `lease.renew`, receipt/harvest/session-file routes, task event/outcome/question routes bound to `G5D_EXEC_ID`, `g5d pr create|status|result submit`, `g5d task|preview|ports`, workspace-scoped `g5-tools`), each resolved to the current owner as actor and bound to the token's `workspace_id`; every other route answers 401. Amend P4.5 item 3 (mint this set, add the matrix to the fence) and P4.12 item 4 (inject only this kind).

**Validation to add**: P4.5 API tests: a workspace token on `POST /workspaces/:id/transfer`, `POST /workspaces`, `POST /orgs/:id/members` and any route of another workspace returns 401/403 with no write; the same token on its own workspace's `g5d pr create` route succeeds with `actor = owner_user_id`; a user JWT on `lease.renew`, `flushes/:id/complete` and session-file harvest returns 401; a token without `token_use`/`aud` fails the incarnation validator and a workspace token fails the user validator. P4.12 fake-upstream test: the injected `Authorization` decodes to the incarnation claim set.

### N28 — [P2] Incarnation may create flush refs without a started receipt row; orphan refs are unbounded and never pruned

**Location**: [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 285; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 341; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 566; [worker-host-api §6.5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 248; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 2 and 4 lines 579, 581.

**Evidence**: design line 285: "| `refs/g5/flush/<workspace id>/<flush id>` | deny | create only (no update, no delete) |"; plan line 581: "allow `create` of `refs/g5/flush/<workspace id>/<flush id>` for the workspace incarnation whose claims name that workspace"; design line 341: "every ref of an incomplete flush 24 hours after its `started_at`"; plan line 579: "`start` inserts the row; `complete` verifies".

**Problem**: Observation: the create rule keys only on the token's `workspace_id` matching the path; it does not require a `workspace_flushes` row for `<flush id>` with matching `vm_id`/`epoch` and `complete_at IS NULL`, and the pruner is defined over receipt rows, so a ref with no row has no `started_at` and escapes both retention rules. Every guest process's push to origin goes through the proxy, which injects the workspace token (design 566, companion 248), so a provider or repository process, not just the dispatcher, can create such refs. Consequence: unlimited immutable `refs/g5/flush/<ws>/<random>` refs carrying arbitrary objects, fetchable by every reader and never reclaimed; unbounded growth of the multi-tenant internal store and an unmanaged retention surface.

**Change**: Amend [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 285 and [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 4: receive-pack accepts a create of `refs/g5/flush/<ws>/<flush id>` only when a `workspace_flushes` row exists with that `flush_id`, whose `workspace_id`/`vm_id`/`epoch` equal the token claims and whose `complete_at IS NULL`; the pushed object must be a commit; otherwise refuse with `FLUSH_REF_UNKNOWN`. Amend §5.4 step 3 and P4.3a item 1: the dispatcher calls `start` (row insert) before pushing (see N29 for who mints `flush_id`). Amend §5.4 Retention (line 341) and P4.3a item 2: the pruner enumerates `refs/g5/flush/<ws>/*` in every member mirror and deletes any ref whose id has no row, whose row is incomplete and older than 24 h, or whose row is complete but outside the retained set; `start` refuses a second incomplete flush for the workspace.

**Validation to add**: P4.3a receive-pack tests at the production route: creates with an unknown flush id, with a completed flush id, and with a row whose `vm_id`/`epoch` differ from the token are refused before receive-pack spawns; the ordered start → push → complete sequence succeeds. Pruning test: seed a rowless ref and an incomplete >24 h ref beside three complete receipts, run the pruner, assert the rowless, stale-incomplete and third-newest refs are gone and the two newest resolve. Both fail without the change.

### N29 — [P2] Retry of a failed multi-repo flush is undefined against create-only immutable refs and non-deterministic commit-tree

**Location**: [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 285; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 335, 341; [design §17.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1122; [workspace-vm-api §5](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) lines 135–168; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 1–2 lines 578–579.

**Evidence**: design line 335: "A flush that fails (a push refused, the API unreachable, the verification failed) is retried with backoff."; line 285: "create only (no update, no delete)"; line 341: "every ref of an incomplete flush 24 hours after its `started_at`"; plan line 579: "`start` inserts the row; `complete` verifies"; line 1122 defers only "the retry and backoff schedules for flush pushes, receipt completion and merge imports".

**Problem**: Observation: the text says retries happen but not what a retry is (same `flush_id` re-running the recipe, or a fresh row), who mints `flush_id` (the VM API's `workspace.flush({reason, interrupt?})` carries none) or who calls `start`; `git commit-tree -p HEAD` run twice yields different OIDs because of the committer timestamp; `complete` idempotency is unaddressed. Consequence: with a two-repo flush where repo A's push succeeded and repo B's failed, a same-`flush_id` re-run produces a new snapshot for A and pushes an update to an existing create-only ref, which the incarnation is denied, so the flush never completes under that id; user stop then aborts forever ("the VM keeps running") and deadline-bound drains record avoidable losses, on the primary durability path for every graceful teardown.

**Change**: Add to [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) after "Flushes of one workspace are serialized" and to [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 1–2: the dispatcher mints `flush_id` (UUIDv7) per attempt and calls `POST .../flushes/:flush_id/start` before pushing; within one attempt only the push and the `complete` call are retried, always with the same snapshot per repository; a new attempt after a failed one is a new `flush_id` and row, and the failed row is marked `error` so its refs fall under the 24 h prune; `complete` is idempotent (a repeat with the identical vector returns the existing receipt; a different vector for a completed id is rejected); a push of a flush ref that already exists at the same OID is a no-op accepted by receive-pack, so re-pushing the same snapshot is safe.

**Validation to add**: Dispatcher integration test with a local bare origin under the receive-pack policy: two-repo shutdown flush, inject one failure on repo B's push, let the retry run; assert the flush completes, repo A's ref was never updated, exactly one complete receipt exists and the failed attempt's row is `error`. API tests: `complete` twice with the same vector returns the same receipt; a second `start` after `error` creates a new row; the maintenance prune removes the failed attempt's refs after 24 h and keeps the complete one. The dispatcher test fails under a same-id re-run implementation.

### N30 — [P2] Admission is four overlapping barriers with no state machine: owner_resume_required, human mutation admission, transfer admission and drain continuation authority

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 266; [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 293, 297; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 427; [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 709; [design §7.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 719; [agent-api §6.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 262; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 13 line 510; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 7 and 9 lines 676, 679; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 0 line 1193.

**Evidence**: design line 297: "An admitted push and transfer share the workspace mutation barrier so a push cannot complete with stale human authority after ownership changes."; line 266: "Transfer admission separately blocks all new wo[rk]"; line 719: "(or a still-valid drain/rollout continuation authorization)"; agent-api line 262: "agent_admission: \"open\" | \"closed\";".

**Problem**: Observation: the text names an in-VM dispatcher barrier (`admission.close/open`), a "human mutation admission" covering laptop receive-pack (API-side, since laptop pushes never reach the dispatcher), Agents admission (`agents.workspace_stops.prepare`), `owner_resume_required`, transfer admission and a continuation authorization, with one persisted flag among them; the API-side guard that must hold an in-flight `git receive-pack --stateless-rpc` against a transfer commit is described only as "share the workspace mutation barrier". The drain-after-transfer leak is textually forbidden (design 266, plan 1193) and needs only a test. Consequence: Workspace (P4.5) and Agents (P4.6) derive different predicates and orderings; a laptop push admitted while the dispatcher barrier is closed for a fork flush, or landing after the new owner is committed, are both plausible; the §7.1 Transfer and Attachment rows have no single predicate to assert.

**Change**: Add to [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) an "Admission barriers" table with columns barrier | persisted representation | closed by | opened by | routes that consult it | evaluation order, and rows: `owner_resume_required` (workspaces column, per line 266 and N22); transfer admission (a pending `workspace_ownership_transfers` row blocks every human mutation and dispatch until completed or aborted); lifecycle mutation admission (`status = running` AND no pending transfer AND no stop in progress; consulted by Save/upload/delete/rename, laptop receive-pack, chat send/edit/withdraw, native reply, task create/cancel, preview, terminal); in-VM dispatcher barrier; Agents admission. Point continuation authorization at N25's table. Define the receive-pack guard: a per-workspace in-flight mutation counter or advisory lock taken after authorization and released at response end; transfer's owner CAS waits for zero and a pending transfer refuses new admissions with `WORKSPACE_STOPPING`. Order: transfer pending → status → `owner_resume_required` → in-VM barrier. Reference from P4.5 items 1, 6, 9, P4.6 items 2–3 and P2.1.

**Validation to add**: API-boundary tests: (a) laptop receive-pack authorized, transfer started before the pack body arrives; the push completes before the owner CAS or is rejected with `WORKSPACE_STOPPING`, never after the new owner is committed (assert audit order); (b) running task with continuation authorization, host drain, transfer of the stopped workspace, rollout resume; zero `vm.start` and the task held; (c) fork live-flush in progress with a concurrent laptop push returns `WORKSPACE_STOPPING` or serializes after the flush; (d) schema test that the pending-transfer partial unique index exists.

### N31 — [P2] The SaaS has no consistent backup until the self-hosted phase: the barrier §16.4 requires is scheduled only for P9.3, which excludes the SaaS

**Location**: [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 303, 341; [design §14](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1038; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1079; [design §16.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1091; [design §16.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1104; [design §16.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 1111–1116; [plan §4 wave table](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 330; [plan P9.3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1308; [repo_gc.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/repo_gc.rs); [main.tf](../infra/qa/platform/main.tf) lines 102–103.

**Evidence**: design line 1104: "`just backup` dumps Postgres and the configuration; it never copies `/var/lib/g5/storage/repos`, the internal git store"; line 1038: "| Backups restore a database whose git objects are gone | Backup barrier across Postgres and the git store (§16.4) |"; plan line 1308: "Do not: change the SaaS backup (Cloud SQL and Filestore snapshots are the SaaS path and are documented, not scripted, here)."; line 330: "| 11 | P9.1, then P9.2 and P9.3 | Platform | the self-hosted phase |"; design line 303: "Pushed commits are never lost, whatever happens to the VM."

**Problem**: Observation: §16.4 documents that a database snapshot restored beside a git store captured at another time names receipts, branches and merge results whose objects are gone, and that convergence cannot recreate them; §14 marks the risk mitigated; the only PR delivering the barrier is P9.3 in wave 11, whose prompt excludes the SaaS and points at documentation that exists in none of the seven documents; main.tf enables Cloud SQL backups and PITR but nothing snapshots Filestore or pairs the two; the retained `repo_gc` and the flush-ref prune (line 341) make objects unreachable between independently timed snapshots. Consequence: production runs the flush/receipt model through waves 7–10 with no restore-consistent backup; a Cloud SQL restore after an incident leaves workspaces unattachable except by manual ref surgery; the P9.3 agent must guess whether to touch the SaaS.

**Change**: 1) Add a "SaaS backup and restore" paragraph to [design §16.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md): a scheduled job POSTs `/api/internal/maintenance/barrier start`, triggers an on-demand Cloud SQL backup and a Filestore backup, ends the barrier and writes a manifest pairing both backup ids with the barrier id; restore refuses an unpaired backup; state RPO and RTO. 2) Amend §14 line 1038 to cite both P9.3 and the SaaS procedure. 3) Split P9.3: a new PR depending only on P4.3a delivers the barrier route (receive-pack 503 with Retry-After; receipt completion, merge, fork, publish and delete refused with `MAINTENANCE_BARRIER`; flush-ref pruning and `repo_gc` suspended; auto-expire at `max_barrier_secs`) plus the SaaS job and manifest; P9.3 keeps bundle scripting and its "Do not" clause names the new PR. 4) Add a SaaS restore rehearsal to §15 line 1079, the P8.7 runbook and the plan §7 milestone table before Governance.

**Validation to add**: API test on a real git store plus Postgres: start the barrier; receive-pack returns 503 with Retry-After; receipt completion, merge, fork and delete return `MAINTENANCE_BARRIER`; the `repo_gc` tick and flush-ref pruner perform zero repacks or prunes; after the barrier ends every refused operation succeeds on retry; an open barrier auto-expires. QA rehearsal: paired backup under the barrier while one workspace is deleted and another flushed, restore into a fresh project, assert every complete receipt and branch resolves; a Postgres backup paired with an older Filestore backup is refused. Add `maintenance_barrier_seconds` and `maintenance_barrier_refusals_total` to observability.

### N32 — [P2] No size policy for snapshot pushes; an oversized non-ignored file makes a workspace impossible to stop gracefully

**Location**: [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 327, 335–339; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 566; [design §17.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 1130–1143; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 1–2 lines 578–579; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3 line 887; [git.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/git.rs) lines 43, 510.

**Evidence**: git.rs 43: `const GIT_SMART_HTTP_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES: usize = 256 * 1024 * 1024;`; git.rs 510: `.layer(DefaultBodyLimit::max(GIT_SMART_HTTP_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES))`; design line 327: "the captured set is what `git add -A` captures"; line 337: "A user-initiated stop, restart, delete, ownership transfer or fork **aborts**: the VM keeps running, admission reopens"; line 339: "Idle reclamation has no deadline: a failed flush cancels the idle stop and the workspace stays up; the idle timer restarts."

**Problem**: Observation: the recipe captures every non-ignored file (an agent-downloaded dataset, a build artifact in a repo without `.gitignore`); flush pushes reach the internal service through the proxy, so the retained 256 MiB body limit applies; no document states a snapshot budget, an oversize behaviour or a typed error, and §17.2 defers a different mitigation (object-storage snapshots). Consequence: one large file turns stop, restart, transfer, delete, idle and purpose-complete cleanup into a permanent failure retried with backoff on a deterministic 413; the VM stays up until an operator force-stops it, which records loss of everything else; idle reclamation leaks the slot silently; deadline-bound drains systematically record losses; P4.3a must guess whether to raise the limit, exclude the file or fail typed.

**Change**: Add a "Size policy" paragraph to [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) and to [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 1–2: `flush_max_file_bytes` (default 100 MiB) and `flush_max_pack_bytes` (default equal to the receive-pack body limit, which P4.3a raises for incarnation identities to that value); files over the per-file cap are removed from the temporary index after `git add -A` (`git update-index --force-remove`) and recorded in the receipt as `excluded_paths [{repo_id, path, bytes, reason file_cap|snapshot_cap}]` (P4.1 adds the column); a total cap `flush_max_snapshot_bytes` (org setting, default 2 GiB) is checked on the temporary index before `write-tree`, and when the remaining set still exceeds it the largest files are excluded in size order until it fits, recorded with reason `snapshot_cap`; exclusion is the only oversize remedy for every stop reason (a user stop never aborts on size and no `SNAPSHOT_TOO_LARGE` error exists; N11 defers to this paragraph); the receipt completes and the UI shows excluded paths on the status strip and stop confirmation; a receive-pack 413 or a pack over budget is the definite error `FLUSH_TOO_LARGE {repo_id, bytes}` and is not retried. Amend line 339 and the P4.5 idle reconciler: a failed idle flush notifies the owner once per distinct `last_error` and appears on the operator host/workspace listing.

**Validation to add**: P4.3a dispatcher integration test with a local bare origin behind the real route limit: a 300 MiB untracked file in one member yields a complete receipt whose `excluded_paths` names it, and a cold restore reproduces every other dirty file; an API test that a pack over `flush_max_pack_bytes` returns `FLUSH_TOO_LARGE` with no backoff retries. P4.5 lifecycle test: an idle stop whose flush fails records `last_error`, emits exactly one notification, and a user stop succeeds after the owner deletes the file. All fail without the change.

### N33 — [P2] Restore step 3 is not idempotent on a dirty warm root whose HEAD differs from the receipt; mid-rebase state and the no-receipt warm path are unspecified

**Location**: [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 350, 351, 355; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 546; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 3 and 6 lines 580, 583; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 5 line 650.

**Evidence**: design line 351: "`git checkout -B <branch> <local_head_oid>` with upstream `origin/<branch>`"; line 355: "the attach runs the same steps on it (steps 3 and 4 are idempotent) and removes untracked, non-ignored paths that the snapshot does not contain"; line 350: "If `last_flush_id` is null, check out the workspace branch at the internal head; done."; plan line 583: "a warm root whose file contents changed after the receipt with the same HEAD, dirty paths and sizes attaches with exactly the cold content".

**Problem**: Observation: a non-forced `git checkout -B` to a commit whose version of a locally modified tracked file differs from HEAD fails with "Your local changes ... would be overwritten" and leaves HEAD unchanged; a retained root after ungraceful loss commonly has both a post-receipt commit and dirty files; the only warm-root test keeps HEAD equal; nothing mentions clearing rebase, merge or cherry-pick state from the lost incarnation, and the null-receipt path (line 350) leaves the lost incarnation's dirty files in place. The ordering worry between clean-up and step 4 is moot because removal is limited to paths the snapshot does not contain. Consequence: the literal commands give a failed attach, leftover sequencer state that breaks the agent's next `git rebase`, or a null-receipt warm tree unequal to the branch head, on the common warm path (§6.3 keeps roots 24 h and placement prefers them).

**Change**: Amend [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) step 3 and [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3: before setting the branch, abandon any in-progress operation of the lost incarnation (remove `.git/rebase-merge`, `.git/rebase-apply`, `MERGE_HEAD`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD`, `BISECT_LOG` and related sequencer files, or run the matching `--abort`), then `git checkout -B <branch> <local_head_oid> --force` (or update the ref and `git reset --hard`) with upstream `origin/<branch>`; keep step 4, then remove untracked non-ignored paths absent from the snapshot; apply the same forcing checkout plus clean to the null `last_flush_id` path on a warm root; state that stash entries and reflog of the lost incarnation are not preserved. Replace "steps 3 and 4 are idempotent" at line 355 with "steps 3 and 4 force the tree to the receipt regardless of the root's prior state".

**Validation to add**: Warm-root dispatcher tests in P4.3a: (1) after a complete receipt, add a commit and modify a tracked file in the retained root, simulate loss, warm attach; HEAD equals `local_head_oid` and the tree equals a cold restore byte for byte; (2) leave `.git/rebase-merge` from an interrupted rebase; attach succeeds with no rebase in progress; (3) an untracked file in the snapshot plus one absent from it leaves only the former; (4) null `last_flush_id` with a dirty warm root restores the branch head exactly. Tests 1, 2 and 4 fail with the literal non-forced checkout.

### N34 — [P2] Retained roots and scratch disks outlive workspace delete and transfer

**Location**: [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 355; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 390; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 546; [design §16.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1104; [worker-host-api §1](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 14; [worker-host-api §3.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 98; [worker-host-api §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 157; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 5 line 650; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 6 line 675; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 901.

**Evidence**: design line 390: "delete the workspace branch, the flush refs and the receipts in every repo; delete chats, session files, tasks and rows | gone |"; line 546: "After `vm.stop` the host keeps the workspace root and scratch disk for `retain_hours` (org setting, default 24 h)"; line 355: "A warm retained root is a cache of ignored files and git objects, never of tracked content"; worker-host-api line 14: "it issues `vm.start`, `vm.stop`, `vm.status`, `vm.grants.update` and `host.drain`".

**Problem**: Observation: delete says "gone" but names only git and Postgres artifacts; the host keeps the working tree, ignored files (`.env`, build outputs with rendered secrets), git objects and scratch disk for up to 24 h; the `ctl` command set has no purge; transfer and repository access revocation leave the previous owner's root on disk; §16 never mentions host roots; design 355 and companion 157 address attach semantics and credential reuse, not removal. Consequence: a P4.5 agent deletes refs and rows and reports "gone" while source and secrets persist on shared SaaS hosts; P4.4b offers no command to remove a root on demand, so delete and transfer cannot honor removal even when noticed; operators cannot prove erasure for an offboarding. Bounded to 24 h and access-controlled, hence P2.

**Change**: Amend [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) commands and [worker-host-api §3.2](worker-host-api-v9.md): add `roots.purge {workspace_id, idempotency_key}` → `purged | not_found`, idempotent, deleting the retained root, scratch disk and retained `vm.stop` result. Amend the §5.8 delete row (line 390): after stop/release, enqueue a durable purge intent for every host whose last `host.hello`/`host.heartbeat` listed the workspace in `retained_roots`; the workspace row carries `purge_status pending|done` until every host acknowledges, and the host reconciler re-sends on reconnect. Amend §5.8 transfer: send `roots.purge` to the retaining host so the new owner's first attach is cold, or state explicitly that the warm root is retained because it holds only ignored files and objects the new owner may read. Add to P4.4b item 5, P4.13 (retained_roots reconciliation) and P4.5 item 6 (delete and force-delete issue the purge). Add one sentence to §16.3 line 1104: host roots are caches excluded from backup and restore.

**Validation to add**: P4.5/P4.13 API test: delete a stopped workspace whose host reports it in `retained_roots`; assert `roots.purge` is sent, the next heartbeat/hello omits the workspace and the root directory and scratch image are gone; drop the tunnel between send and result and assert the same `idempotency_key` is re-sent on reconnect and the row reaches `purge_status=done`. P4.4b host test: purge of an unknown workspace returns `not_found`; a purge while a VM of that workspace runs is refused.

### N35 — [P2] Provider namespace preflight for forks names no credential

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 190; [design §5.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 360; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 392, 399, 403; [plan P1.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 0–1 lines 348–349; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) items 10 and 13 lines 681, 684; [plan §7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1358; [git_auth_resolver.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/auth/git_auth_resolver.rs) lines 46–49.

**Evidence**: design line 399: "For provider-connected repos, also inspect the provider's current branch namespace without importing or mutating its branches; a branch that exists there counts as a collision."; line 360: "using the acting user's stored token"; plan line 349: "fetch `refs/heads/<protected>` from the provider into the mirror with the connecting user's stored token"; line 681: "A provider check is read-only; auth, availability or verification failure keeps the operation retryable and cannot be interpreted as an absent name."; design line 392: "**Forking a workspace** is allowed to any authorized reader"; git_auth_resolver.rs 47: "Only the acting user's own SCM connection may be used."

**Problem**: Observation: outbound publish uses the acting user's token and inbound mirroring "the connecting user's" token, but the fork preflight names no identity, the baseline `repos`/`repo_secrets` tables carry no connecting-user column, and the retained resolver returns `GitAuth::None` for a user without a `user_scm_connections` row. Consequence: a reader forking someone else's workspace (the MVP collaboration and review path, plan 1358) with no connection gets an auth failure, which plan 681 keeps retryable forever; the only alternative is spending another user's token, contrary to invariant 12, P1.5 item 0 and the resolver's rule. Both guesses are wrong, and no criterion bounds provider API cost per fork.

**Change**: Add a "Provider read identity" bullet to [design §5.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) and amend §5.8 step 2 (line 399), [plan P1.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 1 and [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 10: the namespace check and inbound protected-branch mirroring run server-side under the repository's recorded provider connection, a new `repos.provider_connection_id` (or `connected_by_user_id`) set when the repo is connected, never the forking user's token; the listing is read-only (ls-remote or branches API) and the token never reaches the caller or the VM; a missing or revoked connection yields `provider_unverified` with a distinct code, surfaced as retryable with the reason, and the repo owner or org admin must reconnect; the error payload is `{repo_id, provider, kind: collision|unverifiable|not_connected}`. Add the column to design §5.1 `repos(...)` line 190 and to P4.1 item 10 or P1.5's migration.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 fork boundary tests (item 13): a reader with no `user_scm_connections` row forks a provider-connected repo successfully using the recorded connection, the fake provider asserting which token was presented and that the reader's token is never queried; provider 401/5xx yields the retryable verification error; an existing provider branch yields 409 `WORKSPACE_NAME_CONFLICT` naming the provider; a revoked connection yields `not_connected`, not a completed fork. Each fails on main because the resolver returns `GitAuth::None` for the reader.

### N36 — [P2] Task-less invocations (Save, PR create) have no execution identity

**Location**: [design §5.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 370; [design §6.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 607; [design §6.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 612; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 682; [design §7.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 732; [design §9.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 866; [design §9.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 872; [workspace-vm-api §2 and §3](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) lines 45, 80; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 543; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 0 line 709.

**Evidence**: design line 370: "calls it before every UI-triggered mutation of that workspace (Save/upload/delete/rename, Push, Update, preview start, PR create)"; line 607: "`modules.invoke` is idempotent on `exec_id = (task_id, attempt, vm_id)` and returns a disposition"; line 732: "- **Execution identity.** `exec_id = (task_id, attempt, vm_id)`."; line 682: "There are exactly three task kinds."; line 866: "check the association (`workspace.changes get`, §5.7) and call `fs.write`"; plan line 709: "add `GET /workspaces/:id/changes` over `workspace.changes get`".

**Problem**: Observation: every `modules.invoke` carries an `exec_id` built from a task id and attempt, and dispositions and cancellation are keyed by it, yet `workspace.changes get|push|update|switch_back` and `workspace.diagnostics` are invoked from Save, Push and PR-create with no task, and no task kind maps to them. Consequence: P4.2b (dispositions table) and P4.6/P2.1 (facade callers) must each invent a synthetic task, a second identity type or a bypass, which decides whether these invocations are idempotent, cancellable at stop (§5.8 interrupts every module execution by `exec_id`), journaled and auditable per §6.7; divergent choices yield colliding identities or non-idempotent retries; a `get` across every member repo per autosave-on-blur (line 872) also has no stated bound, and whether a running bounded agent turn blocks Save is unstated.

**Change**: Add to [design §7.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 732 and [workspace-vm-api §3](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 80: "Two invocation classes share one identity shape. Task executions use `exec_id = (task_id, attempt, vm_id)`. Service invocations (`workspace.changes get|push|update|switch_back`, `workspace.diagnostics capture|probe`, invoked by the API on behalf of the attached owner without a task) use `exec_id = (op_id, 1, vm_id)` where `op_id` is a UUIDv7 minted by the facade per request and carried as the idempotency key; they are journaled, dispositioned and cancellable exactly like task executions, appear in `modules.history` with `actor`, are not task rows and never enter the task queue or job graph; a retry of the same request reuses `op_id`." Add the rule to the P4.2b and P4.6 prompts and note in §6.7 that `workspace.changes get` counts against `max_inflight_commands` and does not take a bounded agent slot.

**Validation to add**: P4.2b dispatcher-boundary test: two concurrent `modules.invoke(workspace.changes get)` with distinct `op_id`s both return `started` with independent outcomes; a re-invoke with the same `op_id` returns `already_finished(outcome)`; the audit tuple carries the API actor. P4.6 test: `GET /workspaces/:id/changes` during the stop sequence's quiesce is interrupted or refused by `exec_id` and `modules.history` lists it with the owner as actor.

### N37 — [P2] Stuck operational states have no operator surface or runbook

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 390, 403, 434, 456; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 512; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 546; [worker-host-api §7](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 276; [worker-host-api §9](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 306; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1197.

**Evidence**: line 403: "if cleanup cannot finish, expose operation recovery status, keep the destination unavailable, and do not claim the failed fork left no refs." Line 390: "delete the workspace branch, the flush refs and the receipts in every repo; delete chats, session files, tasks and rows". Worker-host-api line 306: "There is no host shell and no inspect command".

**Problem**: Observation: three states persist until something external acts. A fork whose cleanup cannot finish keeps reservations that block the name in every member repo (line 403); no route or rule releases them. A transfer `pending` after a crash resumes the same operation (line 434) with no outcome defined when the target is no longer an eligible member. Delete (line 390) removes refs and rows but never mentions the retained root and scratch disk a host keeps for `retain_hours` (line 546); `host.accept` and heartbeat acknowledgements carry no purge list, and the only root deletion is disk-pressure eviction (line 276). The operator API (line 512) offers host provision, list, drain and delete only; P8.7 delivers only `docs/rollout.md` (line 1197). Consequence: P4.3b must guess what happens when reservations cannot be released, P4.5 must guess the terminal state of a resumed transfer with an ineligible target, and deleted customer working trees stay on host disks with no purge instruction. A permanently held name is resolvable only with SQL against production, which §4.3 forbids.

**Change**: The retained-root purge (command, durable purge intent, `purge_status`, §5.8 delete-row amendment) is N34's and is not restated here. This finding adds the two other states and the runbook. Design §5.8 item 6: a fork operation whose cleanup failed N retries becomes `recovery_required`; operator route `POST /orgs/:org/fork-operations/:id/reconcile` re-verifies each member ref against the fork receipt and releases reservations only for refs proven absent or deleted. Design line 434: a resumed operation whose target is no longer eligible commits `aborted` with the old owner unchanged. Plan: P4.5 step 1 (ineligible-target rule), P4.3b (reconcile route), and `docs/runbooks/stuck-states.md` in P8.7 listing each state (including N34's `purge_status=pending`), its detecting query and its route.

**Validation to add**: Purge tests are N34's. P4.3b: crash after partial ref creation, fail cleanup, call reconcile; reservations released only for refs matching the receipt, a pre-existing branch untouched. P4.5 transfer: crash after release with the target removed from the org, resume; `aborted`, old owner unchanged, admission reopened. P8.7: one runbook drill per state.

### N38 — [P2] Preview activity has no unit and no measuring point

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 414; [design §6.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 627; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 643; [worker-host-api §3.1](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 87; [plan P5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 957; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 717.

**Evidence**: line 627: "Traffic through its relay counts as activity, using the org idle default with a workspace override. An idle service's existence alone does not keep the VM awake." Line 414: "preview traffic (mere service existence is not activity)". Worker-host-api line 87: "a hot-reload WebSocket is one long-lived stream". Plan line 957: "Authorized traffic counts as activity, service liveness alone does not".

**Problem**: Observation: the phrase recurs (also workspace-vm-api line 100, sharing line 61, plan line 1354) but no document names the unit: an admitted relay request, a new `http` stream open, or bytes and frames on an already-open stream. None names where activity is measured or who writes `last_activity_at`. Preview has no wall-clock cap (line 627), so idle is its only reclaimer. Consequence: counting bytes on any open stream lets a forgotten preview tab with a hot-reload keepalive hold a VM slot indefinitely against `max_running_vms` planning (line 643); counting only HTTP requests reclaims a workspace under an owner using a WebSocket-only single-page app. The §15 idle step for preview (line 1081) cannot be written as a discriminating test.

**Change**: Amend design §5.8 idle row, §6.8 "Service lifecycle", workspace-vm-api §3.1 last paragraph, plan P4.6 step 5 and P5.1 step 5 with one definition: "Preview activity is an authorized owner HTTP request admitted by the API relay at `/api/v1/workspaces/:id/preview/:port/*` and an authorized owner `http` stream open, including a WebSocket upgrade. Bytes or frames on an already-open stream are not activity, so a hot-reload connection alone cannot keep a VM awake. The relay records activity per admitted request or stream open by updating `workspace_vms.last_activity_at`, coalesced to once per 10 s; nothing is measured on the host or in the dispatcher. Non-owner attempts are refused before the update."

**Validation to add**: P5.1 integration test with a fake dev server behind the real relay: (a) one WebSocket held open with a ping every 5 s and no owner HTTP request; `last_activity_at` does not advance and the reconciler stops the workspace at the test-shortened idle default; (b) one owner GET advances it; (c) a new owner WebSocket open counts once; (d) a reader's request returns 403 and leaves it unchanged. Case (a) has no rule today.

### N39 — [P2] The `credential` teardown reason is decided by the host, which the protocol forbids

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 417; [worker-host-api §3.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 100; [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 113; [worker-host-api §8](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 296; [worker-host-api §12](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 347; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 909; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1196.

**Evidence**: line 417: "| `credential` | the host whose credential cannot be renewed (renewal refused because the host is draining, terminated, or its runtime identity changed), with a margin before expiry | the credential's expiry minus a margin | as `drain` |". Worker-host-api line 100: "the host never stops a VM on its own except for lease expiry." Line 347: "The host never decides that a VM is done, idle, or lost. It reports; the control plane decides."

**Problem**: Observation: the table names the host as decider of a stop sequence that is control-plane orchestration, against invariant 3. No document assigns a control-plane owner or trigger for a `refused` renewal: line 113 says only that the old credential stays valid until expiry, line 296 says renewal is independent of drain, P4.13 step 1 (line 909) implements `terminated` after expiry and defers drain to P8.7, whose step 3 (line 1196) runs stop sequences only for `drain` and `rollout`. Tunnel behaviour at expiry is unspecified, and a runtime-identity change is observable only at a new `host.hello`, which nothing connects to this path. Consequence: P4.13/P8.7 have no trigger, owner or ordering, so `credential` is dead code or improvised; a host restarted with a changed identity while running VMs ends at expiry as tunnel loss, `unreachable` and `lease_expired_killed` with unflushed loss, exactly what the reason exists to prevent.

**Change**: Design §5.8 credential row: "Decided by: WorkerHostService, when `host.credential.renew` is answered `refused` for a host that still runs VMs, or when `credential_expires_at - now < credential_margin` (config, default 30 min) with no successful renewal; it records `drain_requested_at` and `drain_reason=credential`, sends `host.drain`, and P8.7's drain orchestration runs the stop sequence per VM with reason `credential` and deadline `credential_expires_at - credential_margin`; the host never decides." Worker-host-api §3.3 renew row: "A refused renewal for a host with running VMs starts a credential drain (§8). At `credential_expires_at` the control plane closes the tunnel and refuses reconnects with the expired credential; the host treats this as tunnel loss and re-registers only with a valid credential. A runtime-identity change is observed at `host.hello` after a g5d host restart; the control plane accepts the hello, refuses renewal and drains with reason `credential`." Add `drain_reason` to `worker_hosts` (worker-host-api §2.2, design §12, plan P4.13 step 1); add the credential path to plan P8.7 step 3.

**Validation to add**: P8.7 api_worker_hosts test: a host with two running VMs re-hellos with a changed `runtime_identity`; its next renew is refused; assert `draining` with `drain_reason=credential`, each VM's stop sequence starts with `stop_reason=credential` and `stop_deadline_at = credential_expires_at - margin`, receipts complete, rows reach `stopped`, no `lease_expired_killed`; advance past expiry and assert the tunnel is closed and the old credential refused. Today the test observes running VMs at expiry and a lease loss.

### N40 — [P2] `delivery_unknown` has no owner-visible resolution after ungraceful loss

**Location**: [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 768; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 422; [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 706; [agent-api-surface §8.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 289, 293; [agent-api-surface §8.3](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 320; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 719.

**Evidence**: line 768: "loss after an unacknowledged handoff is `delivery_unknown` and reconciles from the same-attempt journal/outcome before any retry." Agent-api line 289: "+-> delivery_unknown -> delivered | released". Plan line 719: "preserving an explicit uncertain result if handoff cannot be established rather than automatically replaying input". Line 422: "It runs no flush and loses unpushed work back to the last receipt."

**Problem**: Observation: resolution uses the same-attempt journal or the harvested provider session, but after an ungraceful loss both are on the lost VM (line 422; §8.5 harvests only after a turn or in the stop sequence), and plan line 719 forbids automatic replay. No clause says what the row then becomes or what Continue hands to the provider: agent-api §8.3 resumes "the recorded accepted-input cut", which does not exist for an unaccepted row; the §8.5 fallback history is built from delivered rows; withdraw and edit apply only to queued rows. Owner cancel exists, so the session is not wedged forever. Consequence: P4.6, P4.7 and P4.10 must each guess whether Continue re-reserves the row, skips it (losing the message) or refuses; divergent guesses give an inconsistent state after every host crash mid-turn, and until cancel, one unfinished task per session (line 706) blocks all input.

**Change**: Amend design §8.1 after the quoted sentence and agent-api §8.1 after line 293: when reconciliation of journal, attempt outcome and any accepted harvest finds no `input.accepted` for the reservation, the row stays `delivery_unknown`, the task is held `waiting` with `hold_reason = delivery_unknown`, and the owner has two actions: (1) Continue re-reserves the same row and revision for the next attempt with `delivery_generation + 1` (owner-authorized redelivery is not automatic replay; the transcript renders an uncertainty label and a possible-duplicate note); (2) cancel moves the row to `withdrawn` and the task to `cancelled`. `delivery_unknown` rows are excluded from the §8.5 fallback and counted in `delivery_unknown_input_count`. Add both actions to plan P4.6 step 7, P4.7 step 3 and P4.10.

**Validation to add**: Extend the P4.7 durability test: with a fake provider, kill the incarnation after reservation and before journaled `input.accepted` and any harvest; assert the row is `delivery_unknown`, the task `waiting` with `hold_reason = delivery_unknown`, a new message stays queued, and the fallback history for a new provider session omits the row; then (a) Continue yields one new attempt whose `input.accepted` names the same input id and revision with `delivery_generation = 2` and the row becomes `delivered`; (b) cancel yields `withdrawn`, `cancelled`, and the next message creates a new task. Neither branch has a rule at HEAD.

### N41 — [P2] Self-review is asserted by the acceptance matrix and P4.14 but never defined

**Location**: [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 442; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1082; [workspace-sharing "Forking and collaboration"](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 43; [agent-api-surface §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 59; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 930, 934; [plan §7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1359.

**Evidence**: line 442: "The reviewer accepts/starts under their own identity". Agent-api line 59: "The reviewer accepts under their own identity before the ordinary checked fork creates the child". Plan line 1359: "Checked lazy child is owned by its accepting reviewer (the requester for self-review)". Plan line 934: "A non-owner parent review action creates a child; it never sends to the parent's agent".

**Problem**: Observation: the design and companions describe a two-party protocol and the design test list (line 1082) covers only "reviewer accepts a request". Self-review appears only in the plan (lines 930, 934, 1359). No document defines `reviewer_user_id = requested_by`: one transaction or two, the initial status, inbox visibility. Sharing line 43 is consistent in context: an owner chats with their own agent in place and result routes serve non-owner audiences, so no request row is needed there. Consequence: "explain this workspace to me" is the most frequent MVP flow; an implementer following §5.9 literally ships "request, then accept your own request" or a pending row in the reviewer inbox, and the acceptance-matrix row cannot be falsified.

**Change**: Add to design §5.9 "Scope and authority" after the quoted sentence: "When the requester names themselves as reviewer (self-review), request creation and acceptance are one transaction: the checked fork starts immediately under the same collision rules, the request is never pending to anyone, and the requester owns the child. A parent owner needs no request to ask their own agent in place; a request and child exist only when a result must be returned to a parent audience." Mirror one sentence in sharing "Review and explainer children", agent-api §2.4 and workspace-vm-api §5.1; add to plan P4.14 step 1 and P4.1 step 12 as an allowed `reviewer_user_id = requested_by` case. Keep sharing line 43.

**Validation to add**: P4.14 API test at the request service boundary: a reader creates a request with `reviewer_user_id = self`; the response is one request row past `pending` (`running` once the fork completes) with a child owned by the reader and no pending item in any inbox; a repeat with the same idempotency key returns the same request and child; a name collision returns the fork error with the request in its pre-child state.
