Negative: a request naming another reviewer stays `pending` with no child until that reviewer accepts.

### N42 — [P2] VmSpec env allowlist is a prose placeholder and contradicts host-added proxy keys

**Location**: [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 516, 527, 540; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 561, 567, 570; [worker-host-api §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 120, 153; [worker-host-api §6](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 175, 177; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 647; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 885; [agent_runner.rs](../backend/g5d/src/agent/agent_runner.rs) line 509.

**Evidence**: line 527: "env: { G5D_WORKSPACE_ID, G5D_VM_ID, G5D_EPOCH, G5D_WORKSPACE_TOKEN, G5D_API_URL, HOME, PATH, provider paths, G5D_LLM_PROXY_URL },". Worker-host-api line 120: "The host adds nothing from its own state except the base image files and the cache paths." Line 175: "The spec env carries `G5D_LLM_PROXY_URL=http://g5-llm:<port>`." Line 177: "a **per-VM proxy secret** that the host mints at boot and passes only to the dispatcher (`G5D_LLM_PROXY_SECRET`)".

**Problem**: Observation: the control plane fills `env` with a URL whose port the host allocates per VM, and the host mints `G5D_LLM_PROXY_SECRET` for the dispatcher, while both documents say the host adds nothing. No document says who allocates the port or how the control plane learns it. The allowlist exists only as prose with "provider paths" as a placeholder. The retained `guest_allowlist_env` is a provider-process list that forbids `G5D_API_URL`, so it cannot be the host validator. Lines 567 and 570 are consistent: one spec `G5D_API_URL` plus a dispatcher rewrite in launch environments. Consequence: P4.5 builds the spec, P4.4b validates it and P4.12 opens the listener; a port-owner mismatch yields a dispatcher that cannot reach its proxy (every model turn, push and platform call fails) or a validator refusing the control plane's spec. The allowlist backs `spec_rejected(credential_in_env)`, a security check, and must be identical on both sides; P4.4b must also invent the transport for host-added keys.

**Change**: Design §6.3 line 527 and worker-host-api §4 (line 124 block): replace "provider paths" with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`; give the allowlist as a table: control-plane keys {G5D_WORKSPACE_ID, G5D_VM_ID, G5D_EPOCH, G5D_WORKSPACE_TOKEN, G5D_API_URL, G5D_DISPATCH_PORT, HOME, PATH, CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR, CODEX_HOME}; host-added keys, appended to the dispatcher environment after validation and never accepted in the spec {G5D_LLM_PROXY_URL, G5D_LLM_PROXY_SECRET}. Add to worker-host-api §6 and design §6.5: "The host allocates the per-VM loopback port at `vm.start`, opens the listener before boot, and sets `G5D_LLM_PROXY_URL=http://g5-llm:<port>` in the dispatcher environment; the spec never carries the port." State once that the dispatcher sets `G5D_API_URL=http://g5-llm:<port>/api` in launch environments while keeping the spec value for its own calls. Reword lines 120 and 516 to "adds nothing except the base image, the cache paths, the resolved `secrets_env` and grants, and the two proxy keys". Publish the allowlist as a shared fixture in the P4.4a types crate.

**Validation to add**: P4.4a/P4.4b fixture test: the control plane's spec builder output validates on the host; a spec carrying `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GH_TOKEN`, `G5D_LLM_PROXY_SECRET` or `G5D_LLM_PROXY_URL` is `spec_rejected(credential_in_env)`. P4.12: boot through the tunnel; the dispatcher environment carries `G5D_LLM_PROXY_URL` with the host's listener port, a request with the minted secret is accepted, and the spec JSON contains neither key.

### N43 — [P2] No PR builds the CI workspace-VM runtime image three prompts need to boot

**Location**: [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 542; [design §4.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 125; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 552; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 647, 651; [plan P8.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1168; [stage-local-worker-runtime.sh](../scripts/stage-local-worker-runtime.sh) line 9.

The staging script stages the old `agent-runner` entrypoint from `g5d-worker:ci`; no Do-step in plan §6 stages a runtime directory with `/opt/g5/bin/g5d dispatch`, `/opt/g5/modules/catalog.json` and the fake CLIs. Plan P4.2b Do item 1: "export the catalog file and its hash from the crate for the staging script". P4.4b Tests: "the docker-ci isolation lane asserts, not skips: when /dev/kvm is absent the lane fails with an explicit message on Linux CI and is skipped only on macOS development hosts (design §2)". Design §6.3 "Image": one sentence naming the staged runtime directory as the CI form of the image and the hash the host reports.

### N44 — [P2] Cache and startup "measurement" has no metric, fixture, harness or record location

**Location**: [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 546; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1083; [worker-host-api §7](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 274; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 650; [plan P4.9a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 793; [plan §7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1360; [decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 21.

**Evidence**: line 546: "measure cold attach, same-workspace warm resume and a new child from cached repositories, then verify the replacement before deleting old cache code". Line 1083: "deletion of old cache code requires replacement evidence." Plan line 650: "no meeting latency target or same-host packing requirement is implied." Plan line 1360: "measured startup behavior".

**Problem**: Observation: six places require measuring three startup paths and gate deletion of old cache code on "replacement evidence", but none names the metric (wall time, bytes fetched, hit rate), the fixture repository, the environment, the run count, or where the numbers are recorded; plan line 184 has the checkpoint review the numbers, not define them. Consequence: any number satisfies "measured startup behavior". P4.4b (before) and P4.9a (after, months later) measure different things on different machines, so the before/after comparison is not comparable, and P4.9a can delete `repo_cache.rs` and materializer caching while a cold-attach regression from seconds to minutes passes review. This is a recipe, not the latency SLA the meeting declined (decisions line 21).

Plan P4.4b Do item 5: "record the before-values with that recipe". Plan P4.9a Do item 1: "record the after-values with the same recipe on the same fixture and lane; any scenario slower than the P4.4b median must be explained in the PR description before old cache code is deleted". Plan §7 "Attachment and cache" row: reference the recipe.

**Validation to add**: P4.4b: a docker-ci Phase 1b step boots the three scenarios and prints `MEASURE cold|warm|child <wall_ms> <bytes>` lines; `scripts/test_ci_docker_contract.py` asserts the lines are present in a successful run. P4.9a: the same step runs on its head and the PR description carries a before/after table for the same fixture; a PR deleting repo_cache.rs without the table fails the reviewer's check (`grep MEASURE` on the lane log plus the table).

### N45 — [P2] Host command `deadline` has no clock contract, so the lease bound depends on NTP

**Location**: [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 554; [design §14](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1025; [worker-host-api §1](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 20; [worker-host-api §5](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 112, 166; [plan P4.4a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 627, 629; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 648.

**Evidence**: line 554: "since a command received after its deadline is refused, the host's first deadline is again inside the grant." Worker-host-api line 20: "a deadline and an idempotency key. Expired commands are refused before execution." Line 1025: "boot leases carry the command deadline".

**Problem**: Observation: the renewal TTL is a duration so clocks need not agree (line 112), but the command `deadline` that the boot-time bound relies on has no type: an absolute instant compared with the host clock, or a duration compared with receipt time. No document mentions skew, and P4.4a test 4 ("a command after its deadline is refused", line 629) passes for either. Consequence: with an absolute deadline, a self-hosted host whose clock lags accepts a `vm.start` after the control-plane grant expired and arms a lease that outlives it, so a superseded VM keeps its proxies serving past replacement admission; a clock ahead refuses every command as `expired`. With a duration, transit is unaccounted and the bound is unproven. The §6.4 argument and the line 1025 row silently depend on NTP.

**Change**: Worker-host-api §1 line 20 and §3.2: define `deadline_at` (RFC 3339, control-plane clock) plus `issued_at` on every VM-scoped command; the host refuses `expired` when its clock is past `deadline_at` and logs the offset from `issued_at`; state that this refusal is an optimization, not the lease-safety mechanism. Design §6.4: replace the quoted sentence with a clock-free rule: the CAS grant of `lease_ttl_secs + start_deadline_secs` is provisional; on the host's `vm.start` result (or the `booted` event) the control plane sets `lease_expires_at` to its handling time plus `lease_ttl_secs`, and while a `vm.start` has only timed out the reconciler may not place a replacement until it receives a definite result or an ownership-fenced `vm.stop` answers `stopped` or `not_found`. Plan P4.4a step 2 and P4.4b step 3 carry the same wording; add a `G5_HOST_CLOCK_OFFSET_SECS` hook (test builds only).

**Validation to add**: P4.4a ctl tests: with injected offsets of -600 s and +600 s, a fresh command is accepted under both and a command past `deadline_at` is refused when the host clock agrees. P4.4b over the tunnel: hold the `vm.start` frame for `start_deadline_secs + 5` under a -600 s host clock; assert the host-armed deadline (`vm.status.lease_expires_at`) is at or before `workspace_vms.lease_expires_at` after the result is handled, and no replacement is placed while the result is outstanding. Both fail at HEAD: no representation, hook or re-arm exists.

### N46 — [P2] Task secrets reach every module process but transcript redaction is by key name, not value

**Location**: [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 574; [design §6.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 592; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 766; [design §8.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 831; [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 111; [agent-api-surface §12](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 456; [claude_code.rs](../backend/g5d-worker/src/claude_code.rs) lines 1057, 1255.

**Evidence**: line 574: "the host resolves `secrets_env` into the dispatcher's environment at boot, and the dispatcher passes them to module executions." Line 766: "Readers see the same history". Line 831: "Preserve fake-provider and real-boundary tests and session-harvest redaction/durability checks." Agent-api line 456: "Rebuildable, bounded/redacted".

**Problem**: Observation: secrets are environment variables in every agent, preview and terminal process. Tool output (`printenv`, a failing build, a `.env` cat) lands in C2 events, so in `chat_messages` and `task_events`, which every reader of an org-visible workspace reads. The design calls these projections and module logs "redacted" without saying what is redacted. Retained code redacts by value only for keys whose name looks secret-bearing (`is_secret_bearing_env_key`, line 1255), so a `secrets_env` value under `DATABASE_URL` is not redacted. `session_files.rs` harvest has no redaction and no test matches harvest plus redact, so the checks line 831 says to preserve do not exist. `chat_session_files` are worker-only (`api_chat_session_files.rs`), not reader-visible. Consequence: P4.11 says to move existing runner modules, so an agent carries the heuristic forward believing it satisfies "redacted"; a secret with a neutral key name lands verbatim in reader-visible `chat_messages` and in backups.

**Change**: Design §6.6 (module invocation) and §8.3/§8.5: the dispatcher hands `agent.turn` and every module the set of `secrets_env` values plus the grant values it knows, and the module applies value-based redaction of every such value (6 or more characters, whole-value and JSON string leaves, as `redact_overlapping_values` does) regardless of key name, to every C2 payload, module log line and diagnostic bundle before it leaves the VM; harvests stay unredacted for exact resume, remain worker-only (cite the retained guard) and are never served by a reader route. Plan P4.11: "redaction is by value for all `secrets_env` values, not by key-name heuristic; preserve `redact_overlapping_values`". Plan P4.2b: "`modules.logs` redaction uses the same value set". Design line 831: replace with "timeline redaction and session-harvest durability checks".

**Validation to add**: P4.11 fake-provider test: `secrets_env` entries `DATABASE_URL=postgres://u:hunter2secret@h/db` and `MY_TOKEN=...`; a tool output frame prints both; assert `chat_messages`, `task_events` and `modules.logs` contain neither value and contain the marker, while the harvested JSONL restores byte-identically. Fails at HEAD because `is_secret_bearing_env_key("DATABASE_URL")` is false. P4.2b: a module log line containing a `secrets_env` value is redacted in `modules.logs`. Retained `route_auth_static` and `api_chat_session_files` guards stay green.

### N47 — [P2] `workspace.status.get` is a dispatcher RPC but must answer for stopped workspaces and readers

**Location**: [design §6.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 582, 590; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1080; [workspace-vm-api §2](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 47; [plan P4.2a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 533; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 673, 675.

**Evidence**: line 582: "**Workspace Runtime API** (JSON-RPC on the `rpc` stream; events pushed on the same stream and journaled):". Line 590: "when stopped, only durable and last-reported state, never a live claim, and never a wake". Workspace-vm-api line 47: "Any authorized workspace reader ... Does not invoke the VM". Plan line 533: "`workspace.status.get`; `runtime.inspect.summary`; the private protocol `runtime.ready`".

**Problem**: Observation: the design lists the method in the table served over the VM `rpc` stream, and P4.2a step 3 puts it in the set `g5d dispatch` serves with the acceptance "the served method set equals the list above". The same row requires content the guest cannot know (placement, lease freshness, last receipt, current or last `vm_id`), an answer when stopped, and no VM invocation. P4.5 step 4 (line 673) exposes "the status surface of P4.2a" through the facade while step 6 (line 675) adds `GET /workspaces/:id/vm`. Consequence: the P4.2a agent must guess what a guest handler returns; the P4.5 agent may route a running workspace's status to the dispatcher, which for a reader violates "Does not invoke the VM", and the §15 zero-VM-call route list (line 1080) omits status, so no test catches it.

**Change**: Design §6.6: move `workspace.status.get` out of the JSON-RPC table into the facade paragraph: "Workspace status is a control-plane read (`GET /workspaces/:id/vm`, P4.5) composed from `workspace_vms`, `workspace_flushes` and the last host report; it never opens a VM channel. `runtime.inspect.summary` is the only live guest status method." Workspace-vm-api §2: Methods cell becomes `GET /workspaces/:id/vm`, Caller unchanged. Plan P4.2a step 3: delete `workspace.status.get`, keep `runtime.inspect.summary`. Plan P4.5 step 4: replace "status" with "and the control-plane status route of step 6". Design §15 Sharing: add `GET /workspaces/:id/vm` to the zero-VM-call list.

**Validation to add**: P4.2a: the method-set assertion lists no `workspace.status.get` and a call answers `method_not_found`. P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: `GET /workspaces/:id/vm` for (a) a stopped workspace, (b) a reader of a running one, (c) the owner of a running one returns lifecycle state, `vm_id`, placement, lease freshness and last receipt id with the facade call log empty for that workspace in all three cases; fails if the route opens an `rpc` stream or calls the dispatcher.

### N48 — [P2] Save/upload field contract is missing: hash, create semantics, encoding, size, confinement

**Location**: [design §6.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 586; [design §9.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 866; [workspace-vm-api §2](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 43; [plan P4.2a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 533; [plan P2.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 384; [files.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/files.rs) line 807; [instance.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/instance.rs) line 267.

**Evidence**: line 586: "`fs.write(path, content, expected_hash)`, `fs.delete(path, expected_hash?)`, `fs.rename(from, to, expected_hash?)`". Line 866: "Upload follows the same authorized, confined VM path, mutation barrier and overwrite/version policy". files.rs line 807: "/// Draft mutations (`PUT`/`DELETE`) reject `.git/**` and *non-durable*". instance.rs line 267: "let max_upload_size_mb = 100u32;".

**Problem**: Observation: `expected_hash` is named ten times and never defined: not the algorithm, not what `fs.read`/`fs.stat` return, not the create-if-absent value, not directory rename. `content` is a JSON-RPC parameter while uploads are binary and the instance advertises 100 MB; no encoding, chunking or size bound exists for the `rpc` stream. The "overwrite/version policy" at line 866 exists nowhere. The confinement the retained PUT enforces (files.rs line 807) is not carried into `fs.write`; the only confinement text is the boot-time `root_path` check (line 540) and the excludes file, which governs git capture. Consequence: P4.2a, P2.1 and P2.2 implement one precondition from prompts that never name the algorithm, weeks apart. A mismatch makes every Save a 409 and invites relaxing the check, defeating "no silent overwrite". A browser Save can reach `.git/config`, `.g5/cache` or a symlink target outside `/work` if only `..` is stripped.

**Change**: Add a "Files field contract" paragraph to workspace-vm-api §2 below the Files rows, referenced from design §6.6 and §9.2 and mirrored in plan P4.2a step 3 and P2.1 step 2: (1) `hash` is the git blob OID of the bytes (SHA-1 over `blob <len>\0<bytes>`, lowercase hex), equal to the OID the Git read path and receipts expose; `fs.read`/`fs.stat` return `{path, hash, size, mode, mtime, encoding}`; (2) `expected_hash: null` on write means "must not exist" and fails `FILE_EXISTS` (409 with `current_hash`); a non-null hash against a missing path fails `FILE_MISSING`; directory rename takes no hash and fails if the destination exists; (3) `content` is UTF-8 with `encoding: "utf-8"` or base64 with `encoding: "base64"`; at most 32 MiB decoded per call, larger is `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, and the API rejects above the cap before contacting the VM (instance.rs advertises the cap); (4) paths are normalized (no `..`, no absolute), resolve under one member repository root from `/work/.g5/layout.json`, may not enter `.git/` or the `.g5/` runtime subdirectories in `/var/lib/g5d/excludes`, and the dispatcher opens the final component with `O_NOFOLLOW` and refuses a canonicalized parent outside the root; (5) §9.2 "overwrite/version policy" becomes: upload is `fs.write` with `expected_hash` = current hash or null, no server-side versioning, and the 409 carries `current_hash` and content.

**Validation to add**: P4.2a test against `g5d dispatch` on a temp repo: `fs.stat` hash equals `git hash-object`; null hash on an existing path gives `FILE_EXISTS`; the correct OID succeeds and `fs.stat` returns the new OID; writes to `.git/config`, `../x`, `/etc/x`, `.g5/cache/x` and through an outside-targeting symlink are refused with a definite error and leave the target untouched; a base64 payload decoding to 32 MiB + 1 byte is refused. P2.1: PUT with a stale hash returns 409 whose `current_hash` equals the Git read route's OID after flush; base64 PUT round-trips binary; a 100 MB upload is refused with no `fs.write` call.

### N49 — [P2] Reader stream revocation on access removal is unspecified (S6 partially open)

**Location**: [v8 security review](lean-backend-design-v8-security-review.md) line 74; [design §6.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 595; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 766; [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 858; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1080; [workspace-vm-api §2](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 54; [workspace-sharing "Read source and isolation"](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 35; [plan P2.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 395; [plan P5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 957; [decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 45; [channels.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/ws/channels.rs) line 327; [docs.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/docs.rs) line 1096.

**Evidence**: Security review line 74: "Define the revocation ordering and any bounded delay, including cache invalidation and reconnect behavior." Design line 595: "Owner/live routes check identity on every request and stream admission, and revoke streams and cached live content on ownership/access changes." Workspace-vm-api line 54: "close filesystem watches, terminal/preview relays and live diagnostics streams." Decisions line 45: "complete revocation correctness".

**Problem**: Observation: every v9 revocation clause enumerates the live VM-backed streams restricted to owners. Readers are deliberately given durable subscriptions (chat and timeline events, task events, branch/flush/projection hints; plan line 395), which carry new private content: transcript deltas, tool output, results, ref OIDs and paths. No document defines an authorization generation for browser subscriptions, the order of commit, generation bump, stream close, cache invalidation and reconnect refusal, or a bound; P5.1 line 957 binds only preview tickets to a generation. Retained code authorizes at subscribe time only (`authorize_subscribe`, channels.rs line 327) with no revocation hook, and docs.rs line 1096 publishes to `Channel::Workspace`. Consequence: an agent on P4.10/P2.1 binds only owner-live streams, so a reader removed from an invite_only workspace keeps receiving new chat content and ref hints until the socket drops; one implementer closes before commit (spurious closes on abort), another after (an unbounded window). Acknowledged open (design line 28, decisions line 45), but it is an authorization bypass on new private content at MVP.

**Change**: Add to design §9.1 after line 858 and sharing line 35: "Every browser subscription on a workspace channel, owner-live or reader-durable (chat/timeline, task events, branch/flush/projection hints), is admitted with the (user, workspace) `access_generation`. A membership, visibility or owner change increments the generation in the same transaction; after commit the hub closes every subscription and relay with an older generation, invalidates caches and refuses reconnects and tickets carrying the old generation. Close-after-commit is the order; the bound is one event-bus delivery (target 5 s or less), and nothing is delivered on a closed subscription after the commit." Design §15 Sharing (line 1080): add "a removed reader's open chat/timeline/hint subscriptions receive nothing after the barrier". Plan P4.10, P2.1 and P4.7: implement the generation in ws/channels.rs subscribe and a revocation hook called from workspace member, visibility and owner writers.

**Validation to add**: WebSocket-boundary test (api_ws or api_workspace_members): a reader subscribes to chat timeline, task events and projection hints on an invite_only workspace; the owner revokes the invite, then posts a chat message and pushes a ref; assert the reader socket closes within the bound and received no payload after the commit, a second authorized reader keeps receiving, and a reconnect with the old ticket is refused. Repeat for visibility org to invite_only and for a preview ticket.

### N50 — [P2] Preview replacement, loss and readiness have undefined branches

**Location**: [design §6.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 621, 625, 627; [design §4.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 173; [design §6.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 605; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1081; [workspace-vm-api §3.1](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) lines 92, 100; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 715; [preview_profiles.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/preview_profiles.rs) line 70.

**Evidence**: line 625: "wait for its process to stop (or its incarnation to be lost) before launching the successor." Line 621: "is not marked successful merely because a port is ready." Workspace-vm-api line 100: "no queued preview automatically wakes a stopped or transferred workspace." Plan line 715: "settle every active execution bound to it, confirmed or not, as vm_lost. ... Preview is terminal on loss and never auto-requeued." preview_profiles.rs line 70: "pub port: u16,".

**Problem**: Observation: (a) a replacement B recorded while A runs is neither an execution bound to the lost incarnation (line 715) nor a fresh explicit start (line 100); line 625 permits launching it after loss, line 627 forbids any successor across VM loss, and the compound-start path keeps a queued preview through attachment, so a dispatcher that does not exclude B launches it on the next Resume. (b) The profile declares only `port`; line 173 says readiness is `running` and §6.7 allows health probes, but nothing ties `port.opened` for the profile port, a probe or the `started` disposition to the Preview panel or relay, and the retained relay maps only upstream close and timeout to 503. Consequence: P4.6 (loss settlement) and P5.1 (module, relay, panel) can settle B differently, giving a preview the owner did not re-request or a spuriously failed task; a relay opened before the port listens yields errors the profile author cannot control; the §15 step "service readiness still running" (line 1081) is not falsifiable.

**Change**: Design line 625: "durably cancel the previous queued/running intent, deliver its cancellation disposition and wait for its process to stop before launching the successor; if the incarnation is lost first, the predecessor settles `vm_lost` and every not-yet-invoked preview task of that workspace, including a recorded successor, settles `failed(vm_lost)` in the same transaction, so a later Resume dispatches no preview." Mirror in workspace-vm-api §3.1 paragraph 3 and plan P4.6 step 3. Add "Readiness" to design §6.8 and workspace-vm-api §3.1: "The task is `running` from the `started` disposition. The Preview panel and relay treat the service as serving once the dispatcher reports `port.opened` for the profile's `port` (or a declared health probe passes); before that the relay answers 503 `PREVIEW_NOT_READY` with the task state and the panel shows starting. `port.closed` without process exit shows not serving but leaves the task running." Plan P5.1 step 5: the relay checks the port-open fact recorded by TaskService, not the host stream.

**Validation to add**: P4.6: start preview A, request replacement B (recorded, waiting on A's cancellation), fence the incarnation lost before B is invoked; A and B are both `failed(vm_lost)`, and Resume plus a dispatch tick invokes no `workspace.preview start`. P5.1 against the real dispatcher: a fixture listening 3 s after start gives 503 `PREVIEW_NOT_READY` until `port.opened`, then proxies; a fixture that never listens leaves the task `running`, the panel starting, and Cancel ends it `cancelled`; a fixture that exits after listening settles `failed`. The first test fails today because no rule settles B.

**Location**: [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 656–662; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 501; [plan P4.9b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 813; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) lines 4858, 5960, 8640; [job.rs](../backend/g5-api-data/src/models/job.rs) lines 195–201.

No document gives the `task_type` to `kind` mapping, says whether `kind` is added or renamed, or orders `job_id DROP NOT NULL` and the `workspace_id` backfill; the RLS policy `issue_discovery_scheduler_tasks` (line 8640) filters on `task_type`, so a rename breaks it. Consequence: following the list omits eight §7.1 columns and forces P4.6, P4.7 and P4.8 into follow-up migrations; following §7.1 means guessing the mapping, the rename and where limits live.

**Change**: Plan P4.1 step 4: enumerate every §7.1 column, staged beside the legacy ones: `kind text NULL CHECK (kind IN ('agent','command','preview'))` beside `task_type` (no rename; P4.9b drops `task_type` with the policy after P8.1), `workspace_id uuid` backfilled from `jobs.workspace_id` via `job_id` then NOT NULL, `spec jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, `outcome jsonb NULL`, `created_by uuid NULL`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `chat_session_id uuid NULL`, `ALTER COLUMN job_id DROP NOT NULL`. ### N52 — [P2] Hold, continuation and dependency-failure fields have no values or owner

**Location**: [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 661–662, 686; [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 694, 705; [agent-api-surface §8.3](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 320; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 501; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 713, 720; [task_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/task_service.rs) line 26948.

**Evidence**: lines 661–662: "continuation_pending bool, continuation_reason NULL, hold_reason NULL, created_by, created_at, started_at, ended_at)". Line 694: "running | waiting ──continuable attempt ended──► queued or waiting (no live attempt; held)". Line 705: "Final failure/cancellation is propagated through the job's explicit dependency-failure policy; no dangling permanent wait." Plan line 501: "continuation_pending/continuation_reason/hold_reason state."

**Problem**: Observation: the three columns appear only in the DDL and P4.1's list; no prose enumerates values, setters or clearing actions. The queued-versus-waiting split is defined (agent-api line 320, §7.2 bullets, P4.6 step 1) and `jobs.status (derived)` is explained at design line 728, so those are not gaps. The "explicit dependency-failure policy" is named at line 705, agent-api line 320 and P4.6 step 8 (line 720) with no values, storage or rule for jobless tasks with `depends_on` (line 686). Retained code has a fixed cascade (`cascade_dependency_failures`, line 26948), not a vocabulary. Consequence: P4.1 creates the columns, P4.6 writes them in `TaskService::apply` and the dispatch predicate excluding "transfer/owner-resume holds" (line 713), P4.10 renders held versus running; each agent invents the enum, and the P4.6 step 8 test invents the policy contract it tests.

**Change**: Design §7.1 after the DDL: `hold_reason` in {owner_stop, idle_stop, transfer, owner_resume_required, response_timeout, vm_lost, drain_continuation, publication_pending}, with, per value, the setting event and the clearing action (Continue, Resume, Start intent, cancel, or canonical publication); `continuation_pending = true` iff the task is nonterminal with no live attempt and an eligible continuation; `continuation_reason` mirrors the prior attempt's `reason`; both are cleared atomically by the Continue that creates the next attempt or by cancel. `dependency_failure_policy` in {fail_dependents, cancel_dependents, hold_dependents} stored in `jobs.spec`, default `fail_dependents`; jobless tasks with `depends_on` use `fail_dependents`. Reference from plan P4.1 step 4, P4.6 steps 1 and 2, P4.8 step 3.

**Validation to add**: P4.6 TaskService tests enumerating every reachable (status, hold_reason, continuation_pending) tuple from the §7.2 transitions and asserting the dispatch eligibility predicate on each; a property test that no row has `continuation_pending = true` with a terminal status or a live attempt; P4.8 rollup tests per policy value, including a jobless A to B chain where A finally fails and B settles per the default.

### N53 — [P2] Permission policy shape is undefined; `permission_mode` cannot express auto-deny versus auto-allow

**Location**: [agent-api-surface §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 210–211; [agent-api-surface §2.3](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 53, 55; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 749; [design §8.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 803; [provider CLI contract](lean-provider-cli-contract.md) lines 99, 301; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 712.

**Evidence**: agent-api line 210: "permission_mode: \"non_prompting\" | \"tool_approval\";" Design line 803: "Resolve and persist the session/profile's question policy, tool-approval mode, sandbox/filesystem scope, allowed tools and resource limits." CLI contract line 99: "`acceptEdits`, `auto`, `bypassPermissions`, `manual`, `dontAsk`, `plan`". Line 301: "Under `bypassPermissions` the tool is never called. With `--permission-prompts none`, or a prompting mode without a prompt tool, the CLI denies on its own." Agent-api line 55: "The adapter must prove that the chosen policy leaves `AskUserQuestion` available when native questions are enabled".

**Problem**: Observation: the module input carries a two-valued `permission_mode` and an opaque `policy_ref`. Neither `chat_sessions.permission_policy jsonb` (line 749), `policy_ref` nor `policy_snapshot_ref` has a schema; line 803 lists components without values or a mapping to `--permission-mode`, `--permission-prompts` or Codex `approval_policy` plus `sandbox_mode`. Context leans `non_prompting` toward the elevated reading (line 803, plan line 712), but `dontAsk` and `--permission-prompts none` are also non-prompting and auto-deny, and the enum has no "questions enabled" bit although line 55 admits the interaction is unproven. Retained `task_context.rs` carries a raw Claude mode string pushed into argv. Consequence: P4.1, P4.6 and P4.11 each define the shape; `non_prompting` mapped to `bypassPermissions` on one side and `dontAsk` on the other silently changes the safety posture, against §8.3 "no silent escalation", and the frozen snapshot is unauditable.

**Change**: Add `AgentPermissionPolicyV1` to agent-api-surface §5 (or new §5.1): `{ schema_version: 'g5.agent.permission-policy/v1'; questions: 'enabled'|'disabled'; tool_approval: 'prompt'|'auto_allow'|'auto_deny'; sandbox: provider-specific enum (Claude: none; Codex: 'read-only'|'workspace-write'|'danger-full-access'); allowed_tools?: string[]; limits: {...} }`, a table mapping each admitted combination to Claude `--permission-mode`/`--permission-prompts`/`--permission-prompt-tool` and Codex `approval_policy`/`sandbox_mode`, and the combinations each adapter rejects before launch (Codex: anything but `auto_deny` with `approval_policy=never` and an explicit sandbox; Claude: `questions=enabled` with a mode that suppresses `AskUserQuestion`). `chat_sessions.permission_policy`, the `tasks.spec` requested policy and `policy_snapshot_ref` carry this document; derive `permission_mode` (`prompt` gives `tool_approval`, else `non_prompting`) rather than storing it. Design §8.3 "Explicit policy": pointer; plan P4.1 step 7, P4.6 step 1b, P4.11 steps 1, 4, 5: name the type.

**Validation to add**: P4.11: an argv snapshot test per admitted combination against the fake and pinned recordings proving, per Claude mode, whether `AskUserQuestion` yields a `can_use_tool` control request; a rejection test for every disallowed combination before spawn. P4.6: a workflow template with no saved policy is refused (no implicit `auto_allow`); an attempt snapshot round-trips the versioned document and a continuation with changed session policy produces a second snapshot without rewriting the first.

### N54 — [P2] Usage has two producers and `UsageV1`/`QuotaSnapshotV1` are never defined

**Location**: [agent-api-surface §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 341, 372, 391; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 757; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 565; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 980; [worker-host-api §6](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 173; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 886; [chat_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/chat_service.rs) line 10664.

**Evidence**: agent-api line 372: "| { kind: \"usage.updated\"; usage: UsageV1; quota_snapshot?: QuotaSnapshotV1 }". Design line 757: "task_id NULL, attempt NULL, usage jsonb, created_at)". Worker-host-api line 173: "records usage per session for accounting." Design line 980: "`usage_records`, `provider_accounts`, `provider_quota_snapshots`."

**Problem**: Observation: usage reaches Postgres from the adapter (`usage.updated` as a C2 event, `AgentTurnOutcomeV1.usage`, `chat_messages.usage`) and from the host proxy, which meters per session (line 565) and per plan P4.12 step 2 records "usage per session to `usage_records` through the control plane". `UsageV1` and `QuotaSnapshotV1` are referenced at agent-api lines 341, 372 and 391 and defined in none of the seven documents. No sentence names the billing authority or the reconciliation when a turn is lost after the proxy metered requests but before the adapter reported. Retained code has one authoritative writer, `spend_service.record_usage` (line 10664), fed by the worker's `TurnUsage`; v9 adds a proxy writer without subordinating the adapter path. Consequence: P4.11, P4.12 and P4.6 belong to different owners and will define incompatible shapes and both write `usage_records`, double counting spend per owner; spend after `vm_lost` before harvest is undefined.

**Change**: Agent-api-surface §9: add `type UsageV1 = { input_tokens; output_tokens; cache_creation_input_tokens; cache_read_input_tokens; model?; total_cost_usd?; duration_ms?; finish_reason? }` (matching retained `TurnUsage`) and `QuotaSnapshotV1 = { provider; captured_at; headers: Record<string,string> }`. Design §8.3 and worker-host-api §6: proxy-metered `usage_records` (keyed `(workspace_id, vm_id, session_id, task_id, attempt)`, `request_type='chat'`, `source='proxy'`) are the sole billing authority and survive VM loss; adapter `usage.updated`, `AgentTurnOutcomeV1.usage` and `chat_messages.usage` are per-message display projections and never insert `usage_records`. Plan P4.6 step 4 and P4.11 step 1: TaskService does not call `spend_service.record_usage` for agent outcomes. Plan P4.12 step 2: the proxy's control-plane call is the only `record_usage` caller, with `provider_quota_snapshots` fed from captured quota headers.

**Validation to add**: P4.12 plus P4.6 integration test at the internal API boundary: a fake provider behind the proxy serves N metered requests, then the VM is marked lost before harvest; `usage_records` holds exactly N proxy rows for the owner's account, the assistant `chat_messages.usage` is null, and `GET /workspaces/:id/spend` equals the proxy total. Second test: a completed turn with adapter-reported usage adds no `usage_records` row (count stays N).

### N55 — [P2] Docs and ontology projections are single-version rows, but §9.1 requires reader views pinned to a receipt OID and §9.4 projects snapshot refs; no PR re-keys the projections

**Location**: [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 852 and 858; [design §9.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 876; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 986; [plan P2.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 394; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 582; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) lines 3701, 4339 and 5830; [projection_convergence.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/projection_convergence.rs) lines 46, 154 and 230.

**Evidence**: design line 858: "Docs/ontology projections must correspond to the selected OID." Line 852: "selected complete flush snapshot in internal Git; otherwise branch head". Line 876: the convergence loop "lists the authoritative OIDs of every workspace branch, every complete receipt's snapshot ref and every protected branch". Plan line 582: "`flush.completed` feeds the P1.3 convergence loop for the snapshot ref." Plan line 394: "Keep document/ontology projection convergence". projection_convergence.rs line 46: "Enumerate only refs/heads, including refs without a database branch row."; line 230 fails with "Expected branch ref".

**Problem**: Observation: `docs` holds one `content`/`content_hash` per row and `ontology_nodes` is `UNIQUE (workspace_branch_id, parent_id, name)`; neither has a ref or OID dimension, only `projection_state` is keyed `(repo_id, ref)`. The loop enumerates `refs/heads/*` only and binds through `workspace_branch_repos JOIN workspace_branches`, which design line 986 drops. Consequence: an implementer following P4.3a item 5 literally either hits "Expected branch ref" for `refs/g5/flush/*` or, once patched, overwrites the single-version rows with alternating head and snapshot content every 60 s. A reader whose tree is pinned to snapshot OID X gets docs content from whichever ref converged last, which is exactly the mixing §9.1 forbids. No PR names a projection key change or a render-from-snapshot path.

**Change**: Amend design §9.4 (line 876) and §9.1 (line 858): docs and ontology projections are computed only for `refs/heads/<branch>` and protected refs; a `source: flush` view renders docs and ontology from the pinned snapshot tree through the historical read view (`CommittedRepoReadView::from_historical` at the receipt's `snapshot_oid`) and never reads projection rows; a `source: committed` view reads projection rows only when `projection_state.applied_oid` for `(repo_id, refs/heads/<branch>)` equals the selected OID, otherwise it renders from Git with a projection-lagging marker. Delete "every complete receipt's snapshot ref" from §9.4. Replace plan P4.3a item 5's last clause with "`flush.completed` invalidates reader caches; snapshot views render from the receipt tree". Add the `applied_oid` guard to P2.1 item 3, move the loop's binding query to `workspace_repos` in P4.5 item 15, and name projection_convergence.rs in P7.2 item 1's inventory.

**Validation to add**: P4.3a/P2.1 integration test with the real Git store and convergence loop: head H1, complete receipt S1 changing doc D, then push H2 changing D again; a non-owner reads tree and D with the default source and with `?source=committed`; assert D's content matches the reported OID in each response (S1 for flush, H2 for committed) and that `projection_state.applied_oid` for `refs/heads/<branch>` stays at H2 across three ticks with no rows for `refs/g5/flush/*`. P4.5 item 15 migration test: after the legacy mapping tables are dropped, push to one repo of a two-repo workspace and assert re-projection within one tick.

### N56 — [P2] Comment anchors `(path, line, line hash)` have no re-anchoring, orphaning or source-vector rule, and the retained `comment_index` shape is not reconciled

**Location**: [design §9.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 872; [plan P2.2](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 416; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 3626; [comment_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/comment_service.rs) lines 439–443.

**Evidence**: design line 872: "Comments anchor to `(path, line, line hash)`. There is no presence and no co-editing". Plan line 416: "anchor by `(path, line, line hash)`; adjust useComments.ts and the comment components; if `comment_index` lacks the hash, add it with a migration." comment_service.rs line 439: "attempt to map the original `line_number` from the comment's `commit_sha` to the current branch's HEAD using git diff hunk analysis."

**Problem**: Observation: the triple is the whole specification. `comment_index` stores `file_path, line_number, commit_sha, commit_range_base, commit_range_head, origin_branch`, and the retained service already re-anchors by hunk mapping from `commit_sha`. Nothing says what happens when a Save moves or changes the line, whether `commit_sha` remains the durable anchor, how duplicate hashes (blank lines, closing braces) are disambiguated, or which OID a reader pinned to a receipt renders against while the owner sees live content. Consequence: the P2.2 agent invents an algorithm with no backend contract; owner and reader can see one comment on different lines; the required Playwright case "comment anchor survives a save" cannot fail because nothing defines surviving.

**Change**: Amend design §9.3 to a full anchor contract: a line comment stores `(repo_id, path, source_oid, line, line_hash)`, where `source_oid` is the OID of the content the author saw (owner: the branch head returned by the P2.1 PUT/read response; reader: the pinned receipt or committed OID). On render, if the viewer's OID equals `source_oid`, show at `line`; else map through the retained hunk mapping and confirm the hash; else search ±20 lines for the hash; else render orphaned at the original line with a marker; duplicate hashes resolve to the nearest candidate. State that `comment_index.commit_sha` is retained as `source_oid` and `origin_branch` stays a filter label. In plan P2.2 step 3 replace the "if missing" clause with "add `line_hash text` via forward migration NNN, populate on create, implement the §9.3 render rule in useComments.ts and CodeEditor.tsx; live uncommitted edits anchor to the current head OID plus hash, never a commit of the buffer".

**Validation to add**: P2.2 explicit-save.spec.ts: (a) comment on line 10, insert three lines above, Save, comment renders on line 13; (b) delete the commented line, Save, orphan marker at the original line, thread still opens; (c) a non-owner viewing the receipt OID sees the comment at the pre-save line. api_comments.rs: creating a line comment without `line_hash` returns 400; the cross-branch mapping still returns `mapped_line` for a hash-confirmed hunk. Each fails without the §9.3 rule and column.

### N57 — [P2] `vm.event` journal ingestion has no table in the data model or the P4.1 migration

**Location**: [plan P4.4a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 628; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 505; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 978; [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 111 and 303.

**Evidence**: plan line 628: "ingestion of `vm.event` on the unique key `(vm_id, seq)` with `vm.event.ack`". Plan line 505: "`worker_hosts`: add `status` value `unreachable`, `slots_total`, ...". Design line 978: "**Worker & Host (§6):** `worker_hosts` (host id, org, region, kind `gce|self_hosted|macos_dev`, status with `unreachable`, ...". Worker-host line 303 lists the "boot log tail on `boot_failed` and `exited`" as an observability surface.

**Problem**: Observation: P4.4a must insert on `(vm_id, seq)` and acknowledge cumulatively, but §12 lists only `worker_hosts` for Worker & Host, the P4.1 migration (plan lines 498–510) adds no event table, and P4.4a has no migration step. Grep for `vm_events`, `worker_vm_events` or `host_event` across the v9 documents finds nothing. Consequence: the implementer invents the table (name, columns, RLS, retention) or dedupes in memory, which loses idempotency across control-plane restarts: a replayed `exited` is handled twice, or a lost `exited` leaves a `stopping` row stuck. The log tail on `exited`/`boot_failed` has no home except `workspace_vms.last_error`, which the next incarnation overwrites. Plan §2 item 6 (line 26) does let any PR add a forward migration, so the gap is the unspecified shape, not a forbidden migration.

**Change**: Amend design §12 "Worker & Host (§6)" and plan P4.1 step 8 to add `worker_vm_events(vm_id text, seq bigint, workspace_id, host_id, kind text, payload jsonb, at timestamptz, received_at timestamptz, PRIMARY KEY (vm_id, seq))` with org RLS, an index on `(workspace_id, received_at desc)` and a retention rule (rows older than the workspace's `retain_hours` plus 7 days pruned by the reconciler). Amend worker-host §3.3 `vm.event` and design §6.2: ingestion is insert-ignore on `(vm_id, seq)`; the ack watermark is the stored max contiguous seq, not memory; events for a `vm_id` that is not the row's current incarnation are stored as history and never touch `workspace_vms`; `log_tail` lives in `payload`. Amend P4.4a step 3 to read from this table.

**Validation to add**: P4.4a integration test at the ctl boundary: the host sends `exited` (vm_id A, seq 5), the control plane restarts before acking, the host replays seq 5; assert exactly one `workspace_vms` transition and one `worker_vm_events` row. A `lease_expired_killed` for A arriving after the row moved to vm_id B is stored with its log tail and B's row is unchanged. The first case fails with in-memory dedupe (two transitions after restart).

### N58 — [P2] Ownership backfill requires an operator mapping with no mechanism; unattended deploy blocker

**Location**: [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 497, 500, 515 and 517; [plan §2](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 26; [plan §5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 217; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 988; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 5482; [database-migrations.md](database-migrations.md) lines 3–5; [job-migrate.yaml](../infra/helm/g5/templates/job-migrate.yaml) line 14.

**Evidence**: plan line 500: "Backfill from `created_by` only where it resolves to an eligible org member; a missing, departed or ambiguous owner fails a preflight report and requires an explicit operator mapping before migration." Line 497: "Do (one file `002_workspace_vm_runtime.sql`, or the next unused number, ...". Line 515: "unresolved ownership aborts atomically." Design line 988: "missing or invalid owners require explicit resolution before rollout, never silent admin assignment." Baseline line 5482: "created_by uuid," (nullable). database-migrations.md lines 3–4: "Helm's `pre-install,pre-upgrade` migration Job runs `g5-db-migrate`, which invokes the standard SQLx migrator."

**Problem**: Observation: one migration file must add NOT NULL `owner_user_id`, backfill from a nullable `created_by`, and abort atomically when any workspace lacks an eligible owner, with a mapping supplied "before migration". SQLx runs the file in one transaction, so a mapping table created in the same file rolls back with the abort and cannot be populated between the check and the backfill. Migrations run unattended in the Helm pre-upgrade Job. No text says where the mapping lives, who runs the preflight against dev and QA, or what "eligible" means. Consequence: the P4.1 agent must invent storage, eligibility and workflow, and one departed creator in dev or QA turns the deploy lane red with API pods on the old release; P4.3a, P4.4a and P4.13 all wait on P4.1. Under pressure an agent weakens the rule (picks an admin) or the databases become undeployable until an undocumented psql intervention. The deploy-pause variable and the required real-row rehearsal (line 26) give operators a chance to catch it, so P2.

**Change**: Amend P4.1 step 3, plan line 515 and design §12 line 988: (a) define eligibility: `created_by IS NOT NULL`, users row not deleted, `org_memberships` row for `workspaces.org_id`; (b) migration 002 adds `owner_user_id` NULLABLE plus `workspace_owner_resolutions(workspace_id PK REFERENCES workspaces, owner_user_id NOT NULL REFERENCES users, resolved_by, note, created_at)` and a function `workspace_owner_preflight()` returning `(workspace_id, org_id, reason null_creator|creator_not_member|creator_deleted)`, then backfills eligible creators and resolution rows; (c) the API treats a NULL owner as fail closed (`WORKSPACE_OWNER_UNRESOLVED` on Start and writes); (d) the NOT NULL constraint lands in P4.5 step 15's strict-enforcement migration, which RAISEs listing remaining ids; (e) add a runbook section to database-migrations.md: run the preflight against dev and QA before opening P4.1 and P4.5, insert resolutions as an org-admin decision, record the result in the PR; (f) P4.1 acceptance adds "preflight against a dev and a QA dump returns zero rows or every row has a resolution".

**Validation to add**: P4.1 upgrade fixtures: (a) eligible creator backfilled; (b) NULL creator leaves owner NULL and the API refuses Start/write; (c) creator with a deleted membership likewise; (d) case (b) plus a resolution row is backfilled; (e) P4.5's enforcement migration aborts atomically on (b)/(c) with zero owners written and a clean no-op re-run of 002. A docker-ci step runs `workspace_owner_preflight()` against the seeded dev database and fails on any unresolved row.

### N59 — [P2] S5 partially addressed: the transfer cutover guard keys on "active" legacy execution, and the frozen provider-account resolver is outside P4.5's steps and tests

**Location**: [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1007; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 666 and 678; [plan §3.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 132; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 708; [workspace-vm-api](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 133; [task_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/task_service.rs) line 8282; [provider_account_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/provider_account_service.rs) line 468; [config_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/config_service.rs) line 634.

**Evidence**: design line 1007: "P4.5 refuses transfer for a workspace with retained active legacy execution until that work is drained or migrated". Plan line 678: "refuse transfer with a clear `WORKSPACE_LEGACY_EXECUTION_ACTIVE` blocker while any such execution is active, and show it in the UI." Plan line 132: "`ProviderAccountService::resolve_frozen_for_dispatch` and its `config_service.rs` caller currently resolve a saved account id; v9 must not treat a prior owner's saved id as current authority." task_service.rs line 8282: "AND claim_target.status IN ('pending')".

**Problem**: Observation: the blocker is defined on active executions; `pending`, retry and unacknowledged-claim rows are not named. Step 0 (line 666) migrates HTTP routes, not the background claim UPDATE that selects `pending` rows with no owner or transfer predicate. The frozen-resolver replacement is assigned to P4.6 step 0a (line 708), which lands after P4.5, and appears in P4.5 only as §3.5 grounding prose. Consequence: a stopped workspace with an old-owner task waiting for capacity has no active execution, so transfer is admitted; when capacity returns the retained scheduler claims the row and launches it under the former owner's frozen account, bypassing the new owner's explicit Resume (workspace-vm-api line 133) and spending the old owner's account. This is the v8 S5 gap carried into the prompt with a rename.

**Change**: Rewrite P4.5 step 8: refuse transfer with `WORKSPACE_LEGACY_WORK_PENDING` while any retained legacy task/job/chat for the workspace is nonterminal (`pending|claimed|running|paused|blocked|recovering`, any sticky runtime, any unacknowledged claim), not only while an attempt is active; take the inventory under the lifecycle/admission lock and recheck it before the owner CAS. Add the transfer barrier and `owner_resume_required=false AND owner_user_id = <frozen launcher owner>` predicates to the claim UPDATE in task_service.rs so a legacy row cannot be claimed after transfer or under a hold. Make the `config_service.rs` caller of `resolve_frozen_for_dispatch` resolve the current `owner_user_id` account or refuse dispatch when they differ. Keep both until P4.7/P4.8 delete the claim path. Amend design line 1007 from "active legacy execution" to "nonterminal legacy work".

**Validation to add**: P4.5 API test: a `pending` legacy task on a stopped workspace with no capacity, transfer request returns `WORKSPACE_LEGACY_WORK_PENDING`; after drain, transfer succeeds. Insert a pending row directly, race a claim transaction started before the owner CAS commits, assert exactly one of claim or transfer wins, and after transfer with capacity restored `claim_task` selects nothing and no `usage_records` or account resolution names the former owner. Repeat for `paused`, `recovering` and a sticky runtime row. A mutation run that drops the new predicates must fail.

### N60 — [P2] Owner leaving the org and tenant offboarding are undefined

**Location**: [design §13](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1045; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 251; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 390; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 500; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 679; [org_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/org_service.rs) line 851.

**Evidence**: design line 1045: "| The owner is unavailable | An org admin transfers ownership; live pairing requires transfer or a fork |". Line 251: "`owner_user_id` names exactly one org member". Plan line 679: "membership removal/departure never silently assigns a new owner". org_service.rs line 851: `pub async fn remove_member(` deletes the membership row with no workspace check.

**Problem**: Observation: nothing says what a workspace becomes when its owner is removed from the org or deleted: whether its running VM stops while the owner's grants still resolve, whether admins are notified, or whether idle stop is the only end. Once P4.1 adds same-org membership integrity, the retained `remove_member` path either fails (FK or trigger) or leaves a non-member owner (write-time check only); the plan does not choose. The delete row (line 390) omits `usage_records`, host retained roots and scratch disks. User erasure (transcripts, `created_by`, `responded_by`, commit authorship) and org deletion (Filestore repos, `repo_secrets`, hosts, provider accounts) appear nowhere in the design, sharing note, plan or §17.2/§17.3. Consequence: ex-member-owned VMs keep spending under ex-member credentials until idle; workspaces become unwritable until an admin notices; tenant data outlives the tenant with no defensible deletion answer.

**Change**: Add design §5.10 "Owner departure, user erasure and org deletion", mirrored in P4.1 item 3, P4.5 items 0/6/9 and P8.8: (1) removing a member who owns workspaces returns `409 OWNER_OF_WORKSPACES` listing them unless the org admin passes `transfer_to=<member>`; each transfer runs the ordinary §5.8 transfer (stop with reason `transfer`, owner CAS, audit) before the membership row is deleted, and the same-org rule is an application check plus deferred trigger, not a plain FK. (2) Delete row: enumerate stores (refs, receipts, chats, session files, tasks, provider_requests, tombstoned child links, host roots) and state that `usage_records` and transfer/fork audit rows survive with the workspace id. (3) User erasure: personal identifiers in `created_by`, `responded_by`, reply authors and audit rows are replaced by a tombstone user id; workspace content is org-owned and unaffected. (4) Org deletion: a `deleting` state; drain and terminate hosts, purge roots, delete repos, `repo_secrets`, provider accounts, then rows, with an audit record.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 API test: remove the owner of a running workspace via `remove_member`; assert 409 with the list; with `transfer_to`, assert the VM stops with a transfer receipt, no attempt dispatches for the old owner, the new owner can Resume, and the membership row is deleted only after the owner CAS. ### N61 — [P2] No policy for CI lanes red outside the PR or for known flakes

**Location**: [plan §2](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 21, 29 and 31; [plan §5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 246; [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) line 16; [design §16.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1091; [cloudbuild-ci-rust.yaml](../infra/ci/cloudbuild-ci-rust.yaml) lines 671–673 and 734–736; [cloudbuild-ci-e2e.yaml](../infra/ci/cloudbuild-ci-e2e.yaml) lines 714–716; [report-flaky-tests.js](../scripts/report-flaky-tests.js).

**Evidence**: plan line 21: "If it leaves either required PR lane red, repair the shared failure within authorized scope or keep the PR open; a pre-existing failure is not a waiver." Line 29: "Both `g5-ci-rust` and `g5-ci-e2e` must pass on the exact current head before merge." Line 31: "changes to the CI lanes are P8.6 and P8.7 only, except where a prompt names a lane it must adjust." AGENTS.md line 16: "A failure inherited from main still leaves CI red: repair the shared failure or keep the PR open."

**Problem**: Observation: "authorized scope" occurs once and is never defined; grep for flak, re-run, intermittent or quarantine across plan, design and AGENTS.md finds no policy; report-flaky-tests.js only records retry-passes. Both lanes carry explicit `-p` crate lists, so an MVP PR that adds a crate must edit a lane §2.11 forbids. P8.6, the PR that adds new crates to the lists, is in wave 13 behind P8.5 and P7.2. Consequence: an agent meeting a red lane its diff did not cause has no sanctioned exit: it cannot merge, cannot touch the lane, and has no re-run or triage procedure. The plan cannot distinguish "red because of the PR" from "red because of infrastructure", so §2.9 is not meaningful; a human improvises each time on the critical path, and the pressure falls on skipping or weakening tests.

**Change**: Add plan §2.14 "CI lane triage": (a) only the CI/CD owner re-runs a lane and records stage, reason and prior main build id on the PR; (b) an agent whose failing stage log names no file it changed posts stage, log excerpt and the last identical main failure under "Blocked" and stops; it never retries or disables a test; (c) define "authorized scope" in §2.1 as an allowlist any PR may edit without naming the lane: adding its new crate to the explicit `-p` lists in both lane files, updating a fixture or workflow retired by the PR (§2.5), bumping a pin its prompt names; (d) a lane red on main for a non-PR cause is a program-owner tracker item in a CI4 row, never a reason to widen a PR. Pull crate-list and retired-lane pruning out of P8.6 into an early CI4 row that precedes P4.2a.

**Validation to add**: Process/manual: extend the §2.1 PR template's "Validation run" with the exact-head build ids of both lanes and, on a re-run, who requested it and the recorded reason; the reviewer rejects a description missing them (no script checks PR descriptions today). Add a contract test under scripts/run-ci-script-tests.py asserting every workspace crate in Cargo.toml appears in both lanes' explicit crate lists, so a PR that adds a crate fails locally until it edits the allowlisted lines.

The retained `require_push` admits CLI user pushes today. Consequence: between P4.3a and P4.5 (weeks on the critical chain) every laptop push returns WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING, or the P4.3a agent invents a flag, or its regression is satisfied only by seeding `workspace_vms` rows by hand. Amend P4.5 item 0: "Add the running-incarnation and open-admission requirement to user receive-pack (git.rs `require_push`/`require_push_ref_updates`): current owner AND `workspace_vms.status=running` AND human mutation admission open, else WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING/WORKSPACE_STOPPING with no `vm.start`." Add P4.3a to the §7.1 "Workspace authority" owners and P4.5 to "Attachment and cache". If the team prefers the gate in P4.3a, state instead that until P4.5 the running-VM half is behind configs key `workspace.push_requires_running_vm` (default false), which P4.5 flips and removes.

P4.5 (api_workspace_vms.rs plus api_git): the same push against a `stopped` row returns WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING with zero `vm.start` sent to the fake host; after real Resume and `vm.event ready` it succeeds; during the stop sequence it returns WORKSPACE_STOPPING. Each test fails if the gate sits in the other PR.

### N65 — [P2] Grant and secret resolution channel is missing from the ctl protocol that claims to be complete

**Location**: [worker-host-api §1](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 14; [worker-host-api §3](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 78, 154 and 155; [worker-host-api §6](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 181; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 507; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 646; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 893.

**Evidence**: worker-host line 14: "it issues `vm.start`, `vm.stop`, `vm.status`, `vm.grants.update` and `host.drain`, and consumes `host.hello`, `host.heartbeat`, `vm.event`, `lease.renew`, `host.credential.renew` and `host.drained`." Line 154: "The host resolves values at boot and keeps them in memory; an unresolved grant is `boot_failed(grant_unresolved)`." Line 181: "The proxy also re-resolves a grant once on an upstream `401` before surfacing the error". Plan line 646: "`credential_grants` and `secrets_env` resolved through the control plane at boot with values kept in host memory".

**Problem**: Observation: the host must turn grant and `secrets_env` references into values "through the control plane" at boot, on `vm.grants.update` and on a 401, but no ctl message or internal HTTP route is named: the enumerated message set (line 14), §3.2/§3.3, design §6.2's list (line 507) and §11 "explicitly absent" contain none, and the only internal route named is the tunnel (line 78). Line 155 and plan line 893 ("the resolve and update routes") presuppose routes without shape, transport, authentication, incarnation binding or audit. P4.4b needs resolution at boot but may not implement proxies or grants.update; no prompt assigns the control-plane handler. Retained code offers nothing to reuse: llm_proxy.rs reads its credential from the host's ambient environment. Consequence: P4.4b and P4.12 invent a secret-fetch API, possibly differently for boot and re-resolve, and a host-credential route without incarnation and spec binding lets any host in the org read any granted secret for any workspace.

**Change**: Worker-host §3.3 (host → control plane, answered by `WorkspaceVmService` on ctl): add `grants.resolve {workspace_id, vm_id, refs: [grant or secrets_env ref], reason: boot | update | reresolve}` → `{values: [(ref, value)]} | stale | unresolved(ref)`. Rules: accepted only when `(workspace_id, vm_id)` is a current incarnation placed on this tunnel's host and every ref appears in that incarnation's `VmSpec` or its latest `vm.grants.update` list; the control plane re-verifies `owner_user_id` and the provider grant against the current owner binding (line 155); each resolution is audited as `(host_id, workspace_id, vm_id, ref, reason)` without the value; values are never logged or journaled; the host drops values when the incarnation ends. State it is the only resolution path for boot, update and the 401 re-resolve. Add the message to line 14, design line 507, plan P4.4a step 1 (types), P4.4b step 1 (host caller and control-plane handler) and P4.12 step 5.

**Validation to add**: P4.4b test with the test control plane: `grants.resolve` for a workspace not placed on this host, for a superseded `vm_id` and for a ref absent from the spec return stale/unresolved with no value, and `vm.start` with a refused resolution answers `boot_failed(grant_unresolved)`. P4.12: the 401 re-resolve issues exactly one `grants.resolve` with `reason=reresolve` bound to the current `vm_id`; a stale incarnation's re-resolve is refused. API test: one audit row per resolved ref carrying `(host_id, workspace_id, vm_id, ref)` and no value.

**Location**: [plan §3.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 150; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 507; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 688; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 270; [design §1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 64.

P4.5 step 15 reconciles from Git refs and applies a strict constraint migration in the same unattended pre-upgrade Job. P4.5 step 15: add "rebuild the projection from Git refs plus legacy rows; legacy working workspaces with generated names are reconciled by setting `workspaces.name = branch_name` (no Git rename) unless the operator supplies an explicit mapping; add the equality and complete-member constraints `NOT VALID`, then `VALIDATE` in the same PR only after the preflight returns zero rows, else fail the migration with the preflight output."

### N71 — [P2] No metrics, SLOs, alerts or traces are named anywhere; operability rests on heartbeat fields and audit documents

**Location**: [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 116; [worker-host-api §9](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 306 and 319; [plan P8.8](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1213; [routes/mod.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/mod.rs) line 282.

**Evidence**: worker-host line 306: "There is no host shell and no inspect command; anything an operator needs beyond the above is a field added to `host.heartbeat` or `vm.status`." Line 116: "the control plane alerts and stops placing on the host when they cross its thresholds". Line 319: "bug: alert, do not retry the same spec". Plan line 1213: "scalability (the single API replica, the tunnel registry, the event bus, the git store)". mod.rs line 282: `.route("/healthz", axum::routing::get(|| async { "OK" }))`.

**Problem**: Observation: the only alerting language in the seven documents is the two worker-host lines; grep for metric, SLO, tracing, prometheus, opentelemetry or dashboard finds nothing else; design §12 lists `usage_records` and `provider_quota_snapshots` as the only measurement tables; §6.3 (line 546) requires measuring cold attach and warm resume without saying where numbers go. The backend exposes `/healthz` and no metrics endpoint; no prometheus or opentelemetry crate is in Cargo.lock. P8.8 writes audit documents after the MVP with no instrumentation. Consequence: every new failure state (`unflushed_loss_at`, `cleanup_status=blocked`, aged `stopping` rows, `workspace_forks.status=aborting`, `unreachable` hosts, proxy `no_grant`/`stale_incarnation` refusals, `lease_expired_killed`) exists only as rows and heartbeat fields nobody aggregates; the P4.13 agent must invent the alert line 116 requires or emit a log line; first incidents are found by users and the P8.8 scalability audit has no data.

**Change**: Add design §6.11 "Metrics and alerts" (or a §13 subsection): g5-api exposes `GET /metrics` (Prometheus text, internal network only), and each bounded context owns a named set. Worker & Host: `g5_host_unreachable_total`, `g5_vm_lease_expired_killed_total`, `g5_vm_stale_killed_total`, `g5_slots_in_use`/`g5_slots_total` per host, `g5_vm_boot_seconds{warm|cold}`, `g5_proxy_refusals_total{code}`. Workspace: `g5_flush_seconds`, `g5_flush_failures_total{reason}`, `g5_unflushed_loss_total`, `g5_stopping_rows_age_seconds` (max), `g5_cleanup_blocked_total`, `g5_queued_for_capacity_depth`. Agents: `g5_attempt_outcomes_total{outcome}`, `g5_native_request_timeouts_total`. Git: `g5_receipt_verify_failures_total`, `g5_repo_bytes{repo}`. Control plane: `g5_tunnel_reconnects_total`. Define the alert conditions the text implies (line 116 thresholds, line 319 `spec_rejected`, `lease_expired_killed > 0`, `stopping_rows_age_seconds > stop_deadline + lease_ttl`). Plan §2.1 PR template: a "Metrics added" line; P4.13 item 1 wires the threshold alert to a recording rule in the QA project; P8.7 item 4 adds a QA dashboard and records three MVP SLIs (attach success rate, warm attach p95, receipt completion rate at graceful stop).

**Validation to add**: Per affected PR, an integration test scrapes `/metrics` and asserts the named counter increments on the tested failure path: P4.5, a refused flush increments `g5_flush_failures_total{reason=push_refused}`; P4.13, three missed heartbeats increment `g5_host_unreachable_total`; P4.4b fake-host test, a lease expiry increments `g5_vm_lease_expired_killed_total`. The P8.7 rehearsal reads the dashboard and records the three SLI values in docs/rollout.md.

### N73 — [P3] Child deletion tombstone and result FK behavior undefined

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 193–196, 216, 223, 227; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 390; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 458; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 988; [agent-api §2.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 82; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 515; [workspace_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/workspace_service.rs) lines 5644–5653.

**Evidence**: §5.8 line 390: "delete chats, session files, tasks and rows | gone |". §5.9 line 458: "Ordinary Delete must preserve parent-visible published results and provenance; delete child compute/history under its normal explicit policy, with a tombstoned child reference in the request." §5.1 line 227: "workspace_results(id, request_id UNIQUE, submission_id UNIQUE, producer_task_id, producer_attempt,"; line 223: "workspace_result_submissions(id, request_id, task_id, attempt, exec_id, owner_user_id, idempotency_key,"; line 196: "created_by, created_at, deleted_at)". Agent-api line 82: "The immutable candidates retain truthful provenance and remain pending/history rather than published findings."

**Problem**: Observation: the delete row hard-deletes tasks and rows and yields "gone", while `workspaces` keeps `deleted_at` (retained `attempt_workspace_tombstone` soft-deletes by setting it), §5.9 requires a tombstoned child reference with results and provenance preserved, and `workspace_results.producer_task_id` and `workspace_result_submissions.task_id` point at the tasks Delete removes. No document says which row is the tombstone (soft-deleted `workspaces` row, nulled `child_workspace_id` plus snapshot, or a separate table), whether `child_workspace_id UNIQUE` survives, or the FK semantics. Consequence: P4.1 must pick one now, and the options are mutually exclusive: CASCADE deletes result history (forbidden), RESTRICT blocks Delete for any reviewed child (contradicts "gone"), no FK dangles provenance, soft delete contradicts "delete ... rows". P4.14 and P9.3 will then be written against whatever P4.1 chose.

**Change**: Amend design §5.1 (after `workspace_results`) and the §5.8 delete row: child Delete soft-deletes the `workspaces` row (`deleted_at`) and hard-deletes chats, session files, refs and receipts; `tasks`, `task_attempts` and `workspace_result_submissions` rows referenced by a `workspace_results` row are retained (FK ON DELETE RESTRICT from results and submissions to tasks; unreferenced tasks are deleted). `workspace_requests.child_workspace_id` is never nulled and keeps UNIQUE; the tombstone is the child's `deleted_at`, which result routes render as "child deleted". Change the delete row's Result to "gone from listings; rows retained as result provenance". Copy the rule into plan P4.1 item 12 and P4.5 item 1 (`delete`).

**Validation to add**: P4.1 migration fixture plus P4.14 API test: publish a result, Delete the child (normal and force); assert `result get/list` still return payload and provenance to a parent reader, the request shows the tombstoned child, referenced `tasks` and submission rows resolve, a new request cannot bind the tombstoned child id, and Delete of a child without results removes its tasks.

### N75 — [P3] `exec_id` is stored as a column and used in UNIQUE keys but its text encoding is defined nowhere

**Location**: [design §7.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 732, 735; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 674–679; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 223; [agent-api §4.2](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 145, 167; [agent-api §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 223; [worker-host-api §6.5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 243; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 555.

**Evidence**: Design line 732: "`exec_id = (task_id, attempt, vm_id)`." Line 679: "UNIQUE (exec_id, provider_request_id))". Line 223: "workspace_result_submissions(id, request_id, task_id, attempt, exec_id, owner_user_id, idempotency_key,". Worker-host line 243: "plain `G5D_EXEC_ID` and `G5D_SESSION_ID` correlation values". Agent-api line 167: "The task/attempt fields must match `exec_id`".

**Problem**: Observation: `exec_id` is defined as a tuple, yet `provider_requests` and `workspace_result_submissions` store it as a scalar beside `task_id, attempt, vm_id`, the uniqueness key uses the scalar, the journal path is `/var/lib/g5d/journal/<exec_id>`, and the VM receives it as a `G5D_EXEC_ID` string. No document gives the canonical string form or names the minter. Consequence: P4.6 (API), P4.2b (dispatcher directory name), P4.11 (env export) and P4.14 (CLI echo) must agree on an encoding for a uniqueness key. A mismatch (separator, case, UUID form) fails loudly at the first invoke or, worse, yields duplicate provider_requests rows or `stale_execution` on valid replies. The API cannot check "fields must match `exec_id`" without a fixed rule.

**Change**: Add one sentence to design §7.5 "Execution identity" and agent-api §4.2: "The API mints the scalar `exec_id` as `<task_id>.<attempt>.<vm_id>` (lowercase hyphenated UUID, decimal attempt, `vm_id` text as stored) when it persists the invocation intent; the dispatcher, host, adapter and `g5d` treat it as an opaque filesystem-safe token and never derive it. Every table storing `exec_id` also stores `task_id, attempt, vm_id`; the API checks the triple matches on insert, and P7.2 may make `exec_id` a generated column." Reference it from the P4.1 step 6, P4.2b step 3, P4.6 steps 4 and 6 and P4.14 step 3 prompts.

**Validation to add**: P4.6 unit test: `exec_id_for(task, attempt, vm_id)` matches a golden string; an insert into provider_requests whose `exec_id` disagrees with its triple is rejected. P4.2b integration test: `modules.invoke` with the golden id creates `/var/lib/g5d/journal/<exec_id>` and a repeat returns `already_running`. P4.14 test: `g5d pr result submit` sends `G5D_EXEC_ID` unchanged; the API accepts it for the current attempt and answers `stale_execution` for a prior attempt's value.
