P6.4/P7.2 acceptance grep: `WorkspaceStatus` and `workspaces.status` absent from backend and frontend; an upgrade fixture with rows in every legacy value migrates and the column is gone.

### N77 — [P3] Base workspace ownership has undefined administration semantics

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 251; [design §5.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 271; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 383–390; [sharing "Access and identity"](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 9; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 670, 676; [workspace_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/workspace_service.rs) line 5567.

**Evidence**: Design line 251: "Base workspaces retain an owner for administration but remain read-only and have no VM." Sharing line 9: "Ownership alone does not make a base workspace writable; base workspaces have no VM and require a checked fork."

**Problem**: Observation: `owner_user_id NOT NULL` applies to base workspaces, which "retain an owner for administration". No clause defines that administration: stop/resume/restart are meaningless without a VM, the §5.8 delete row does not exempt bases although retained code refuses "Cannot remove Base" (line 5567), and nothing says whether a base may be transferred, whether its visibility may be `invite_only`, or who owns a base at repository onboarding. Consequence: P4.5 must return something for the six actions on a base row and P4.10 must decide which controls to render; the two agents will invent error codes and base rules independently, and an `invite_only` base would silently remove fork-from-base for org members.

**Change**: Add to design §5.2 (base workspace bullet, line 271): "A base is owned by the user who connected the repository; an org admin may transfer it. Base administration is transfer, visibility and invite administration only; base visibility defaults to `org`, and `invite_only` is permitted with the same read rule. stop/resume/restart on a base return WORKSPACE_IS_BASE (409) without touching `workspace_vms`; delete of a base is refused while the repository is connected (the current Cannot-remove-Base rule)." Reference this from §5.7 and add a matching sentence to plan P4.5 item 0 and P4.10 item 2.

**Validation to add**: api_workspace_vms.rs: stop/resume/restart on a base by its owner return WORKSPACE_IS_BASE with no `workspace_vms` row and no `vm.start`; delete of a base returns the refusal; transfer of a base by an org admin succeeds and the new owner still cannot save or push; fork-from-base by an ordinary member succeeds with `visibility=org` and is 403 under `invite_only` until invited. Frontend test: the base shell renders no Stop/Resume/Restart and shows "Fork and ...".

### N78 — [P3] Org role vocabulary is inconsistent: 'org admin' vs org role owner|admin, and viewer's ability to fork/own/receive transfers is undefined

**Location**: [design §10.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 922; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 251, 253; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 392; [sharing "Access and identity"](workspace-sharing-v9.md) lines 7–9; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) steps 9–10; [plan P6.4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) step 2; [git.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/git.rs) line 1368; [permission_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/permission_service.rs) lines 245, 340.

**Evidence**: Design line 922: "Org roles `owner|admin|member|viewer` and the org visibility/invite read model are kept." Line 251: "Org admins read any workspace; other org members read when visibility is `org` or their invite is present". Line 392: "**Forking a workspace** is allowed to any authorized reader and creates a new workspace owned by that reader." git.rs line 1368: `if matches!(role.as_str(), "owner" | "admin" | "member") {`.

**Problem**: Observation: the design says "org admin" throughout while the retained role set has `owner` and `admin`; retained code treats them together, so that half is answerable by tracing. The `viewer` half is not: `viewer` is a kept role and an "org member", hence an "authorized reader" entitled to fork (creating an owned workspace and consuming compute) and a transfer target ("any org member"), yet on main a viewer holds only read scopes and cannot create workspaces, and P6.4 drops the scope tables that enforce that. Consequence: an agent implementing P4.5 step 10 (authorize source read, then fork) lets a viewer own workspaces; an agent keeping today's scope check silently changes viewer behaviour when P6.4 lands. Divergent authorization across routes, and a viewer role that either cannot review (blocking the P4.14 value loop for read-only staff) or spends compute unexpectedly.

**Change**: Amend design §10.4 and the sharing doc "Access and identity": (1) "'Org admin' in this design means org role `owner` or `admin`; both may read any workspace and transfer to any eligible member including themselves." (2) "Only `owner|admin|member` may own a workspace: a `viewer` may browse, clone/fetch and comment per visibility and invites but cannot fork, accept a review request, be a transfer target or be a backfilled owner (P4.1 step 3); refuse with 403 ORG_ROLE_READ_ONLY." Add the rule to P4.5 steps 9 and 10, P4.14 step 1 and P6.4 step 2.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api test `workspace_role_matrix`: for each org role, exercise read of an org-visible workspace, fork, being named as transfer target and admin transfer-to-self through the real routes; expect viewer fork and transfer-target 403 ORG_ROLE_READ_ONLY and org `owner` treated exactly like `admin`. It fails without the rule because fork authorizes on source read alone. Re-run it in P6.4's coverage after the scope tables are dropped.

### N79 — [P3] Org-admin self-transfer needs no owner consent and triggers no notification; the audit row is the only trace

**Location**: [design §10.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 922; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 251; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 337; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 389, 420; [design §6.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 637; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 984; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) step 9; [decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) rows 12 and 18.

**Evidence**: Design line 922: "Org admins may read any workspace and transfer ownership to any org member, including themselves, but must take ownership before writing." Line 251: "The transfer audit preserves the old owner, new owner, requester and completion time (§5.8); a pending or aborted operation is not a completed transfer." Line 637: "Terminals are not recorded or audited in this iteration." Line 337: "the user is notified. Nothing is lost because nothing was torn down."

**Problem**: Observation: admin transfer is a meeting decision and is audited in `workspace_ownership_transfers`. The design notifies users on flush failure (line 337) and before deadline-bound teardowns (line 420), but not the previous or new owner when a transfer completes; no route or view reads the audit table; the new owner then has unaudited terminal access to the workspace's secrets. Consequence: an admin can stop an owner's agents mid-turn, take the workspace, read secrets and dirty edits in the terminal and transfer back, while the old owner sees only "stopped" and a changed owner field. Insider-risk review has no trail beyond a table nobody surfaces.

**Change**: Amend design §5.8 "Transfer completion and recovery": in the final owner-commit transaction also insert a `notifications` row for the previous owner and one for the new owner (kind `workspace.ownership_transferred`, carrying workspace, from, to, requester and whether the requester was an org admin) and append a `notice` history event to the workspace's chat sessions; write the same rows for a stopped-workspace transfer. Add to plan P4.5 step 9: "The owner-commit transaction writes the two notifications and the notice event; an aborted transfer writes only the existing failure notification." Note an org-level transfer audit list and an optional reason field in §17.2 as later phase.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api test: an org admin transfers a stopped workspace to themselves; assert `workspace_ownership_transfers` has the completed row, `notifications` has exactly one row for the previous owner and one for the admin with the transfer fields, and a workspace reader sees the notice event in chat history; a replayed identical transfer adds no second notification; an aborted transfer (flush failure) produces no transferred notification. Fails today because no notification rows exist.

### N80 — [P3] Vocabulary drift: 'response deadline' vs 'runner wait', three idle-activity definitions, 'healthy' undefined, 'C3' used before definition, `timeout`/`timed_out`, handle state `ready` vs `running`, delivery diagram `released`, receive-pack STOPPING vs NOT_RUNNING

**Location**: [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 293; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 414; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 450; [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 665, 677; [design §7.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 755; [design §8.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 780; [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 858; [design §9.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 864; [agent-api §4.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 123; [agent-api §8.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 289; [agent-api §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 385; [workspace-vm-api §1](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) line 24; [workspace-vm-api §2](workspace-vm-api-v9.md) lines 42–49; [worker-host-api §3.1](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 89; [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 27; [plan](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 101, 670, 710–720.

**Evidence**: Design line 414: "activity = running bounded executions, an open terminal, preview traffic (mere service existence is not activity), file RPC, a live native request within its response deadline; not an open browser tab"; line 858: "attached-owner live file RPC may count as interactive activity under §5.8"; plan line 670: "an open question within its runner wait". Vm-api line 42: "Current owner with attached healthy VM". Design line 450: "a delayed old-attempt submission or C3 cannot replace it"; line 780 defines C3. Design line 665: "reason exit_code|provider_error|vm_lost|timeout|cancelled|interrupted|suspended,"; agent-api line 385: "\"timed_out\" | \"suspended\";". Agent-api line 123: "state: \"starting\" | \"ready\" | \"stopping\" | \"failed\";". Agent-api line 289: "+-> delivery_unknown -> delivered | released". Design line 293: "reject with `WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING` or `WORKSPACE_STOPPING`"; vm-api line 24: "otherwise `WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING` without auto-boot".

**Problem**: Observation: one concept, several names: the native wait is "response deadline" in the design and Agent API but "runner wait" in the plan (lines 101, 670); file RPC is definite activity in §5.8 but "may count" in §9.1 and "while already running" in P4.5; every companion row is gated by "attached healthy VM" but "healthy" is never defined (the design says "running, admission-open"); C3 is used at line 450 and defined at 780; attempt reason `timeout` vs outcome status `timed_out` with no status-to-reason mapping in the P4.6 prompt; the handle uses `ready` where `workspace_vms.status` uses `running`; the inbox diagram target `released` is not one of the five delivery values (line 755); receive-pack on a draining incarnation returns STOPPING in the design but the companion names only NOT_RUNNING. Consequence: P4.6 invents the `AgentTurnOutcome.status` to `task_attempts.reason` mapping, P4.5 decides whether `starting` is healthy for owner admission and whether an owner `fs.read` advances `last_activity_at`; the idle timer then depends on which reading won.

**Change**: (1) §7.1: adopt N88's direction, which matches the normative Agent API: rename the reason value `timeout` to `timed_out` and use N88's mapping (`reason` is the outcome status plus the API-only settlements `exit_code` and `vm_lost`); this finding adds no mapping of its own. Paste N88's table into P4.6 item 4. (2) Companions (vm-api §2 intro, worker-host line 89, sharing line 27): "attached healthy VM" = `workspace_vms.status = running`, admission open, latest private `health` passing; `starting` and `stopping` are not healthy. (3) §5.8 idle row is the single activity list; §9.1 says "counts as activity per §5.8"; P4.5 item 1 quotes §5.8 and plan lines 101 and 670 say "response deadline". (4) Agent-api line 123: `ready` becomes `running`. (5) Agent-api line 289: `released` becomes `-> queued (reservation released)`. (6) Vm-api line 24: add `WORKSPACE_STOPPING`. (7) §5.9 line 450: "(C3, the terminal attempt outcome, §8.3)".

**Validation to add**: P4.6: a unit test over every `AgentTurnOutcomeV1.status` asserting the persisted `task_attempts.reason`, with an exhaustive match so a new status without a mapping fails the build. P4.5: owner `fs.read` against a `running` VM advances `last_activity_at`; the same call against `starting` returns WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING without touching it; receive-pack on a `stopping` incarnation returns WORKSPACE_STOPPING and never boots.

### N81 — [P3] stop_reason vocabulary differs between the actions table, the teardown table, the flush table, the decisions register and the P4.5 prompt

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 237; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 309; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 386, 388, 411, 413; [design §18](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1175; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 498; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 670.

**Evidence**: Design line 388: "| **restart** | the stop sequence with reason `restart`, then resume immediately". Line 411: "| `user` (stop, restart, delete) | the user | none | abort, the VM stays up |". Line 413: "| `purpose_complete` | request/result reconciler after durable final result (§5.9) |". Line 1175: "Teardown reasons: user, transfer, idle, drain, rollout, credential, capacity; nudge before deadline-bound teardowns". Line 237: "stop_reason NULL, stop_deadline_at NULL,". Plan line 670: "abort for user/restart/transfer/idle on failure; purpose_complete preserves the published result".

**Problem**: Observation: the actions table uses `restart` as a stop reason; the teardown table folds restart into `user` and adds `purpose_complete`; the §18 register lists seven reasons without `restart` or `purpose_complete`; the P4.5 prompt writes `restart` as a distinct reason; the §5.1 sketch and P4.1 prompt give `stop_reason NULL` with no value set. ("operator drain" in the flush table maps to `drain`, whose decider is "an operator or the rotation loop".) Consequence: a P4.1 agent or reviewer who derives a CHECK from the teardown table or the register omits `restart` and `purpose_complete`, and P4.5's later write of `restart` violates it; the failure-policy lookup (abort vs proceed-at-deadline) has no row keyed `restart`; the register misleads reviewers about the accepted set.

**Change**: Design §5.1 `workspace_vms`: replace `stop_reason NULL` with `stop_reason user|restart|transfer|purpose_complete|idle|drain|rollout|credential|capacity NULL`. §5.8 teardown table: split the first row into `user` (stop, delete) and `restart` (same decider, deadline and failure policy as `user`) so each row is one value. §5.4 flush table: replace the prose list with "every graceful teardown (§5.8, any stop_reason)". §18 line 1175: list all nine values. Plan P4.1 step 1: state the same enum (text with CHECK or a Postgres enum, per docs/database-migrations.md).

**Validation to add**: P4.1 migration test: inserting each of the nine values into `workspace_vms.stop_reason` succeeds and an unlisted value (`explicit`, `operator_drain`) fails the CHECK; fails without the constraint. P4.5 test: call stop(reason) for every value and assert the row's `stop_reason` equals the input and the failure policy resolves (abort, cancel-and-restart-timer, proceed-at-deadline, preserve-result) with no unmatched arm; fails if `restart` or `purpose_complete` has no policy row.

### N82 — [P3] No rate limits or bounds on the new tenant-driven amplifiers: forks trigger provider namespace checks per member repo, lifecycle actions consume host boot capacity, inbox/replies/result payloads have no stated caps

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 399, 446; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 231; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 510; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 643; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 661ff; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 931; [rate_limit.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/middleware/rate_limit.rs) lines 1–19.

**Evidence**: Design line 510: "**Limits** are host configuration with defaults and definite refusals: `slots_total`, `max_concurrent_boots` (2), `max_pty_streams_per_vm` (8), `max_http_streams_per_vm` (256), `max_inflight_commands` (16, `busy`), `max_ctl_message_bytes` (1 MiB), `proxy_max_body_bytes` (32 MiB), `result_retention_secs` (600), `log_tail_bytes` (64 KiB)." rate_limit.rs line 1: "//! Rate limit middleware for the g5-api HTTP processing pipeline."

**Problem**: Observation: host-side limits are concrete; control-plane limits are absent (grep for rate limit, RATE_LIMITED, 429, 413 across the seven documents finds nothing). The only retained limiter is the pre-auth governor protecting JWT verification. The checked fork inspects the provider branch namespace in every provider-connected member repo on each attempt (line 399), and a verification failure is retryable rather than a free name; nothing caches provider ref listings or bounds forks per org. Restart/Resume have no per-user rate while `max_concurrent_boots` (2) is per host. `workspace_results` payloads are "bounded" with no number (line 446, plan line 931); `workspace_result_replies` is append-only with no cap; inbox depth and `chat_attachments` sizes are unstated. Consequence: implementers do not add limits nobody names. A fork storm or UI retry loop exhausts the org's GitHub or GitLab quota, secondary rate limits make every fork a retryable failure for hours, and one owner's restart loop delays other tenants' boots on the same host. Impact is intra-org availability and third-party quota, not data exposure.

**Change**: Add a "Control-plane limits" table to design §6.2 beside the host limits (or a new §6.11) and copy defaults into §17.1: `fork_ops_per_org_per_hour`, `pending_forks_per_user`, provider namespace listing cached per repo for `provider_ref_cache_secs` (e.g. 30 s) with conditional requests, `resume_restart_per_workspace_per_minute`, `inbox_depth_per_session`, `result_payload_max_bytes` (e.g. 1 MiB, enforced identically by the route and `g5d pr result submit`), `replies_per_result`, `attachment_max_bytes`; each with its refusal (`429 RATE_LIMITED` with Retry-After via the existing middleware shape, or `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`) and its key (user or org). Add the cache and fork bounds to P4.5 steps 10 and 11, the payload and reply caps to P4.14 steps 3 and 4, the restart rate to P4.5 step 1.

**Validation to add**: HTTP-boundary tests: fork against a fake provider returning secondary-rate-limit errors makes at most one listing per member repo per attempt and zero provider calls on a second attempt within the TTL; a storm past `fork_ops_per_org_per_hour` returns 429 with Retry-After and creates no ref; `g5d pr result submit` and the route reject a payload one byte over the cap with 413 and accept one at the cap; replies past `replies_per_result` are refused; a restart loop past the rate is refused while another org's boot on the same host completes within `max_concurrent_boots`.

### N83 — [P3] Lease timer arming after renewal is contradictory in the host note

**Location**: [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 112; [worker-host-api §5](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 166; [worker-host-api §10](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 351; [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 554; [design §14](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1025; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 648; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 670.

**Evidence**: Worker-host line 112: "The host resets its local timer to `lease_ttl_secs` from receipt (a duration, so host and control-plane clocks never need to agree)". Line 166: "On renewal it is armed from the request's departure time plus the returned TTL, never from reply arrival". Design line 554: "The host arms its timer from the *monotonic time at which the renewal request left the host* plus the TTL, not from the reply's arrival". Plan line 670: "a lease of `lease_ttl_secs + start_deadline_secs`".

**Problem**: Observation: inside the normative companion, the `lease.renew` message row says the timer is reset from receipt of the reply, while §5, §10, design §6.4 and the P4.4b prompt say departure time with sequence numbers. The row is stale text carried from v8 and v7 (the V7-3 fix touched §6.4 and companion §5 but not this row) and omits the sequence-number rule. A second, smaller gap: design §6.4 ties the boot grant to the `vm.start` command deadline, but no text says that deadline is computed from the placement CAS (grant time) rather than send time, or that a re-sent `vm.start` (after `busy` or `timeout`) carries the original deadline. Consequence: the message table is the row a handler is written from; arming from reply arrival lets a delayed reply extend the host deadline past the control-plane grant, the exact superseded-VM-keeps-writing hazard of design §14 line 1025. For boot, a send at T1 > T0 (CAS) with deadline T1+D lets the host expire at T1+D+TTL > T0+D+TTL.

**Change**: In worker-host §3.3 replace the line 112 sentence with: "Each renewal carries a per-VM monotonic sequence number. The host arms its timer at the monotonic time the request left the host plus `lease_ttl_secs` (a duration, so host and control-plane clocks never need to agree), never at reply arrival; a reply whose sequence number is not the latest outstanding one is ignored." In design §6.4 and worker-host §5 add: "The `vm.start` command deadline is `lease_granted_at + start_deadline_secs`, computed in the placement CAS transaction; a re-sent `vm.start` carries the same deadline and idempotency key." Amend P4.4b item 3 and P4.5 item 1 accordingly.

**Validation to add**: Keep the P4.4b test "a delayed renewal reply followed by a partition expires the host timer before the server grant". Add a proptest in backend/g5d-host: for random departure times, handling delays and reply delays, `host_deadline <= grant_expires_at` always holds and a stale-sequence reply never extends the deadline. P4.5 test: a `vm.start` re-sent after `busy` carries the original deadline, and a host receiving it after that deadline answers `expired` while the control-plane lease has already lapsed.

### N84 — [P3] Proxy re-resolves a grant on every upstream 401 with no rate limit, and a guest can force 401s by sending its own Authorization header

**Location**: [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 570, 572; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 510; [worker-host-api §6](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 181; [plan P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 889.

**Evidence**: Design line 570: "A real `Authorization` header a client set itself is forwarded untouched." Design line 572: "re-resolves a grant once on upstream `401`". Plan line 889: "re-resolve once on an upstream 401."

**Problem**: Observation: "once" is per request, not per grant or time window, and the no-clobbering rule means any guest process can send a bogus `Authorization` header that the provider rejects with 401, triggering a control-plane secret resolution each time. No document restricts re-resolution to requests where the proxy attached the credential, or bounds it per grant; §17.1 lists backoff schedules for flush pushes, receipts and merge imports only. Consequence: an agent implementing P4.12 step 5 literally builds a guest-triggerable, unbounded secret-store read path bounded only by `max_http_streams_per_vm` (256): Secret Manager or KMS quota use and control-plane load from one VM, and noisy audit on the secret resource that masks real rotations. Operability, not a credential boundary breach, since the exposure stays inside the VM.

**Change**: Amend design §6.5 line 572, worker-host §6 line 181 and plan P4.12 step 5 to: "The proxy re-resolves a grant on an upstream 401 only when it attached the credential itself (never when the client supplied its own Authorization header), at most once per grant per `grant_reresolve_min_interval_secs` (default 60) with exponential backoff up to 15 minutes on repeated 401s; a suppressed re-resolution surfaces the 401 unchanged. Count `grant_reresolutions` and `grant_reresolutions_suppressed` on `host.heartbeat` next to `proxy_upstream_errors`." Add the interval to the §6.2 limits list and to §17.1.

**Validation to add**: P4.12 proxy unit tests against the fake upstream: (a) 100 requests carrying a client-supplied bad Authorization header receive 401 and the fake control plane records zero grant resolutions; (b) a proxy-attached credential receiving 401 triggers exactly one re-resolution, and 50 further 401s inside the interval trigger none and surface as 401; (c) after the interval one more re-resolution occurs; (d) the heartbeat carries both counters. Tests (a) and (b) fail against a literal once-per-request implementation.

### N85 — [P3] `runtime.ready` payload: P4.2a omits `module_catalog_version`, P4.2b does not add it, but P4.5 records it on `ready`

**Location**: [design §6.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 475; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 542; [design §6.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 578; [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 111; [agent-api §4.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 124; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 498; [plan P4.2a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 533; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 543–565; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 670.

**Evidence**: Design line 578: "report `runtime.ready(vm_id, epoch, module_catalog_version, dispatcher_contract_version)`." Plan line 533: "`runtime.ready` (sent to the peer on boot with `vm_id`, `epoch`, `dispatcher_contract_version`)". Plan line 670: "`ready` (`vm.event ready` → `running`, record `module_catalog_version`)".

**Problem**: Observation: P4.2a's handshake carries three fields and its Do-not forbids the catalog; P4.2b loads the catalog but never mentions extending `runtime.ready`; the host companion's `vm.event` payload (line 111) and P4.4a step 1 carry no catalog field either; P4.5 records `module_catalog_version` on `ready` into a column declared without NULL (plan line 498). Consequence: a field the consumer requires is owned by no producer and no forwarder. P4.5 records null or empty, `WorkspaceRuntimeHandleV1.module_catalog_version` (agent-api line 124) is empty, and invariant 13 of §4.3 (catalog version negotiated per incarnation) is silently unmet, or the P4.5 agent invents the field across g5d-dispatch, g5d-host and the API.

**Change**: P4.2b Do: add step "1a. Extend the private `runtime.ready` frame of P4.2a with `module_catalog_version` (the sha256 of `/opt/g5/modules/catalog.json` as loaded), sent after the catalog is loaded; keep P4.2a's three identity fields." P4.4a step 1 and worker-host line 111: `vm.event kind: ready` carries `{module_catalog_version, dispatcher_contract_version}` forwarded verbatim from `runtime.ready`. P4.5 step 1: `ready` records the forwarded value and refuses a `ready` without it (`failed`, `last_error=catalog_version_missing`). Amend the design §6.2 `vm.event` payload text accordingly.

**Validation to add**: P4.2b integration test: the test client receives `runtime.ready` whose `module_catalog_version` equals the sha256 of the catalog file, and mutating the file changes the value. P4.4a test: a `vm.event ready` replayed after a tunnel drop still carries both version fields. P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: after a `ready` event the row is `running` with a non-null `module_catalog_version` equal to the event's, and `workspace.runtime.get` returns it in the handle; a `ready` without the field leaves the row not `running`.

### N86 — [P3] `agent.turn` manifest declares no limits; the per-VM bounded-execution slot count that dispatch depends on is undefined, as is its interplay with `jobs.max_concurrency`

**Location**: [design §6.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 605; [design §7.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 724; [design §7.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 728; [agent-api §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 170–190; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 1; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 713; [plan P4.8](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 756ff; [capacity.rs](../backend/g5-api-contracts/src/capacity.rs) lines 124–134.

**Evidence**: Design line 605: "limits (time for bounded runs, CPU, memory, disk, concurrency, output and log size; preview explicitly has no wall-clock cap or bounded-work concurrency charge)". Agent-api line 182: "requested_capabilities:" (the manifest has operations, capabilities and observability but no `limits` block). Design line 724: "A preview uses its one-active-preview gate, while bounded tasks require capacity." Plan line 713: "for running VMs with a free bounded-execution slot (or a preview service task, which uses no such slot)". Design line 728: "Child sessions may run concurrently up to `max_concurrency` in the same member-repository checkouts."

**Problem**: Observation: §6.7 says every module declares limits including concurrency, but the `agent.turn` manifest has no `limits` block; the dispatch loop needs "a free bounded-execution slot" per VM, yet no document gives the slot count, its owner (manifest, VmSpec, org or workspace setting) or how `jobs.max_concurrency` is capped by it. P4.2b's runner limits list time, CPU, memory and output only. Retained code has a per-worker `max_concurrent_tasks` budget that v9 does not map to the per-VM model. Consequence: the P4.6 implementer picks a number and a source of truth; P4.8 may launch `max_concurrency` children P4.6 cannot admit, leaving them `queued` with no visible reason, or an unbounded default exhausts a slot-sized VM.

**Change**: Amend agent-api §5 manifest to add `limits: { wall_clock_secs, cpu, memory_mib, output_bytes, log_bytes, concurrency }` (MVP default concurrency 2 per VM, from the catalog entry, overridable downward by an org setting) and state that the catalog rejects a `bounded_run` module without `limits`. Amend design §7.3 after "bounded tasks require capacity" and plan P4.6 item 2: a free slot is `agent.turn.limits.concurrency` minus running bounded executions on that incarnation (preview and `workspace.bootstrap` exempt as stated), with a `queued_for_slot` wait reason distinct from `queued_for_capacity` (placement). Amend design §7.4 and plan P4.8: effective fan-out is `min(jobs.max_concurrency, free slots)`, remaining children `queued_for_slot`. Add concurrency to P4.2b item 1 runner limits.

**Validation to add**: P4.6 TaskService test at the dispatch boundary: a running VM with catalog `agent.turn.limits.concurrency=2` receives a job with `max_concurrency=4`; exactly two `modules.invoke` calls are issued, the other two children stay `queued` with `wait_reason=queued_for_slot`, and a preview task on the same VM is still admitted. P4.2b catalog test: a `bounded_run` entry without `limits` fails the catalog integrity probe.

### N87 — [P3] Per-org single-tenant host capacity model and its consequences unstated

**Location**: [worker-host-api §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 149; [worker-host-api §7](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 270–278; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 510, 512; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 641, 643, 645; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 978, 984; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 910; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) lines 4413–4422; [rotation.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/cloud/rotation.rs) line 46.

**Evidence**: Worker-host line 149: "A host serves one org." Design line 643: "then `no_capacity`, surfaced to the user as `queued_for_capacity` with a position and retried by the dispatch loop. Org quota is `max_running_vms`, enforced before `vm.start`." Plan line 910: "Placement function `place(workspace, org)`: a host in the org's region with a free slot holding the workspace's retained root, else any host in the region with a free slot, else `no_capacity`". Baseline line 4413: "CREATE TABLE public.orgs (" (id, name, slug, created_at, two base-image columns; no region). rotation.rs line 46: "Opt-in: `G5_HOST_ROTATION_ENABLED` defaults to false."

**Problem**: Observation: hosts are single-tenant per org with equal slots. Placement is "a host in the org's region", but no table defines an org's region: `orgs` has none, §12 adds none, and `region` is a required per-host field, so an org can have hosts in several regions. `max_running_vms` is the org quota but appears in no table and no P4.1 item. Hosts are operator-provisioned one at a time (line 512); no rule says how many hosts a new org gets, when the SaaS adds or removes them, or that the retained provision-then-drain rotation loop is off by default. `queued_for_capacity` has "a position" but no ordering, aging, expiry or cancellation rule. Operators cannot tell whether a shared multi-org pool was rejected or overlooked. Consequence: P4.1, P4.5 and P4.13 will guess the region source and quota storage differently; MVP acceptance passes on hand-provisioned QA hosts while the production SaaS has no defined way to give a new org capacity, bound cost or tell a user how long `queued_for_capacity` lasts.

**Change**: In design §6.10 and worker-host §7 replace "a host in the org's region" with "one of the org's hosts (a host serves one org); when the org has hosts in several regions, prefer the region in `configs(org, <org_id>, 'worker_host_region')`, else any region", and state that `max_running_vms` is `configs(org, <org_id>, 'max_running_vms')` with a named platform default (e.g. 10) counting `starting|running|stopping` rows. Add to §6.10 a "Capacity provisioning" paragraph: for the MVP an org's pool is static and operator-provisioned; a new org has zero hosts until an operator provisions one; the retained rotation loop provides provision-then-drain replacement; automatic scale-out/scale-in and shared multi-org pools are listed in §17.2 as deferred, to be decided at the P8.8 audit with `queued_for_capacity` depth and wait as inputs. Amend P4.1 item 9 to seed the two org config keys and P4.13 item 2 to read them.

**Validation to add**: P4.13 tests: placement considers only hosts with the workspace's `org_id`; with hosts in two regions and the region key set, the retained-root host wins, then the configured region, then any; the quota is read from `configs` with the platform default when unset; a `stopping` row counts. P8.8 checklist item: report `queued_for_capacity` depth, wait p50/p95 and slot utilization per org from QA before deciding on automatic provisioning.

### N88 — [P3] Status vocabularies disagree across design table, companion schema and chat rows; attempt outcome to chat_messages.status mapping is undefined

**Location**: [design §7.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 665; [design §7.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 739; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 756; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 988; [agent-api §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 385; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 4; [plan P4.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 3; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 3436; [persist.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/execution_lifecycle/persist.rs) line 1261.

**Evidence**: Design line 665: "reason exit_code|provider_error|vm_lost|timeout|cancelled|interrupted|suspended,"; agent-api line 385: "\"timed_out\" | \"suspended\";". Design line 756: "status pending|streaming|complete|error|stopped,". Line 739: "Transcript status and attempt/task settlement commit together; repair stranded pending rows from those authorities." Line 988: "Task and chat status vocabularies are one enum each in the database, Rust and the frontend." Baseline line 3436: "CONSTRAINT chat_messages_status_check CHECK ((status = ANY (ARRAY['pending'::text, 'streaming'::text, 'complete'::text, 'error'::text, 'stopped'::text])))".

**Problem**: Observation: `task_attempts.reason` spells `timeout` while the normative `AgentTurnOutcomeV1.status` spells `timed_out`; `vm_lost` exists only API-side. The design requires transcript status and attempt settlement to commit in one transaction but never states which `chat_messages.status` an assistant row takes for `interrupted`, `suspended`, `timed_out`, `cancelled` or `vm_lost`; the only mapping in retained code is the legacy `ChatTurnStatus` that P7.1 deletes. (`delivery` beside `status` is not a contradiction of "one enum each", which means one shared enum per vocabulary.) Consequence: the P4.6 implementer picks `stopped` versus `error` per outcome with no rule; the spelling mismatch lands as two names for one fact; the frontend rule that a held task must not look like a running process depends on the missing mapping.

**Change**: In design §7.1 rename the reason value `timeout` to `timed_out` and state that `reason` is the outcome status plus the two API-only settlements `exit_code` (non-zero exit with no module outcome) and `vm_lost`. Add to §7.5 "Shared transcript": the assistant row settles in the attempt's transaction with `complete` for `succeeded`, `error` for `failed|timed_out|provider_error|exit_code`, and `stopped` for `cancelled|interrupted|suspended|vm_lost`; the reason is exposed through the row's `task_id, attempt` linkage, and the frontend labels a held task from `tasks.status=waiting` plus `hold_reason`, never from the chat row alone. Mirror the table in plan P4.6 item 4 and P4.7 item 3.

**Validation to add**: P4.6 API-boundary test: drive one fake attempt per `AgentTurnOutcomeV1.status` value plus API-side `vm_lost` and `exit_code` settlements through `TaskService::apply`; assert `chat_messages.status`, `task_attempts.reason` and `tasks.status` in one SELECT after commit match the table; a crash injected between the attempt update and commit leaves the assistant row `pending` and the repair pass settles it from the attempt row. Fails today because no mapping exists to assert against.

**Location**: [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 748–755; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 972–994; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 504; [plan P4.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 741; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) lines 3427–3436, 3496–3500; [task_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/task_service.rs) line 18365.

Design line 752: "chat_messages(id, session_id, index, role user|assistant|system,"; baseline line 3432: "message_index integer NOT NULL,". Plan line 504: "`chat_sessions.permission_policy jsonb` and `session_manifest_generation int default 0`; `chat_messages.input_origin human|workflow|continuation`".

Consequence: an agent reading §8.1 as the target adds `provider`, `provider_session_id` or `index` beside the existing columns, giving two writers for one fact (provider, ordering) across P4.7 and P4.8, and P7.2's "drop what nothing uses" cannot decide between them.

Retained physical columns keep their baseline names (`agent_provider`, `provider_state`, `message_index`, `artifacts`, `input_tokens/output_tokens`, `chat_messages.role` including `tool`); renames happen only in P7.2 with a compatibility view, as for timeline_entries." P4.1 step 7: add "`provider_session_id` is a new nullable text column on `chat_sessions`; do not read it from `provider_state`."

**Validation to add**: A schema-lint integration test added in P4.1 that fails if any table has both a baseline column and its design alias (`provider` and `agent_provider`, `index` and `message_index`, `attachments` and `artifacts`, `usage` and `input_tokens`). P4.7 test: session resume reads `chat_sessions.provider_session_id` and fails when only `provider_state` carries it.

**Location**: [design §17.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1123; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 768; [design header](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 5; [agent-api §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 170–230; [agent-api §8.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 297; [agent-api §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 346; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 7; [plan P4.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) acceptance.

Agent-api line 346: "| { kind: \"input.accepted\"; reservation_id: string; input_id: string;". Design line 768: "Delivered revisions remain transcript truth, withdrawn rows remain audit only, and session inputs are processed in FIFO order."

**Problem**: Observation: §17.1 lists coalescing as an implementation-time choice, carried verbatim from v7 and v8. The companion, normative for the surfaces it defines (design line 5), fixes one input per attempt; `input.accepted` carries a single `input_id` and `reservation_id`; `AgentTurnInputV1.task.initial_input` is a single frozen reservation; P4.6 item 7 keys queued, reserved and delivered per `chat_messages` row on the journaled `input.accepted`. Consequence: §17.1 sits in a section the P4.6 and P4.7 prompts direct the agent to read and reads as license to concatenate queued rows, which breaks the per-input reservation, acceptance and `delivery_unknown` bookkeeping and produces a transcript where one `input.accepted` covers several rows.

Coalescing would require `input.accepted` to carry several input ids and is a P3.6 topic." Add the same sentence to plan P4.6 item 7.

### N91 — [P3] Which evidence moves a provider_request from `reply_pending` to `responded` is unspecified

**Location**: [design §8.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 813; [agent-api §4.4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 164, [agent-api §8.2](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 299–317, [agent-api §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 362; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 718; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 554; [plan P4.11](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 864.

**Evidence**: design line 813: "it emits the native response exactly for the active request and records delivery evidence. Do not assert native acknowledgement when the protocol only proves a successful write; preserve ambiguous delivery explicitly." Agent-api line 164: "-> delivered | already_delivered | request_gone | stale_execution". Agent-api line 362: "provider_request.replied". Plan line 718: "record responded only on the defined adapter evidence".

**Problem**: Observation: two durable signals describe one fact, the `modules.input.respond` disposition journaled by P4.2b item 2a and the C2 event `provider_request.replied`. No document defines "the defined adapter evidence", which signal flips `reply_pending` to `responded`, or the row state when the RPC times out and the event replays later. Consequence: P4.6 and P4.11 are separate PRs; one agent may transition on the RPC result and the other expect the event, giving `reply_pending` rows that never settle or `responded` rows the journal cannot corroborate after replay.

**Change**: Amend design §8.4 (line 813) and agent-api §8.2: "`responded` is set only by `TaskService::apply` consuming the journaled `provider_request.replied` event for the same `exec_id`/`provider_request_id`/`reply_version`. The C1 disposition `delivered|already_delivered` confirms the write was accepted and may drive immediate UI feedback but does not change the row; `request_gone|stale_execution` moves the row to `invalidated`. An RPC timeout leaves `reply_pending`; a later replayed `provider_request.replied` or `provider_request.invalidated` settles it, and attempt settlement invalidates any still-pending row." Replace "the defined adapter evidence" in plan P4.6 item 6 with that rule; add to plan P4.11 item 4 that the adapter journals `provider_request.replied` immediately after the stdin write succeeds.

**Validation to add**: P4.6 test with the P4.2b test module: (1) `modules.input.respond` hangs past the API timeout after the module journaled `provider_request.replied`; the row stays `reply_pending` until `events.replay` delivers the event, then becomes `responded` with `response_version` equal to `reply_version`; (2) a second respond with the same idempotency_key returns `already_delivered`, adds no second event and leaves one `responded_at`; (3) respond after attempt settlement returns `request_gone` and the row is `invalidated`. Fails today because no rule fixes the expected row state.

### N92 — [P3] Session-file harvest: an over-cap transcript is skipped and a later resume restores stale files without the §8.5 fallback firing

**Location**: [design §8.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 823; [design §17.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1124; [agent-api §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 382–393; [plan P4.11](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 861; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 716; [session_files.rs](../backend/g5-internal-api/src/session_files.rs) line 17; [runner/session_files.rs](../backend/g5d-worker/src/runner/session_files.rs) line 1324.

**Evidence**: design line 823: "the module harvests files whose sha256 changed and upserts them (zstd-compressed, 128 MiB per file, tagged with the harvesting `vm_id` and `epoch`" and "or the CLI refuses the restored session before any frame, the module starts a new provider session". Line 1124: "zstd level and caps for session files." Code line 17: `pub const CHAT_SESSION_FILE_MAX_BYTES: u64 = 128 * 1024 * 1024;` Code line 1324: "the cap is skipped on its size and never uploaded."

**Problem**: Observation: the primary `<session id>.jsonl` grows monotonically; past the cap the harvest stops persisting it while older stored files remain. The retained worker records `persist_failed` in a timeline checkpoint, but no api-core or frontend consumer reads it, nothing clears `provider_session_id`, and §8.5 restores the last stored file on a new incarnation. Consequence: the CLI resumes the older transcript without refusing it, the newest turns are lost, the fallback never triggers, and the checkpoint text promises a new-session fallback no code implements.

**Change**: Amend design §8.5 after the 128 MiB clause: "A harvest that skips any file of the session for size or upload failure emits `notice(code=session_files_persist_failed, level=warning, retryable=false)` in C2 and records `session_files_state=persist_failed` on the attempt; while that state is set, a resume on an incarnation without the local files does not restore the older stored files but starts a new provider session from delivered transcript rows and records the new `provider_session_id`, exactly as the Postgres-empty case." Add `session_files_state: "present" | "absent" | "persist_failed"` to `AgentTurnOutcomeV1` in agent-api §9, to plan P4.11 item 1 and P4.6 item 4. List "chunked or append-only storage of the primary JSONL" in §17.2 as a P3.6-era refinement.

**Validation to add**: P4.11 module test with a fake CLI: grow the primary JSONL past `CHAT_SESSION_FILE_MAX_BYTES` during a turn; assert the C2 notice and `session_files_state=persist_failed`, that `chat_session_files` keeps the previous sha256 and `session_manifest_generation`, and that the next attempt on a fresh incarnation reports `provider.session phase=started` with a new `provider_session_id`, not `phase=resumed`. Fails today because restore runs whenever local files are absent.

### N93 — [P3] Fork receipt refs have no bounded retention, unlike merge receipts

**Location**: [design §5.8 step 6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 403; [design §10.6 step 5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 936; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 341; [design §5.8 delete](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 390; [plan P4.5 item 12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 683; [plan P7.2 item 5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1115.

**Evidence**: line 403: "Retain fork receipts through durable settlement, then prune them through the API." Line 936: "Receipt refs are pruned when the PR closes or 30 days after `applied`." Line 341: "The API prunes, per workspace, every flush ref older than the two newest complete receipts".

**Problem**: Observation: flush refs and merge receipts have explicit prune triggers; `refs/g5/forks/<fork id>`, written into every member repo on every Create and Fork, has only "durable settlement" with no definition, grace or trigger. The only removal named is workspace deletion (line 390); P7.2 item 5 says to preserve them. Consequence: an implementer of P4.5 cannot write the pruner without inventing the trigger; refs accumulate per fork per repo and slow ref advertisement for every clone and fetch. Pruning too early would break rollback proof, so the rule also matters for cleanup correctness.

**Change**: Replace the sentence at line 403 with: "Durable settlement is the operation row reaching `completed` with its `workspace_repos` rows inserted, or `aborted` after every branch and receipt created by the operation was verified deleted. The API prunes `refs/g5/forks/<fork_id>` in every member repo 7 days after `completed_at`, immediately after verified cleanup for `aborted`, and on workspace deletion. Receipts of `pending` or `aborting` operations are never pruned; pruning is a delete-if-equals-OID ref transaction." Add the rule beside the flush-ref retention paragraph (line 341) and to plan P4.5 item 12; add "7 days" to §17.1 as a tunable.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 test at the internal-git boundary: after a completed fork, advance the clock 7 days and run the retention job; `refs/g5/forks/<id>` is gone from every member repo while the child branches remain; a pending operation's receipt survives the same job; an aborted operation's receipt is removed only after cleanup verification, and a retry of that operation still reports its recovery state from the row. Fails without the change because no pruner exists.

### N95 — [P3] The test-strategy checkpoint has no owner, artifact or enforceable gate

Design §13 line 1007: "P4.7, P4.8 and P5.1 narrow the restriction as each producer moves under the common stop sequence; P4.9b deletes it once no legacy producer remains." Fails at the head of P4.9a because the blocker path still exists and is reachable.

### N97 — [P3] The release path from QA to production is undefined

**Location**: [design §16.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1091; [design §13](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1009; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1197, 1204; [plan P8.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1168–1183; [values-staging.yaml](../infra/helm/g5/values-staging.yaml); [infra/qa/README.md](../infra/qa/README.md) line 74.

**Evidence**: design line 1091: "Production and staging follow the same roots with their own inputs." Design line 1009: "the QA project rehearses control-plane-first deploys, a host drain and a workspace rollout before the first production deploy." Plan line 1204: "Acceptance: the QA rehearsal in the runbook passes end to end once."

**Problem**: Observation: staging exists in Terraform and Helm, but every rehearsal and acceptance names QA, P8.7's acceptance is one successful rehearsal, and the QA README says the deploy trigger has no downstream promotion. No document defines promotion (main to QA to staging to production), the promoted artifacts (image digests, migration set), per-release checks, production host rotation order or abort criteria. Consequence: "passes once" is not falsifiable per release; the P8.7 agent stops at the first green run and writes a rollback section but no abort criterion for a rollout that by design interrupts running work (§6.10) and can record `unflushed_loss` and `lease_expired_killed`; drain-path regressions reach customers as loss events.

**Change**: Add a "Release path" paragraph to design §16.1: promotion by image digest main to QA to staging to production; the per-release checklist (P8.7 rehearsal on the candidate digest, migration upgrade rehearsal on a prod-shaped copy per §12, backup/restore rehearsal once the SaaS backup exists, the three MVP SLOs within bounds); production host rotation one host first with a soak window, then the fleet; abort criteria on named metrics (any `unflushed_loss` or `lease_expired_killed` during the rollout, or a flush-failure rate above a threshold, halts rotation and starts the rollback section). Amend P8.7 step 4 (line 1197) so `docs/rollout.md` holds these gates; change its Acceptance (line 1204) to "the rehearsal passes on the candidate and is a required lane rerun for every release candidate". Amend P8.6 to add the rehearsal lane that blocks promotion and records downtime, loss events and SLOs as build artifacts.

**Validation to add**: A Cloud Build lane (P8.6/P8.7) runs the P8.7 rehearsal against the candidate digest on QA with an old host connected, a host drain and a workspace rollout, fails on any recorded `unflushed_loss` or `lease_expired_killed`, and publishes downtime, loss and SLO artifacts; a negative run with a fault-injected flush failure asserts the lane fails and the runbook abort step is exercised; a checklist dry run on staging precedes the first production deploy.

### N98 — [P3] §15 bullets without an owning PR test: module input argv and host paths, native `git add -A` excludes, zero-VM-call observation

**Location**: [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 1066, 1070, 1080; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 544; [plan P4.2b step 5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 557; [plan P4.2a steps 4–5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 534–535; [plan P4.3a step 6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 583; [plan P2.1 step 6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 397; [plan P4.3b step 4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 607; [plan P4.5 step 4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 673; [plan §7.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1355.

Line 1080: "Assert zero VM calls for all reader file/tree/docs/ontology/Changes/watch routes and source forks". Plan line 397: "neither browse nor reader fork boots/flushes a source". Plan line 607: "without any source RPC, flush or interruption".

**Problem**: Observation: (a) the §7.1 row at line 1355 assigns "selected without client argv injection" to P4.2b, but P4.2b's test list has no rejection test for argv, env or host-path input. (b) Provider state lives outside every working tree (line 544), so the `git add -A` half of line 1066 concerns the `.g5/` runtime subdirectories guarded by the excludes file P4.2a writes (line 534); P4.3a tests the flush half (line 583) and no prompt tests the `git add -A` half. (c) "Assert zero VM calls" presupposes a counter; P2.1 and P4.3b describe an absence without naming how it is observed, and P4.2a/P4.5 define a test client and a facade but no call log. Consequence: agents following the enumerated tests can land a runtime that forwards argv, an excludes regression that stages runtime files into a user commit, or a reader route that calls `fs.list`, with every listed test green.

**Change**: Plan P4.2b step 5: add "`modules.invoke` with input containing argv, env, cwd, a host path (absolute or `../` escaping `/work`) or signal fields is rejected with a typed validation error before resolution and spawns nothing, for a built-in module and the test module." Plan P4.2a step 5: add "after writing `/var/lib/g5d/excludes` and the global gitconfig, a native `git add -A` in a member checkout containing `.g5/cache/x` and another listed runtime subdirectory stages neither, while a tracked non-excluded file under `.g5/` is still staged." Plan P4.5 step 4: add "the facade records every rpc, pty and http stream open and every JSON-RPC method sent, per `workspace_id`, in a test-visible call log; P2.1 step 6, P4.3b step 4 and P4.10 assert on this log." Design §15 Sharing bullet: append "observed through the facade call log".

**Validation to add**: P4.2b: `modules.invoke` with `{input:{argv:["sh","-c","id"]}}` and `{input:{path:"/etc/passwd"}}` returns a validation error and the runner spawn counter is zero. P4.2a: `g5d dispatch` against a temp repo, touch `.g5/cache/a`, `git add -A` with the installed gitconfig, `git diff --cached --name-only` is empty; fails without `core.excludesFile`. P2.1/P4.3b: every reader route and a reader fork against a running source leave the facade call log for that `workspace_id` empty.

### N99 — [P3] Lease TTL, start deadline and grace defaults are undefined, and slot reservation atomicity is unstated

**Location**: [worker-host-api §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 146, 151; [worker-host-api §7.1](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 278–293; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 521; [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 553–554; [design §17.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 1119–1129; [plan P4.4b items 2–3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 647–648; [plan P4.5 item 1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 670.

**Evidence**: host note line 151: "`size` equals the slot size, a slot is free, no other VM of this `workspace_id` is on the host, and fewer than `max_concurrent_boots` boots are in progress, else `slot_unavailable(reason)`." Line 146: "Rules the host enforces before boot, in this order; the first failure is the answer". Plan line 670: "a lease of `lease_ttl_secs + start_deadline_secs`".

**Problem**: Observation: every §7.1 limit has a default; `lease_ttl_secs`, `start_deadline_secs` and `grace_secs` appear only as fields and formula terms across the v9 documents, and §17.1 lists `quiesce_secs` and backoffs but not these. `max_inflight_commands` is 16, so `vm.start` commands are concurrent, yet nothing says the step-4 check reserves the slot and boot token under one lock or that a refusal at steps 5–8 or `boot_failed` releases them. Consequence: P4.5 (the grant) and P4.4b (the timer) must invent the two user-visible windows the TTL sets, how long a tunnel blip a running VM survives and how long after host loss the owner can resume elsewhere; two concurrent `vm.start`s may both pass step 4 and oversubscribe a slot.

**Change**: Add to design §17.1 and host note §7.1 the one timing table of this review (N4, N27, P1-4 and the Worker & Host gap G2 cite it and state no default of their own): `lease_ttl_secs` 300 (renewal at half, 150 s; rationale: N4 requires `lease_ttl_secs >= 2 x control_plane_deploy_budget_secs` and the Recreate deploy budget P8.7 measures is set at 120 s, so 300 s keeps every VM alive through one deploy with margin; the cost is that a host lost without a report holds its slot and blocks resume elsewhere for up to 300 s), `start_deadline_secs` 60, `grace_secs` 30 (after the 15 s quiesce of §5.8), `heartbeat_secs` 10, with `lease_ttl_secs >= 6 * heartbeat_secs` and "a VM survives a tunnel outage shorter than `lease_ttl_secs / 2`; the owner can resume on another host at most `lease_ttl_secs` after a host is lost". In host note §4 after the rule list: "Step 4 (slot, `workspace_busy`, boot capacity) runs under one host admission lock and reserves the slot and the boot token; a refusal at any later step, and `boot_failed`, release them before the answer is sent." Amend P4.4b item 2 to name the admission lock and P4.5 item 1 to use the configured defaults.

**Validation to add**: P4.4b host tests: three concurrent `vm.start` on a two-slot host yield two `started` and one `slot_unavailable(slots)`; three concurrent starts with `max_concurrent_boots=2` yield one `slot_unavailable(boot_capacity)`; a spec refused at step 6 (`credential_in_env`) leaves the slot free; `boot_failed(grant_unresolved)` frees it. P4.5: the CAS lease equals `now + lease_ttl_secs + start_deadline_secs` from config, and one override changes both the grant and `VmSpec.incarnation.lease_ttl_secs`.

### N100 — [P3] The idempotency key scope for `workspace_forks` is undefined and has no uniqueness constraint

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 200–202, 208, 225; [design §5.8 step 1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 398; [plan P4.1 item 10](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 507; [plan P4.5 items 11–13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 682–684; [workspace-sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 65.

**Evidence**: design line 208: "requested_by, idempotency_key, proposed_name,". Line 225: "UNIQUE (request_id, task_id, attempt, idempotency_key)". Line 398: "A retry with that key and the same inputs resumes its durable phase; different inputs under the same key are refused". Plan line 507: "A retry with the same key and payload resolves to the same operation; key reuse with different name/member/source input is rejected."

**Problem**: Observation: `workspace_result_submissions` declares a scoped UNIQUE, but `workspace_forks.idempotency_key` has no constraint, no scope (requester, source, org) and no stored input digest for the mismatch check; `workspace_ownership_transfers` (lines 200–202) has no `idempotency_key` column although plan line 500 and the sharing note require one. Consequence: a global UNIQUE makes one user's client key reject another user's unrelated fork with a spurious 409; no constraint lets two concurrent identical requests insert two pending operations, after which the `(repo_id, branch_name)` reservation fails the second as `WORKSPACE_NAME_CONFLICT` instead of resuming the first, contradicting "the same key returns the same completed workspace" and leaving P4.5 item 13's tests unfalsifiable.

**Change**: In design §5.1 add to `workspace_forks` a `request_digest` (sha256 over source_workspace_id, proposed_name, ordered member repo set, chosen source receipt or OID vector, chat-carry options) and `UNIQUE (org_id, requested_by, idempotency_key)`; add `idempotency_key` and `UNIQUE (workspace_id, requested_by, idempotency_key)` to `workspace_ownership_transfers`; add `UNIQUE (org_id, requested_by, idempotency_key)` to `workspace_requests`. In §5.8 step 1 replace the retry sentence with: "The key is scoped to (org, requester). A retry whose `request_digest` matches resumes the durable operation (insert with ON CONFLICT on the unique key, then compare digests); a different digest under the same key is refused with 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`. Another requester's identical key is an independent operation." Mirror it in plan P4.1 item 10 and P4.5 items 11–12.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 API test at the fork route: (a) two concurrent POSTs with the same requester, key and payload yield one `workspace_forks` row, one child workspace and identical bodies; (b) same key and requester with a changed `proposed_name` returns 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED` and creates no row or ref; (c) the same key from another org member creates an independent fork. (a) and (c) fail without the scoped constraint and digest column.

### N101 — [P3] `vm.stop graceful` as SIGTERM to the guest init names no delivery mechanism under libkrun; the retained code signals the VMM

**Location**: [worker-host-api §3.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 98; [worker-host-api §13](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 372; [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 503; [plan P4.4b item 2](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 647; [microvm.rs](../backend/g5d-isolation/src/isolation/microvm.rs) line 1003.

**Evidence**: host note line 98: "`graceful` delivers SIGTERM to the guest init process, which is the dispatcher, and SIGKILLs at `grace_secs`". Line 372: "`vm.stop graceful` is an OS signal to the guest init, not an rpc method". microvm.rs line 1003: `super::signal_process_group(pid, libc::SIGTERM)?;`

**Problem**: Observation: the only retained stop path signals the host-side process group of the `g5d vm-exec` child, which is the libkrun VMM after `krun_start_enter`; it terminates the guest without delivering anything to guest PID 1. No vsock, serial, ACPI or guest-agent path is named in the design, either companion or any prompt, and whether libkrun forwards SIGTERM to guest init is not verifiable from the repository. Consequence: the contract names a delivery the backend does not perform and no PR is told how to build; a P4.4b or P4.2a implementer searches for a guest-signal mechanism that does not exist, or writes a test asserting guest-init delivery that only measures VMM exit. Nothing downstream depends on the dispatcher observing SIGTERM, since the stop sequence completes on `rpc` first.

**Change**: Amend host note §3.2 `vm.stop` (line 98), design §6.2 `vm.stop` row (line 503) and plan P4.4b item 2 (line 647) to: "`graceful` sends SIGTERM to the VMM process group (the `g5d vm-exec` libkrun process) and SIGKILL at `grace_secs`; the guest receives no signal, and the dispatcher needs no SIGTERM handler because the stop sequence has already completed on `rpc` (admission barrier, `workspace.flush shutdown`, harvest, journal drain and ack) before `vm.stop` is sent." Keep the §13 settled note but replace "signal to the guest init" with "signal to the VMM". If guest-init delivery is ever wanted, list it in §17.1 as an implementation-time choice requiring a named mechanism.

**Validation to add**: P4.4b test: after the stop sequence, `vm.stop graceful` returns `stopped(exit)` with the VMM exit receipt within `grace_secs`, and the slot is freed only after the VMM process is reaped; a fixture VMM that ignores SIGTERM is SIGKILLed at `grace_secs`. No test asserts a guest-side SIGTERM observation.

**Location**: [plan §7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1338; [plan §7.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1347–1364; [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 1067–1068; [worker-host-api §6](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 175.

Take wording from the §15 "Host protocol and lease" and "Proxies" bullets.

**Validation to add**: Each observation maps to a named test already in the owning prompt (P4.4a step 4, P4.4b step 6, P4.5 step 7, P4.12 step 7); record in the tracker Notes of those PRs the exact-head CI run (g5-ci-rust job and the docker-ci isolation lane) that executed them, so the row is falsifiable per candidate.

**Location**: [plan §5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 15, 217, 220, 222, 224, 233; [plan §5.3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 312; [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 497, 500, 507; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 5339.

### N104 — [P3] The stated critical chain is wrong, and schedule row 1 starts P4.4a in the same row as its dependency P4.1

**Location**: [plan §5.3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 308, 312, 320; [plan §5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 209–256, in particular 222, 234.

**Evidence**: plan line 320: "The critical chain runs down the first column and then over to the third: P4.2a → P4.3a → P4.5 → P4.6 → P4.7 → P4.9a → P4.9b, with P5.1 also gating P4.9a." Line 308: "Each row starts when the dependencies of its PRs are merged" and "Rank by the longest outstanding dependency path to an MVP exit node". Line 312: "| P4.2a | P4.4a | P4.1, then P4.13 |". Line 222: P4.4a depends on "P4.1". Line 234: P4.14 depends on "P4.3b, P4.7, P4.8, P4.10".

**Problem**: Observation: the longest unmerged path from §5.1 is P4.14 at depth 9 (P4.1 → P4.4a → P4.4b → P4.5 → P4.6 → P2.1 → P2.2 → P4.10 → P4.14, size weight 27 with S=1 to XL=4); P4.9b and P2.4 are depth 8 (weight 25); the chain named critical is 7 PRs and its prefix into P4.5 is shorter than P4.1 → P4.4a → P4.4b. Row 1 assigns P4.4a to the second column while P4.1, its only dependency, is in the same row; P8.9, whose dependency is merged, sits fourth in that column. Consequence: one of three people has nothing startable on day 1 while an unblocked MVP gate waits; people following the named chain prioritize P4.7 and P4.9a over P2.1, P2.2 and P4.10, so P4.14, the meeting's headline value loop, lands later than necessary; the correction is deferred to a checkpoint with no date (N95).

**Change**: Replace the sentence at line 320 with: "The longest outstanding path is P4.1 → P4.4a → P4.4b → P4.5 → P4.6 → P2.1 → P2.2 → P4.10 → P4.14 (9 PRs); the runtime-retirement chain through P4.6 → P4.7/P4.8/P5.1 → P4.9a → P4.9b and the editor chain to P2.4 are one PR shorter." Rework the table so row 1 holds only PRs whose dependencies are merged today (P4.2a, P4.1, P8.9), move P4.4a to the second column's row 2 and shift that column down one row, and add: "Recompute the ranking from the tracker whenever a PR merges; a row may not contain a PR whose dependency is in the same or a later row." Optionally commit `scripts/plan-critical-path.py` that parses §5.1 and prints the ranking.

**Validation to add**: `scripts/plan-critical-path.py` (proposed in the Change; no `just lint-docs` recipe exists in the Justfile) parses §5.1 and §5.3 and exits non-zero when a schedule row contains a PR whose dependency appears in the same or a later row, or when the chain named in the critical-path sentence is not the maximal-depth path; run it on the current file to reproduce both defects before the edit, and wire it into `scripts/run-ci-script-tests.py`.

### N105 — [P3] P8.9 (search removal) is unblocked today and an MVP gate, yet is scheduled fourth in a column while that executor idles at program start

**Location**: [plan §5.3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 312, 315; [plan §5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 243; [plan §3.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 154; [plan P8.9](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1228, 1233; [plan P4.10 item 5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 839.

**Evidence**: plan line 315: "| P4.3b | P8.9 | P4.6 |". Line 1228: "Depends on P1.3 (merged)." Line 154: "P8.9 is an MVP exit gate and an input to cleanup/audits; no surviving screen or seed tool may resurrect it." Line 839: "coordinate removed workspace search with P8.9 without reintroducing its header slot or results." Line 243: P8.1 is "idle-time work".

**Problem**: Observation: P8.9's only dependency is merged; it edits WorkspacePage.tsx and TopBar.tsx (line 1233), `workspace_settings`, seed scripts, Cloud Build contracts and adds a drop migration. The second column's row 1 is P4.4a, which depends on P4.1 in the same row, so that executor has nothing startable at program start; the only named idle-time work is the non-MVP P8.1. Under the schedule as written P8.9 (row 4) lands before P2.2 (row 5, same column) and P4.10 (row 6), so a rebase-cost argument does not hold. Consequence: throughput only: an unblocked MVP gate waits while one of three executors idles, and P4.10's "coordinate with P8.9" is ambiguous whenever P8.9 has not landed. Starting P8.9 (size L) naively could delay P4.4a → P4.4b → P4.5 once P4.1 merges.

**Change**: Amend §5.3: replace "P8.1 is idle-time work for whoever is waiting on a dependency" with "P8.9 is the idle-time PR for whoever is blocked at program start (the second column until P4.1 merges); a critical-chain PR whose dependency merges preempts it, and the P8.9 branch is handed to the next idle executor rather than held. P8.1 is idle-time work only after P8.9 is merged." Keep the §5.3 ranking rule for other choices. Amend P4.10 item 5 to "P8.9 has removed workspace search; do not reintroduce its header slot, results or state keys". Do not add P8.9 to the Depends on of P2.2 or P4.10; the schedule orders them.

**Validation to add**: Process/manual: tracker check at the first grouping checkpoint: the P8.9 Branch cell is filled before P2.2's row starts; `grep -n coordinate docs/lean-implementation-plan-v9.md` shows no hit in the P4.10 prompt. No code test applies.

### N106 — [P3] Prompt Validation lines contradict the §2 docker-ci rule for dispatcher and host PRs

**Location**: [plan §2 item 8](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 28; [plan P4.2a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 540; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 566; [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 592; [plan P4.4a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 634; siblings [P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 658, [P4.9a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 801, [P4.12](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 898.

**Evidence**: plan line 28: "Validation ladder (run what applies; the prompt says which are required)" and "`just docker-ci` for PRs that touch worker, host, isolation, dispatcher or CI configuration". Lines 540 and 566: "Validation: cargo check, lint, fmt, `cargo test -p g5d-dispatch`." Line 634: "Validation: cargo check, lint, fmt, `cargo test -p g5d-host -p g5-internal-api`, `just test-integration`."

**Problem**: Observation: §2 requires docker-ci for dispatcher and host PRs, but the precedence clause makes the prompt authoritative, and the P4.2a, P4.2b, P4.3a and P4.4a Validation lines omit it while P4.4b, P4.9a and P4.12 include it, so the omission is not a deliberate rule. The ladder's clippy item already covers test targets, so Phase 0b is not the gap. Consequence: an agent follows the Validation line, so the P4.4a host-protocol rewrite, which removes the host JWT path, reaches Cloud Build without the only local end-to-end host bring-up (docker-ci Phases 3–4b); the e2e Cloud Build lane fails structurally on every PR today, so nothing exercises the host tunnel before merge. The cost is a CI round trip or an unexercised tunnel, not a wrong implementation.

**Change**: Append `just docker-ci` to the Validation lines of P4.2a (line 540), P4.2b (line 566), P4.3a (line 592) and P4.4a (line 634); for P4.4a name the phase: "`just docker-ci 4b`: the host registers over the new `ctl` protocol and workers start", so the removed JWT path is exercised end to end. In §2 item 8 replace "run what applies; the prompt says which are required" with "the prompt lists the required items; the touch rules below add to that list and are never dropped by a prompt", so the two rules cannot disagree again.

**Validation to add**: Process/manual for the wording change; the runnable check is `just docker-ci 4b` on P4.4a, which must show a host registering through the `ctl` stream with the renewable credential and no `G5_HOST_JWT`-style fixed token in the compose environment.

### N107 — [P3] P4.1 acceptance names a docker-ci "bootstrap phase" that does not exist

**Location**: [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 517–518; [Justfile](../Justfile) lines 12, 331, 953, 1294–1299, 1456.

**Evidence**: plan line 517: "Acceptance: fresh install and upgrade from the current schema both pass; the no-op re-run passes; `just test-integration` passes; `just docker-ci` bootstrap phase passes." Justfile line 1456: `ALL_PHASES="0 0b 1 1b 2 3 4 4a 4b 4c 5 5b 5c 6 6b 7"`.

**Problem**: Observation: `just docker-ci` is an alias of `ci-docker` (Justfile line 12); its phases are 0, 0b, 1, 1b, 2, 3, 4, 4a, 4b, 4c, 5, 5b, 5c, 6, 6b, 7 and none is called "bootstrap"; the only "bootstrap" recipes are the one-time libkrun and NFS host setup (lines 331, 953). Migrations apply when the API starts in Phase 4. The phrase is inherited unchanged from plan v1 line 1132, v7 line 577 and v8 line 497, which predate the current phase list. Consequence: the P4.1 agent must guess which phase satisfies the claim; the likely guesses (full `just docker-ci` or `just docker-ci 4a`) both work, but "bootstrap phase passes" is not a claim a reviewer can reproduce against a command.

**Change**: Plan P4.1 acceptance (line 517): replace "`just docker-ci` bootstrap phase passes" with "`just docker-ci 4c` passes (builds the service images, starts the stack so the API applies the new migrations against a fresh Postgres, seeds the CI org and the worker and browser fixture workspaces against the new schema), in addition to the migration checks of §2 item 6", and add the same command to the P4.1 Validation line (line 518). If a migration-only phase is wanted, commission it under P8.6 or P8.7 as a Justfile change rather than naming it here.

**Validation to add**: `just docker-ci 4c` on the P4.1 head either exits 0 with the Phase 4, 4a, 4b and 4c banners or fails; no other test.

### N108 — [P3] The worker-host note carries stale plan references: "P4.4" unsplit, `(§6.5)` pointing at the wrong section, placement "settled by plan P4.5" (now P4.13), and a "Needed change" partly made

**Location**: [worker-host-api](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 5, 132, 235, 250, 370, 378; [agent-api §6.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 238; [design §6.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 528, 565; [plan](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 100, 670, 692, 909–910.

**Evidence**: host note line 5: "including P4.4, P4.5 and P4.12". Line 132: "secrets_env: [{ name, secret_ref }], -- repository and task secrets, resolved by the host into the dispatcher's environment (§6.5)". Line 378: "Settled by plan P4.5: placement is a function of `WorkspaceVmService` (the `resume` step chooses the host". Plan line 692: "placement (P4.13); the proxies (P4.12)." Plan line 910: "Placement function `place(workspace, org)`". Agent-api line 238: "`agent.turn` configures the host proxy with the accounting session id and placeholder credentials, never real keys."

**Problem**: Observation: the plan split P4.4 into P4.4a and P4.4b; inside the host note §6.5 is "How each client is steered to the proxy" (line 235) and secrets are §6.6 (line 250), so the `(§6.5)` comment copied from design line 528 points at the wrong topic; line 378 contradicts plan lines 100, 670, 692 and 909–910, which put `place()` in `WorkerHostService` under P4.13; the §13 seam row (line 370) asks the agents API to state the per-session base URL, but agent-api line 238 covers only placeholder credentials while the base-URL sentence lives in design line 565. Consequence: P4.13, P4.4a and P4.4b agents are told to read the host note and find a "Settled" statement contradicting their prompt, which costs a blocker report or verification time; the wrong pointer sends a P4.4b agent to the steering table instead of the secrets rules. The prompts themselves are consistent, so duplicated placement logic is unlikely.

In the P4.13 placement test already at plan line 913 and P4.5's resume test, assert that the chosen `host_id` equals the `place()` stub's return, proving `resume` does not pick hosts on its own.

### N109 — [P1] Self-hosted and macOS registration has no exchange step, single-use rule or lifetime, and its only retained table (`worker_tokens`) is dropped by P4.9b while P4.13 and P9.1 still register hosts with it

**Location**: [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 512; [design §16.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1101; [worker-host §2.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 58; [worker-host §3.1](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 78; [worker-host §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 105–117; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 909; [plan P4.9b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 815; [plan P9.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1260; [worker_token_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/worker_token_service.rs) lines 121, 341–382; [worker_tokens.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/worker_tokens.rs) line 50; [worker_hosts.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/worker_hosts.rs) line 521; [task_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/task_service.rs) line 8743.
