**Evidence**: design line 512: "mint a one-time `g5w_*` registration token for a self-hosted or macOS host". Worker-host line 58: "Returns `host_id` and, for self-hosted, a one-time `g5w_*` token. Exists today." Line 78: "authenticated with the **host credential**: a renewable token with claims `host_id`, `org_id`, `runtime_identity_hash`, TTL 24 h". Plan line 909: "self-hosted and macOS by registration token". Plan line 815: "a migration dropping `workers`, `worker_tokens`, `worker_session_generation`". worker_token_service.rs line 121: `pub expires_in_secs: Option<i64>,`. worker_hosts.rs line 521: `let host_jwt = minter.mint_host_jwt(host.host_id, host.org_id, state.host_jwt_ttl)?;`

**Problem**: Observation: the tunnel opens only with a host credential; §3.3 lists `host.credential.renew` but no first-issue message or route; no document says how a `g5w_*` token becomes a host credential, whether it is consumed, how long it lives, or how the credential's `host_id`/`org_id` are fixed. "Exists today" is wrong: `POST /orgs/:org/worker-hosts` is GCE-only and mints a `host_jwt`; `g5w_*` tokens are `worker_tokens` rows, reusable (`validate_registration_token` checks only `revoked_at` and an optional `expires_at`, so a token may never expire) and redeemed by `/api/internal/workers/register` into a worker-session JWT and a `workers` row. P4.9b drops that table and route while P4.13, P9.1 and §16.3 ("the registration token flow stays") keep the token as the join mechanism. Consequence: the P4.13 implementer must invent table, redemption route, single-use rule, lifetime and claim binding, and the plausible reuse is deleted by P4.9b. Nothing forbids redeeming one token twice (two machines behind one `host_id`), after months, or for a host in another org; each such host receives full `VmSpec`s with `secrets_env` and the org's `credential_grants`.

**Change**: Design §6.2 "Operator API" and worker-host §2.3/§3.1: "For `kind in (self_hosted, macos_dev)` the route inserts the `worker_hosts` row in `provisioning` and returns a `g5w_*` token bound to that `host_id` and `org_id`, stored hashed in `worker_host_registration_tokens(host_id PK, org_id, token_hash, expires_at NOT NULL DEFAULT now() + 24h, redeemed_at NULL)`. The host redeems it once with `POST /api/internal/hosts/:host_id/register {registration_token, runtime_identity, protocol_version}`; the control plane atomically sets `redeemed_at` (`UPDATE … WHERE redeemed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now()`), records `runtime_identity`, mints the first host credential with `host_id`, `org_id` from the row and `runtime_identity_hash` (N123), and returns it; a second, expired, unknown or cross-org redemption is 401 and audited. The GCE path mints the same credential at bring-up (§16.1). Only a host credential opens the tunnel." Plan P4.1 or P4.13 step 1: migration and route; P4.13 step 4: the route contract; P4.9b step 1: "`worker_tokens`/`WorkerTokenScope` are the per-task worker tokens; `worker_host_registration_tokens` and `/api/internal/hosts/:host_id/register` stay"; P9.1 step 2: reference the P4.13 token.

**Validation to add**: P4.13 api_worker_hosts.rs: (1) redeem one token twice: second call 401, one `worker_hosts` row, one credential; (2) redeem after `expires_at`: 401; (3) a token minted in org A at a `host_id` of org B: 401, no credential; (4) the credential's `host_id`/`org_id` equal the row; (5) a `g5w_*` token on `GET /api/internal/hosts/:host_id/tunnel` is refused. P4.9b: after its migration (1) still passes. All fail today: no such table, route or single-use check exists.

### N110 — [P2] Repo-selected `base_image` has no org allowlist or digest rule: whoever commits to the branch chooses the guest root filesystem

**Location**: [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 542; [design §2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 67; [design §14](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1015; [worker-host §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 152; [worker-host §13](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 364; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 670; [assignment.rs](../backend/g5d-host/src/assignment.rs) lines 857, 931; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 4420.

**Evidence**: design line 542: "The VM image is the org's default image or the one named by `base_image:` in the code repo's `.g5/workspace.yaml`, resolved at each attach and recorded on `workspace_vms`." Worker-host line 152: "`image.digest` matches the pulled or cached image, else `spec_rejected(image_digest_mismatch)`." Plan line 670: "the image resolved from the org default or `.g5/workspace.yaml`". assignment.rs line 931: `g5d_foundation::oci::parse_image_ref(&selected).map_err(|error| {`. Baseline line 4420: `CONSTRAINT orgs_default_base_image_ref_digest_pinned CHECK (…@sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}…)`.

**Problem**: Observation: the only host-side rule checks that the pulled bytes match the digest the control plane sent, not that the image is one the platform or org approved; worker-host §13 line 364 rejects the options that would bound accepted images; grep of all nine documents for allowlist finds only the env-key allowlist (design 540, worker-host 153) and no §14 row mentions images. On main the retained resolver accepts any syntactically valid OCI reference, tag or digest, while `orgs.default_base_image_ref` is CHECK-constrained to a digest, so the repository path is strictly weaker than the org path and v9 preserves that. Consequence: an implementer of P4.5 step 1 has no rule to refuse an image and, following the retained code, accepts any reference; P4.4b validates only digest equality. A commit to `.g5/workspace.yaml` (an agent, a terminal user, a PR author whose head a review child pins per line 444) makes the next attach boot an arbitrary registry image as the trust root of the guest that holds `G5D_WORKSPACE_TOKEN` and `secrets_env`, and the host pulls unbounded bytes from arbitrary registries. Bounded to the owner's own org (hosts serve one org, worker-host line 149), hence P2.

**Change**: Design §6.3 "Image", first sentence: "The VM image is the org's default image or the image named by `base_image:` in `.g5/workspace.yaml` (resolution rules: N117). In both cases the reference must be digest-pinned (`<registry>/<path>@sha256:<64 hex>`, `g5-contracts::validate_base_image_ref`) and its digest must appear in the org's image allowlist `configs(scope=org, key=allowed_images)`, which always contains the platform default image and is edited only by org admins through the operator API. The control plane checks this before building the `VmSpec`; a tag-only or malformed reference fails the attach with `IMAGE_INVALID`, an unlisted digest with `IMAGE_NOT_ALLOWED`; both record `last_error`, leave `workspace_vms.status` unchanged and send no `vm.start`; there is no silent fallback to the org default." Worker-host §3.1 `host.accept.config`: add `allowed_image_digests: [sha256]`; §4 order: after the slot check, `image.digest` in `allowed_image_digests` else `spec_rejected(image_not_allowed)`, before any pull. Design §14: add "A repository selects an unapproved guest image | digest-pinned org allowlist checked by the API before `vm.start` and by the host before pull". Plan: P4.1 adds the `allowed_images` key; P4.5 step 1 the check and the two codes; P4.4b step 1 the host check and config field; P4.13 operator routes `GET/PUT /orgs/:id/allowed-images` (admin only).

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: with a head whose `.g5/workspace.yaml` names (i) a tag-only reference, (ii) a digest not in `allowed_images`, `POST /workspaces/:id/resume` returns `IMAGE_INVALID` / `IMAGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, the row is not `starting`, `last_error` is set and the `ScriptedHostClient` receives no `vm.start`; after an admin adds the digest the same resume sends `vm.start` with that `image.digest`. P4.4b g5d-host test: a `VmSpec` whose `image.digest` is absent from `allowed_image_digests` is answered `spec_rejected(image_not_allowed)` and the fake registry sees zero requests. Both fail today because any parseable reference is accepted.

### N111 — [P2] Inbound mirroring is post-MVP (P1.5, after P4.5) while every consumer of the mirrored protected ref is MVP; the MVP-window path by which provider truth reaches base workspaces, base forks and review pins is undefined

**Location**: [plan §4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 200; [plan §5.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 210; [plan P1.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 349; [plan §7.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1352, 1358; [design §3.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 82; [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 271; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 399; [design §13](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1005; [branch_integration_worker.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/branch_integration_worker.rs) line 1547.

**Evidence**: plan line 210: "| P1.5 | Remove remote tracking; add protected-branch mirroring and Publish | Git | XL | P1.4, P4.5 | not started |". Plan line 200: "**After the MVP, in priority order.** Governance and publish (P1.5, P1.6, P6.1 to P6.4)". Design line 82: "Remote tracking still exists (P1.5)." Design line 271: "Base workspace: is a read-only view of each member repository's existing protected/default ref." Plan line 349: "Inbound: backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/protected_branch_mirror.rs". branch_integration_worker.rs line 1547: `repo.fetch_single_ref("origin", &remote_branch, &auth_clone)`.

**Problem**: Observation: `protected_branch_mirror.rs` is created by P1.5 (the file does not exist on main), and P1.5 is post-MVP and depends on P4.5. Its consumers are MVP: base-workspace reads (§9.1 line 854, P2.1 item 3), fork-from-base pins (§5.8 step 3, P4.5 items 11 and 13), review pins (P4.14 item 1) and "Update from origin" (§5.6 line 361). The retained inbound code fetches into `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>`, not `refs/heads/*`; I found no retained writer of the bare mirror's protected heads after onboarding, and no MVP prompt names one. Hypothesis (the legacy path is large and not fully traced): during the MVP the internal protected refs are frozen at onboarding or follow legacy remote-tracking semantics the design has declared removed, while the fork preflight (line 399) checks names against the live provider namespace and then pins the internal OID. Consequence: either MVP base workspaces and review children are built on weeks-old source with no owner for the inbound path, or the P4.5 implementer bolts an ad hoc provider fetch into the fork service (a second inbound writer outside the future merge lock, with N116's forced-refspec hazard). The acceptance rows "Reads" and "Fork" (plan lines 1352, 1358) have no producer of a current base. The post-MVP placement of P1.5 is an explicit §13 decision; the undefined MVP-window contract is the finding.

**Change**: Split P1.5 at a real seam per plan §2: P1.5a "Protected-branch mirror" (new `protected_branch_mirror.rs` with N116's update policy and N113's `repo_mirror_refs`, poll only, connecting user's stored token per N35; depends on P4.1 only; size M; MVP wave, scheduled before P4.5 in plan §4) and P1.5b "Remove remote tracking; Publish; webhook (N118)" (post-MVP; depends on P1.5a, P4.5). Add to design §3.2 line 82 and plan §4: "Until P1.5b lands the legacy tracking sync writes only `refs/remotes/*` and `workspace_branches` tracking rows; P1.5a's mirror is the sole writer of `refs/heads/<protected>` from the provider, and P4.5 forks from base pin the mirror OID." Update the P1.5 row, the tracker and P4.5 item 11 ("fork from base pins `repo_mirror_refs.oid` and records `mirror_observed_at`"). If the team keeps P1.5 whole and post-MVP, write into §17.2 "MVP base refs are frozen at onboarding and labelled `mirror_state=unsynced`" and make P2.1 item 3 and P4.14 item 6 render that label.

**Validation to add**: P1.5a: N116's and N113's mirror tests run in the MVP lane (g5-ci-rust) against a `file://` provider. P4.5 fork boundary test (item 13): create a base workspace from a `file://` provider repo, advance the provider by one commit, run one mirror tick, fork from base and assert the child branch OID equals the provider head and the manifest records `mirror_observed_at`; this fails on a build with no MVP inbound path. Static check: `grep -rn 'fetch(' backend/g5-api-core/src/services/workspace_fork*` is empty, proving the fork service never fetches from the provider itself.

### N112 — [P2] Runtime directory provenance contradicts itself: `/opt/g5/bin` and the catalog are "part of the runtime image" yet a read-only host mount, and the host "adds nothing from its own state"

**Location**: [design §4.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 125; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 542, 544; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 647; [worker-host §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 120, 157; [worker-host §13](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 364; [agent-api §7](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 278; [plan P4.2b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 552; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 647; [supervisor.rs](../backend/g5d-host/src/supervisor.rs) line 512; [assignment.rs](../backend/g5d-host/src/assignment.rs) lines 345–348.

**Evidence**: design line 542: "The runtime directory `/opt/g5/bin` (g5d, the pinned provider CLIs, the fake binaries in CI images) and the module catalog are part of the runtime image and are hash-pinned". Line 544: "`/opt/g5/bin` read-only; provider assets read-only". Worker-host line 120: "The host adds nothing from its own state except the base image files and the cache paths." Line 157: "`/opt/g5/bin` is the runtime directory, read only". Plan line 552: "Catalog: `/opt/g5/modules/catalog.json` in the runtime image, hash-pinned". Agent-api line 278: "Workspaces supplies a pinned module/runtime image, the actual CLI binaries". supervisor.rs line 512: `/// Host directory virtio-fs-mounted into every microVM at `/opt/g5/bin`.`

**Problem**: Observation: "runtime image" denotes the host's own image at design 125 and 647 and worker-host 364 (`runtime_identity` reports the g5d version), so line 542 can be read as "host runtime image, mounted read-only", and on main the runtime directory is a host directory mounted over virtio-fs. Three defects survive that reading: (1) worker-host line 120 excludes a host-supplied runtime directory; (2) the catalog path `/opt/g5/modules/catalog.json` lies outside the only runtime mount the design names, `/opt/g5/bin` (design 544, worker-host 157, P4.4b step 2 "runtime bin"), so its provenance is unspecified: the VM image supplies it or an unnamed mount does; (3) agent-api line 278 assigns the runtime image to Workspaces while design 125 assigns it to Worker & Host. No text says the host mount shadows any `/opt/g5` content of the VM image, and no glossary distinguishes "VM image" from "runtime image". Consequence: the P4.2b implementer must guess where the catalog lives; the P4.4b implementer must guess whether the mount shadows image content and whether `module_catalog_version` is checked against the host's identity. If the catalog lands in the repo-selected VM image, "hash-pinned" and the capability intersection of workspace-vm-api line 71 are hollow.

**Change**: Design §6.3 "Image", after the first sentence: "Two artefacts are distinct. The VM image (`VmSpec.image`) is the guest userland only; it never supplies `g5d`, the provider CLIs or the module catalog. The runtime directory `/opt/g5` (`bin/g5d`, the pinned provider CLIs, `modules/catalog.json`, the fake binaries in CI) is part of the host's runtime image, is hash-pinned, and is mounted read-only over virtio-fs at `/opt/g5`, shadowing any `/opt/g5` the VM image contains; its hash is `runtime_identity.runtime_dir_hash` and the catalog's hash is `runtime_identity.module_catalog_hash`." Change "Mounts" at design 544 and worker-host 157 from `/opt/g5/bin` to `/opt/g5`. Worker-host line 120: "except the base image files, the runtime directory `/opt/g5` and the cache paths". Agent-api 278: "Worker & Host supplies the pinned runtime directory (g5d, module catalog, the actual CLI binaries)". Plan P4.2b step 1: "in the host runtime directory mounted read-only at `/opt/g5`, never in the VM image"; P4.4b step 2: "mount the host runtime directory at `/opt/g5` (it shadows image content)"; P4.5 step 1 `ready`: "refuse a `ready` whose `module_catalog_version` differs from the placing host's `runtime_identity.module_catalog_hash`: row `failed`, `last_error=catalog_mismatch`".

**Validation to add**: P4.4b docker-ci isolation lane: boot a workspace VM from a test image that ships its own `/opt/g5/bin/g5d` (a script printing a marker) and a catalog with a different hash; assert the dispatcher reporting `runtime.ready` is the host's (its `dispatcher_contract_version` and `module_catalog_version` equal the host's `runtime_identity` values) and `fs.read` of `/opt/g5/modules/catalog.json` returns the host file. P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: a `vm.event ready` whose `module_catalog_version` differs from `worker_hosts.runtime_identity.module_catalog_hash` leaves the row not `running` with `catalog_mismatch`; a matching one goes `running`. Both fail today: no shadowing mount and no comparison exist.

### N113 — [P2] Mirror freshness has no data-model home or label: base reads and fork/review pins say `committed` with no observation time, and a persistently failing poll freezes the mirror silently

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 190, 218; [design §5.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 361; [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 444; [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 852, 854; [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 33; [plan P2.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 394; [plan P4.14](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 929, 934; [repo_fetcher.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/repo_fetcher.rs) lines 266–294.

**Evidence**: design line 190: "repos(id, org_id, provider NULL|github|gitlab, provider_full_name, git_url,". Line 852: "`source: flush` or `committed`, OID and capture time". Line 854: "| base workspace, any reader including owner | existing protected/default Git refs | `source: committed`, OID |". Sharing line 33: "A response identifies `source=live|flush|committed`, the repository/OID vector or live `vm_id`, and observation time/freshness." Plan line 394: "Return source, per-repo OIDs, snapshot/observation time and stale/loss indicators". Plan line 929: "immutable per-repo source_manifest/OIDs/age".

**Problem**: Observation: `repos` has no column recording when a protected ref was last fetched or whether the last fetch failed (grep of all documents for last_fetch, mirrored_at, observed_at, mirror_state, mirror_error finds nothing that applies to the mirror); the base-workspace read row returns `committed, OID` where the two rows above return "OID and capture time"; the sharing note, P2.1 item 3 and P4.14 items 1 and 6 require observation time, "stale/loss indicators" and an "age" that no field supplies, and nothing says what "age" measures for a `committed` source: commit author time or the last successful provider observation. `source_manifest` is `jsonb` (design 218) with no field list. N35 fixes the poll identity and `provider_unverified` at preflight but not what readers see when the poll has failed for hours; §5.6 line 361 rebases onto the mirror, so a stale mirror rebases onto old upstream silently. Consequence (hypothesis on the natural implementation): P1.5 logs fetch failures with `warn!` (the retained repo_fetcher.rs pattern) and moves on; the base keeps serving a `committed` OID that may be days old, forks and reviews pin it, "age" is implemented as commit age, which hides mirror staleness, and a token revoked when the connecting user leaves the org (N60) freezes the mirror under a green label with no operator signal. P2.1, P4.5 and P4.14 must each invent the value.

**Change**: Design §5.1: add `repo_mirror_refs(repo_id, ref_name, oid, provider_oid NULL, state fresh|diverged|provider_missing|fetch_failed, last_success_at, last_attempt_at, last_error NULL, PRIMARY KEY (repo_id, ref_name))`, written only by the inbound mirror under the per-repo lock (N116). §5.6 Inbound: `stale` is `now - last_success_at > 3 x poll interval` or `state != fresh`; `fetch_failed` after N consecutive attempts appears on the repo settings page with a reconnect action. §9.1 line 854: "`source: committed`, OID, `mirror_observed_at`, `mirror_state`". §5.9 "Source and navigation" and agent-api §5.9: a normative per-repo `source_manifest` entry `{repo_id, ref, oid, receipt_id?, source: flush|committed, captured_at, mirror_observed_at?, mirror_state?}`; "age" is `now - captured_at` for flush sources and `now - mirror_observed_at` for committed provider-backed sources, with `stale` shown when `mirror_state != fresh` at pin time. §5.6 "Updating a workspace from upstream": `workspace.changes update` returns the mirror state. Copy into plan P1.5 item 1 (P1.5a of N111), P2.1 item 3, P4.5 item 11, P4.14 items 1 and 6.

**Validation to add**: P1.5(a) with a `file://` provider and the fake token store: (a) delete the `user_scm_connections` row between two ticks: the next tick records `fetch_failed` with `last_error`, `last_success_at` and the internal ref unchanged; restoring it clears the state. (b) With the poller stopped and the provider advanced two commits, `GET /workspaces/:base/tree` returns `source: committed`, an old `mirror_observed_at` and `mirror_state: stale`; a P4.5 fork from that base writes `mirror_state: stale` into `source_manifest`; after one tick the read reports `fresh` with the new OID while the fork's pinned OID is unchanged. (c) P4.14: a request accepted against a stale base carries the marker, so a build deriving age from commit time fails. Each fails without `repo_mirror_refs`.

### N114 — [P2] "Notification to the workspace's members" names the read invite list, may omit the only person who can act (the owner), and would fan out to readers who cannot commit or push; the loss and flush-abort notifications share the undefined recipient

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 198, 251; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 337, 338; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 420; [design §12](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 984; [sharing](workspace-sharing-v9.md) line 24; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 671; [plan P6.4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 2; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 4292; [notification_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/notification_service.rs) lines 176, 365; [notifications.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/notifications.rs) lines 121, 129.

**Evidence**: design line 420: "a notification to the workspace's members". Line 198: "workspace_members(workspace_id, user_id, PRIMARY KEY (workspace_id, user_id)) -- read invite list". Line 251: "The owner always reads their workspace. Org admins read any workspace; other org members read when visibility is `org` or their invite is present in `workspace_members`." Line 337: "the user is notified". Line 338: "a notification names the workspace". Baseline line 4292: `recipient_user_id uuid NOT NULL,`. notifications.rs line 121: `.audited_route("/read-all", post(mark_all_read), &[Scope::WorkspacesWrite])`.

**Problem**: Observation: the only defined "members" structure is the read invite list; the owner reads by ownership, not by a member row, and an org-visible workspace has no member rows at all. Readers cannot push, Save, use a terminal or chat (sharing line 24 and neighbours), so "commit and push" is actionable by the owner only. The retained table is per recipient, `create_notification` takes one recipient, no fan-out helper exists, and the mark-read routes require `workspaces:write`, a scope P6.4 drops and a reader never holds. Lines 337 and 338 define two more lifecycle notifications with no recipient; N79 covers transfer notifications only; grep of all documents for "recipient" finds nothing. Consequence: a literal implementation notifies every invitee of an invite-only workspace and nobody for an org-visible one, possibly excluding the owner; a generous one broadcasts to every org member per drained VM. Category, severity, one-row-per-operation idempotency and whether readers may mark rows read are undefined, so P4.5, P8.7 and P4.10 each invent a rule, and the owner, the one person who can save the work, may never learn of the teardown.

**Change**: Design §5.8 (line 420) and §5.4 (lines 337–338): "Lifecycle notifications (`workspace.teardown_pending`, `workspace.unflushed_loss`, `workspace.flush_aborted`) are written to `notifications` for exactly one recipient, the current `owner_user_id`, with `workspace_id`, `actor_user_id` (the operator, or NULL for the rotation loop, reconciler or host), `severity` (`warning` for teardown_pending, `error` for the other two) and a `message` naming the stop reason and deadline; one row per (workspace_id, category, stop operation id), so a retry or lifecycle restart writes none. Readers and non-owning org admins receive no lifecycle notifications; they see the pending-teardown banner and `unflushed_loss_at` through the workspace status." Plan: add the recipient rule, categories and idempotency key to P4.5 item 2 and P8.7 item 3; P6.4 item 2: "notification list and mark-read routes are authorized by `recipient_user_id = caller`; remove the `workspaces:write` gate together with the scope tables".

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs and P8.7 api_rollouts.rs: an invite-only workspace with owner, two invitees and a non-owning org admin; trigger the nudge; exactly one `notifications` row with `recipient_user_id = owner_user_id` and `category = workspace.teardown_pending`, zero for invitees or the admin; repeat for an org-visible workspace with three other members (still one row); replay the same stop operation id after an API restart and assert the count is unchanged; force a failed flush past the deadline and assert one `workspace.unflushed_loss` row for the owner. P6.4 api_notifications.rs: after the scope tables are dropped the owner marks the row read with no write scope; an invitee's mark-read of that id returns 404. Fails at HEAD: no writer exists and mark-read requires `workspaces:write`.

### N115 — [P2] Nudge window has no lifecycle state: the VM must stay `running` for 30 minutes to honor "commit and push", yet `stop_deadline_at` exists only in the `stopping` row and the deadline means both "interrupt turns" and "stop retrying the flush"

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 237; [design §5.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 338; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 415, 420, 426, 427, 429; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 645; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 671; [plan P4.10](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 835; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 911; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1196.

**Evidence**: design line 420: "asking to commit and push. Turns that are still running at the deadline are interrupted and continue after the resume (§7.2)." Line 426: "1. **Decide.** `workspace_vms.status` → `stopping`, `stop_reason` and `stop_deadline_at` recorded in the same update." Line 427: "2. **Close admission.** The Workspace Runtime facade refuses new Save, module invocations, preview, terminal and agent-turn requests for the workspace with `WORKSPACE_STOPPING`". Line 338: "retries until `stop_deadline_at`, then **proceeds without a receipt**". Line 645: "run the stop sequence with reason `drain` (nudge, deadline, flush, release)". Plan line 671: "a `notice` appended to every running chat session before the stop sequence starts; the deadline default 30 minutes after the nudge."

**Problem**: Observation: the nudge precedes the stop sequence and expects the user to commit and push and turns to run until the deadline, but the only persisted deadline is written in step 1 together with `status=stopping`, after which step 2 refuses Save, terminal and agent turns, everything the nudge asks for; lines 338, 415, 429 and 645 also make the same deadline the point at which flush retries stop. No column records that a nudge was sent, when, or the pending reason while the row is `running`; P4.10 item 1 renders a banner and P8.7 item 3 tracks drain progress with no field; nothing says what a lifecycle service restarted between nudge and deadline does. Consequence: two consistent readings produce different systems: (A) nudge, then step 1 at once with `stop_deadline_at = now + 30 min`, admission closed within seconds, so the nudge cannot be honoured; (B) the VM stays `running` for 30 minutes and the stop sequence starts at the deadline, so "retries until `stop_deadline_at`" is already past when the flush starts and a drained host never reaches `host.drained` when one push keeps failing. An API restart mid-drain double-notifies every owner or drops the teardown. P4.5's implementer picks (A) because the data model supports only it.

**Change**: Design §5.1 `workspace_vms`: add `pending_stop_reason NULL, pending_stop_deadline_at NULL, nudged_at NULL`. Design §5.8 line 420: "A deadline-bound teardown has two phases. Nudge phase: one update sets `pending_stop_reason`, `pending_stop_deadline_at` (default `nudged_at` + 30 min; for `credential`, the expiry minus the margin if sooner) and `nudged_at`, and the same transaction writes the notification (N114) and the session notices (N121); status stays `running`, admission stays open and new turns are admitted; `GET /workspaces/:id/vm` exposes the three fields. Stop phase: at `pending_stop_deadline_at` step 1 copies the reason into `stop_reason`, sets `stop_deadline_at = now + flush_deadline_secs` (default 10 min, §17.1) and clears the pending fields; running turns are interrupted in step 3 and continue after the resume (§7.2); the flush retries until `stop_deadline_at`, then proceeds and records the loss (§5.4). A `running` row with `nudged_at` set is already nudged: a restarted lifecycle service re-arms the timer from `pending_stop_deadline_at` and writes no second notification or notice. A user stop, transfer or delete during the nudge phase supersedes it and clears the pending fields." Amend 338, 415 and 645 to "until `stop_deadline_at` (the flush deadline)". Plan: columns to P4.1 item 1; the two phases and restart rule to P4.5 items 1 and 2; the fields to P4.5 item 6 and P4.10 item 1; name them as the `drains`/`rollouts` progress source in P8.7 item 3; `flush_deadline_secs` to §17.1.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: operator stop with reason `capacity` on a running workspace with a fake turn; the row stays `running` with `pending_stop_*` and `nudged_at` set, an owner terminal open and an owner agent-turn request are admitted after the nudge, `GET /workspaces/:id/vm` returns the pending deadline; advance the clock past it: the row becomes `stopping` with `stop_reason=capacity`, the fake turn receives SIGINT, `stop_deadline_at` equals the quiesce time plus `flush_deadline_secs`; with a failing flush the release happens at `stop_deadline_at` with `unflushed_loss_at` set. Restart the lifecycle service between nudge and deadline: exactly one `notifications` row and one notice row per session exist afterwards and the stop still happens at the recorded deadline. Fails at HEAD: no pending fields, single-phase stop.

### N116 — [P2] Inbound mirror has no rule for a non-fast-forward or deleted provider protected ref, and the retained fetch primitive is a forced all-heads refspec that rewinds the mirror and imports workspace branches

**Location**: [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 282; [design §5.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 359, 361; [design §9.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 854; [design §10.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 932; [plan P1.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 349, 353; [repo.rs](../backend/g5-scm/src/repo.rs) lines 557–558, 575, 626; [merge.rs](../backend/g5-workspace-ops/src/merge.rs) line 99; [repo_fetcher.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/maintenance/repo_fetcher.rs) line 255.

**Evidence**: design line 359: "For each connected repo the API fetches the protected branches from the provider into the mirror on webhook and on a periodic poll (default 5 minutes). … Workspace branches are never fetched from the provider." Line 282: "repository bootstrap or provider import (§5.2, §5.6), honoring fork reservations". Plan line 349: "fetch `refs/heads/<protected>` from the provider into the mirror with the connecting user's stored token (`ScmTokenManager`), under a per-repo lock that P6.2 will share." Plan line 353: "add protected_branch_mirror.rs (fetch updates the mirror, workspace branches are never fetched)". repo.rs lines 557–558: `if repo.is_bare() { return Ok("+refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*".to_string());`.

**Problem**: Observation: line 359 is the only normative sentence on inbound mirroring; neither it nor P1.5 says what the mirror does when the provider ref moved non-fast-forward or was deleted, and the only test is "fetch updates the mirror". Grep of all documents for force-push, non-fast-forward, rewind, diverge, prune or a deleted provider branch finds nothing about the protected-ref mirror. The plan names no primitive; the retained bare-repo fetch (`GitRepo::fetch`, used through `g5_workspace_ops::fetch` by repo_fetcher.rs) is a forced all-heads refspec and `fetch_single_ref` is forced too. Plan 349 does direct an explicit `refs/heads/<protected>` fetch, so the wildcard-import hazard is a footgun, not an instruction. Consequence: forced-follow and fast-forward-only are an unspecified choice with divergent user-visible behaviour, and both pass the specified test: forced, a provider force-push silently rewinds the base view, every projection and "Update from origin", and a fork pinned a minute earlier points at a commit no longer on the branch; fast-forward-only, the mirror silently freezes after a rewrite (N113's failure mode). A deleted provider branch is either pruned, removing the base workspace's only source ref, or left stale forever. "Honoring fork reservations" at import has no defined outcome.

**Change**: Design §5.6 "Inbound", an "Inbound update policy", copied into plan P1.5 item 1 (P1.5a of N111): (1) the mirror fetches exactly `refs/heads/<p>:refs/heads/<p>` per name in `repos.protected_branches`, never a wildcard, through a new `GitRepo::fetch_protected_ref(remote, name, auth)` in g5-scm; `GitRepo::fetch` and `g5_workspace_ops::fetch` are not used by the mirror; (2) a fast-forward update is applied under the per-repo lock and publishes `branch.updated`; (3) a non-fast-forward provider head is either applied and recorded as `rewrite` in `repo_mirror_refs` (N113) with old and new OIDs, surfaced in the base header and in "Update from origin", or recorded as `diverged`, not applied, and resolved by an org-admin `POST /repos/:id/mirror/accept-rewrite`; the design picks one; (4) a provider ref that disappears is never deleted internally; record `provider_missing`; (5) adding a name to `protected_branches` fails 409 when that name is an existing workspace mapping or a live fork reservation; (6) merge step 4's post-merge import follows the same policy.

**Validation to add**: P1.5(a) `protected_branch_mirror.rs` against a `file://` bare provider remote: (a) a provider fast-forward moves the mirror ref and emits `branch.updated`; (b) `git push --force` on the provider protected branch produces the documented outcome (ref moved plus a `rewrite` row, or ref unchanged plus `diverged`) and a base-workspace read reports that state; (c) `git push --delete` leaves the internal ref and records `provider_missing`; (d) a provider with 50 heads, one named like an existing internal workspace branch, leaves `git for-each-ref refs/heads` unchanged except the protected names; (e) mutation: swapping the refspec for `+refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*` fails (d), and dropping the state write fails (b) and (c). Each fails on a build that only does "fetch updates the mirror".

### N117 — [P2] Image resolution inputs are unspecified: which tree, which member repo, tag-to-digest resolution and malformed or ambiguous YAML behaviour

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 204; [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 347; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 542; [worker-host §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 126, 129; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 670, 676; [assignment.rs](../backend/g5d-host/src/assignment.rs) lines 855, 857, 931; [ports.rs](../backend/g5d/src/host/ports.rs) line 124; [worker_host.rs](../backend/g5-contracts/src/types/worker_host.rs) line 348.

**Evidence**: design line 542: "the one named by `base_image:` in the code repo's `.g5/workspace.yaml`, resolved at each attach". Worker-host line 126: "image:         { ref, digest },                       -- OCI, in the host cache or fetchable". Line 129: "repos:         [{ repo_id, root_path, git_url, branch, base_branch }]". Design line 204: "workspace_repos(workspace_id, repo_id, root_path, role code|docs,". assignment.rs line 855: "two+ code repos naming DIFFERENT values is ambiguous and". ports.rs line 124: "`Ok(None)` while an unreadable or malformed workspace.yaml is a resolution".

**Problem**: Observation: line 542 says "the code repo" in the singular while a workspace has N repos with `role code|docs`; the API resolves before the VM exists ("resolved at each attach", plan 670), so it reads `.g5/workspace.yaml` from git, but no document names the tree (the receipt's `local_head_oid`, its `snapshot_oid` which may carry an uncommitted edit, `refs/heads/<branch>` or `base_branch`); `VmSpec.image` requires `{ref, digest}` but nothing says who resolves a tag or whether tags are permitted, and the retained resolver accepts tag form while `g5-contracts::validate_base_image_ref` is digest-only; no document states the outcome for malformed YAML or two code repos disagreeing, which the retained ports.rs and assignment.rs treat as fail-closed errors. The P4.5 test at plan 676 ("a restart picks up a changed `base_image`") covers the happy path only. Consequence: P4.5 step 1 cannot be implemented without four guesses; reading the snapshot tree makes the image depend on an uncommitted edit, a control-plane tag resolution adds a registry dependency at every attach and moves the digest between restarts (defeating worker-host line 152 as a reproducibility guarantee), and a silent org-default fallback hides a broken config; P4.5 and P4.4b can land incompatible expectations.

**Change**: Design §6.3 "Image", after N110's sentence: "Resolution runs in the control plane before the `VmSpec` is built and reads `.g5/workspace.yaml` from the committed tree at the receipt's `local_head_oid` of every `role=code` member repo (never the snapshot tree, never `role=docs` repos; on a first attach without a receipt, the branch head OID being restored). Exactly one distinct value selects the image; two code repos naming different images fail the attach with `IMAGE_AMBIGUOUS`; unreadable or malformed YAML fails with `IMAGE_INVALID`; absence in every code repo selects the org default. Only digest-pinned references (`validate_base_image_ref`) are accepted; the control plane never resolves tags. The resolved `{ref, digest}` is written to `workspace_vms.image_ref/image_digest` in the CAS that sets `starting`, before `vm.start`." Plan P4.5 step 1: cite the rules and codes and "reuse `g5-contracts::validate_base_image_ref`, not `g5d_foundation::oci::parse_image_ref`"; plan P4.1: add `IMAGE_AMBIGUOUS` and `IMAGE_INVALID` to the error catalogue beside N110's `IMAGE_NOT_ALLOWED`.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs against the real internal git service: (a) a receipt whose `snapshot_oid` tree edits `base_image:` while `local_head_oid` does not: the recorded `image_digest` is the head's; (b) two code repos with different `base_image:`: `IMAGE_AMBIGUOUS`, row not `starting`, no `vm.start`; (c) only the docs-role repo names an image: the org default is recorded; (d) a tag-only reference: `IMAGE_INVALID`; (e) unparseable YAML: `IMAGE_INVALID` with `last_error`; (f) a valid digest: `image_ref/image_digest` populated before the `ScriptedHostClient` observes `vm.start`. (a), (b), (d) and (e) fail without the rules because the retained resolver accepts tags and has no tree selection.

### N118 — [P2] The webhook trigger has no route, signature verification or payload-trust rule; the plan tells the implementer to grep for handling that does not exist on main

**Location**: [design §5.6](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 359; [plan P1.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 349; [plan P8.8](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 0; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) lines 2664, 4543.

**Evidence**: design line 359: "fetches the protected branches from the provider into the mirror on webhook and on a periodic poll (default 5 minutes)". Plan line 349: "on the provider webhook if one exists (grep for webhook handling; `repo_secrets.webhook_secret_enc` suggests one)". Baseline line 4543: `webhook_secret_enc bytea,`. Line 2664: `CREATE FUNCTION public.repo_webhook_secret_for_current_org(target_repo_id uuid) RETURNS bytea`.

**Problem**: Observation: the design's only word on the subject is "on webhook" and the plan defers the question to the implementer. I ran that grep: `webhook` appears only in the baseline migration, two integration tests that insert into `repo_secrets`, and comments or constants in `branch_integration_queue.rs` and `branch_sync_run.rs`, both deleted by P1.5 item 3. There is no webhook route, signature check, provider registration flow, production writer of `webhook_secret_enc`, or rule on whether a payload's `after` SHA may be trusted; §17.2/§17.3 do not defer webhooks and P8.8 does not list one. Consequence (hypothesis on the two natural outcomes): the implementer drops the webhook, making 5-minute staleness the floor for every provider-backed base, fork and review pin (N113), or builds an unauthenticated `POST /api/webhooks/:provider/:repo_id` that triggers a fetch; without HMAC verification any Internet client can drive provider fetches with the connecting user's token, burning its rate limits (an amplifier in the sense of N82) and holding the per-repo lock that merges share (§10.6 step 1); a handler that trusts payload OIDs poisons the mirror; and because nothing writes the secret column, treating NULL as "skip verification" is the natural wrong default. P2 because the poll remains authoritative and no data is written from payloads unless the agent chooses to.

**Change**: Design §5.6 Inbound: "Webhook: `POST /api/webhooks/git/:repo_id` is the only unauthenticated route on the public ingress; it verifies the provider signature against `repo_secrets.webhook_secret_enc` (GitHub `X-Hub-Signature-256` HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body; GitLab `X-Gitlab-Token` constant-time compare), rejects with 401 when the secret is absent or the check fails, and on success only enqueues a fetch of the named protected ref for that repo; payload OIDs are never written to the mirror or to `branch.updated`. Repo connect registers the webhook with a freshly generated secret when the token has the hook scope and records `webhook_registered_at`; otherwise `webhook: none` and the poll is the only trigger. Deliveries coalesce to one in-flight fetch per repo. The poll remains authoritative; a missed webhook is only latency." Plan P1.5 item 1 (P1.5b of N111): the route in `routes/webhooks.rs`; item 2 (Make Remote): secret generation and registration; P8.8 item 0: the route in the authority-matrix audit list.

**Validation to add**: P1.5 `api_git_webhook.rs`: a delivery with a valid signature for a `file://` provider whose protected branch advanced triggers exactly one fetch and the mirror moves; an invalid or missing signature returns 401 and `git for-each-ref` is unchanged; a valid delivery whose `after` SHA is fabricated leaves the mirror at the provider's real head; 20 valid deliveries in one second produce at most one in-flight fetch; a repo whose `webhook_secret_enc` is NULL rejects every delivery. Mutation: removing the signature check fails the 401 case. P8.8: the unauthenticated-route inventory lists exactly this route.

### N119 — [P2] Image change authority is unassigned: agents commit `.g5/workspace.yaml` freely while P4.5 lists config edits as owner mutations, and no rollout, audit or UI surfaces the image a restart will boot

**Location**: [design §5.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 235; [design §5.7](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 377; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 388; [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 542; [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 647; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 668, 676; [plan P4.10](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 835; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1196.

**Evidence**: design line 388: "| **restart** | the stop sequence with reason `restart`, then resume immediately, with the image re-resolved from `.g5/workspace.yaml` (§6.3) |". Line 542: "Changing it takes effect at the next restart; the Restart button exists for that and for repairing a broken workspace." Line 377: "Nothing prevents an agent or a terminal user from running `git checkout`; the profile text tells agents to stay on the branch". Line 647: "workspaces pick up a new VM image or dispatcher by a rollout drain". Plan line 1196: "`POST /orgs/:org/rollouts` naming the new image or dispatcher version". Plan line 676: "a restart picks up a changed `base_image`".

**Problem**: Observation: P4.5 step 0 (plan 668) puts "preview-profile/config edits" under current-owner checks, but `.g5/workspace.yaml` is a tracked file that agents commit with native git, so the owner check on a UI route does not bound who changes the image; a restart re-resolves it with no confirmation and the only test asserts the change is picked up. The rollout names "the new image" at org level while the effective image is repo-selected, and neither text says whether a rollout overrides a repo pin or leaves pinned workspaces on the old image. `workspace_vms` (line 235) records only the last boot's `image_ref, image_digest`; no audit record, `image_source` or pending-image field exists; P4.10's status strip (plan 835) has no image element. Consequence: P8.7's agent must guess whether a CVE rollout is effective for pinned repositories; P4.5's agent must guess whether a changed image needs owner acknowledgement; owners cannot see or veto an image switch an agent committed, and nothing records who selected the image that ran with their grants (after a transfer, the next owner's).

**Change**: Design §6.3 "Image" (with N110's allowlist as the bound): "Each boot records `image_source: org_default | workspace_yaml` and `image_resolved_from_oid` on `workspace_vms` and appends an audit record (actor, workspace, image_ref, digest, source, oid). `GET /workspaces/:id/vm` returns `pending_image_digest` resolved from the current branch head; the status strip shows it beside the running digest when they differ." Design §6.10 "Rollout" and plan P8.7 step 3: "a rollout sets the new org default and may remove old digests from `allowed_images`; an allowed repo pin is honored, a disallowed pin makes the rollout resume fail with `IMAGE_NOT_ALLOWED` and holds the work until the repository is updated." Plan P4.1: the two `workspace_vms` columns; P4.5 step 1: record source and OID, audit; P4.10 step 1: pending-image element; P4.11 profile text: "agents do not edit `.g5/workspace.yaml base_image:` unless asked".

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: (1) a push setting `base_image:` to a digest not in `allowed_images`, then `POST /restart`: `IMAGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, no `vm.start`, row stays `stopped`; (2) an allowed digest: the new row has `image_source = workspace_yaml`, `image_resolved_from_oid` = head, one audit record; (3) `GET /workspaces/:id/vm` shows `pending_image_digest` ≠ `image_digest` after the push and before restart. P8.7 api_rollouts.rs: a rollout removing the old default from the allowlist resumes an unpinned workspace on the new default and fails a workspace pinned to the old digest with `IMAGE_NOT_ALLOWED` and held work. P4.10 Playwright workspace-vm.spec.ts: the strip shows the pending digest after the push. All fail today: no columns, field or rollout rule exist.

### N120 — [P2] The nudge is an MVP deliverable of P4.5 with no MVP trigger, consumer or test: its only orchestrators (drain, rollout) are post-MVP P8.7, P4.13 defers the per-VM sequence, and P4.6/P4.11 never mention the notice

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 417, 418, 420; [plan §4](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 200; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 671, 676; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 719; [plan P4.10](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 835; [plan P4.11](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 861, 866; [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 911; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1198.

**Evidence**: plan line 671: "2. Teardown nudge: for deadline-bound reasons, a notification to the members and a `notice` appended to every running chat session before the stop sequence starts". Plan line 911: "The per-VM stop sequences with reason `drain`, their retries until the deadline, `host.drained` handling and rotation are P8.7". Plan line 200: "**After the MVP, in priority order.** … rollout gates (P8.7)". Plan line 1198: "api_rollouts.rs (nudge delivered, deadline interrupt, flush, resume with the new image". Design line 418: "| `capacity` | an operator action to free a slot (never automatic) | as `drain` | as `drain` |".

**Problem**: Observation: P4.5 item 2 owns the nudge and P4.5 is MVP (plan 198), but P4.5's tests (line 676) contain no nudge assertion; P4.13 item 3 defers the per-VM drain sequence to P8.7, which is post-MVP and the only PR whose tests mention "nudge delivered". The two MVP deadline-bound reasons, `capacity` (an operator action, 418) and `credential` (417, whose actor N39 already reports as undefined), have no route or reconciler named in P4.5 item 6, P4.13 or P4.4a (plan 629 tests only that renewal is refused). P4.6 item 7 and P4.11 items 1 and 6, which own inbox delivery at the turn boundary, never mention a platform-authored notice. Consequence: in the MVP no deadline-bound teardown is reachable from any route or reconciler, so the nudge writer ships as untested code against an invented representation (N121) or is omitted; P4.6/P4.11 ship inbox semantics that cannot carry a system notice; P8.7's first rehearsal, on the critical path of the first production rollout, is where the whole notice path is discovered missing and must be retrofitted into merged PRs. MVP impact is bounded (no user sees a wrong nudge), hence P2.

**Change**: Plan: P4.5 item 6 or P4.13 item 4 names the MVP trigger for `capacity` (`POST /workspaces/:id/stop {reason: capacity, deadline_secs}`, org admin or operator only) and P4.5 item 3 names the reconciler that issues reason `credential` from `worker_hosts.credential_expires_at` minus the margin (resolving N39's actor); P4.5 item 2 names the notice row (N121) and item 7 adds a service-level test calling `stop(reason=drain, deadline)` on a workspace with a fake running turn; P4.6 item 7 gains "queued system rows are delivered with the next reservation and withdrawn at release"; P4.11 item 6 gains "render queued system rows as a preamble and list them in `input.accepted.system_notice_ids`"; P4.13 item 3 states that P8.7 reuses P4.5's `stop(reason, deadline)` including its nudge; P8.7 item 5 spells out "nudge delivered" as rows written once per session, recipient (N114), delivered/withdrawn transitions, zero duplicates after a lifecycle-service restart between nudge and deadline; P4.10 item 1 names `pending_stop_reason`/`pending_stop_deadline_at` (N115) as the banner source.

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: `stop(reason=drain, deadline)` on a running workspace with one running fake turn and one idle session writes exactly one owner notification and exactly one queued system row in the session with the nonterminal task (none in the idle session) before admission closes; a second call with the same operation id writes nothing more; the same through the operator `capacity` route once it exists. P4.6: dispatch of the next input after the nudge reserves the system row with the ordinary input and `input.accepted.system_notice_ids` names it; release without a next turn leaves it `withdrawn`. P4.11 fake-provider test: the first frame begins with the notice text followed by the ordinary input. P8.7 api_rollouts.rs: the same for `drain` and `rollout`, plus zero duplicate rows after killing and restarting the lifecycle service between nudge and deadline. All fail at HEAD: no route, row or delivery rule exists.

### N121 — [P2] The nudge "notice event" has no table, row shape or delivery rule; every candidate (`chat_messages` system row, C2 `notice`, `notifications`) contradicts a stated rule

**Location**: [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 420, 427; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 752, 755, 766; [design §8.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 772; [agent-api §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 214; [agent-api §8.1](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 297; [agent-api §9](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 374, 379; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 671; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 719; [chat_context_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/chat_context_service.rs) line 3173; [chat_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/chat_service.rs) line 1461; [001_lean_baseline.sql](../backend/g5-api/src/db/migrations/001_lean_baseline.sql) line 3435.

**Evidence**: design line 420: "a notice event appended to every running chat session that is delivered at the next turn boundary (a running CLI turn cannot receive a mid-turn message in the MVP)". Line 752: "chat_messages(id, session_id, index, role user|assistant|system,". Line 772: "Human send or workflow launch persists a session input and creates an `agent` task only when the session has no nonterminal logical task." Line 427: "no new inbox reservations, queued inbox rows stay queued". Agent-api line 297: "Both MVP providers receive one ordinary user input per attempt." Line 379: "A C2 `notice` may describe an error, but it does not terminate the execution." chat_context_service.rs line 3173: `fn is_eligible(role: &str, status: &str, content: &str) -> bool {`.

**Problem**: Observation: the notice appears only at design 420, plan 671 and plan 1198 ("nudge delivered"); no document names its table or delivery rule. Each retained candidate collides with a rule: (a) a `chat_messages` row with `role system` carries the `delivery` states of line 755, but P4.6 item 7 defines them for reserved input rows only, agent-api 297 fixes one ordinary input per attempt (N90), §8.2 step 1 says only human send or workflow launch creates a task, line 766 says only the owner sends input, and on main `is_eligible` excludes system rows from provider context while chat_service.rs reserves them for a legacy "wake turn" protocol; (b) the C2 `notice` variant (agent-api 374) is a journaled provider event keyed by `exec_id`, emitted inside an execution and display-only, so the lifecycle service cannot append one to a session with no live execution and nothing routes it into a provider's stdin; (c) `notifications` is per recipient, not per session. Stop step 2 closes new inbox reservations, so any inbox-shaped notice is undeliverable once the sequence starts (N115). Nothing says which sessions are "running", what happens to a notice never delivered before the deadline, or how the text reaches the provider. Consequence: P4.5 ships a writer with no target row, P4.6/P4.11 never pick it up, P8.7's assertion is unfalsifiable; the worst guesses (a system row that creates a task on the owner's account; concatenation that makes one `input.accepted` cover several rows) each break an explicit invariant, though §8.2 step 1 already forbids the first, hence P2.

**Change**: Design §8.1, after the "Inbox" paragraph: "A platform notice is a `chat_messages` row with `role=system`, `input_origin=NULL`, `status=complete`, `delivery=queued`, `content={code, stop_reason, deadline_at, operation_id}`, written by the lifecycle service in the nudge transaction (N115) for every session of the workspace whose logical agent task is nonterminal (running, waiting or held). It creates no task, is never itself a reservation and is never a separate provider frame. When the next input of that session is reserved, the API renders the queued system rows created before that reservation as a platform preamble inside the `prompt_ref` artifact and lists their ids in `input.accepted.system_notice_ids[]`; the transaction that journals `input.accepted` sets them `delivered`. When the teardown releases, is cancelled or the deadline passes with no further attempt, still-queued notices become `withdrawn` (audit only) and the §7.2 continuation input (N122) carries the interruption. System rows appear in history for every reader and are excluded from the §8.5 fallback history (retained `is_eligible` behaviour)." Agent-api §9: `system_notice_ids?: string[]` on `input.accepted`. Plan: P4.5 item 2 names the row and transaction; P4.6 item 7 adds the queued→delivered/withdrawn transitions for system rows; P4.11 item 1 the preamble rendering and extended `input.accepted`; P4.1 item 7 states that the `chat_messages.role` CHECK keeps `system` and drops or retains `tool` explicitly (baseline 3435 has four roles, design 752 three).

**Validation to add**: P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs (operator stop, reason `capacity`) and P8.7 api_rollouts.rs: sessions A (running fake turn), B (terminal task, idle), C (held task). After the nudge: exactly one `role=system, delivery=queued` row in A and C, none in B; `tasks` count unchanged; zero new `task_events` for A and C; zero provider processes spawned. Owner sends to C before the deadline: one attempt starts, the fake provider's first frame contains the notice text once, `input.accepted.system_notice_ids` names it, the row is `delivered`, exactly one `input.accepted` exists. At release A's notice is `withdrawn`. Fails at HEAD: no writer, preamble rendering or system-row transitions.

### N122 — [P2] The platform-authored continuation input ("continue note", `input_origin continuation`, "persisted continuation input") has no row shape, writer or delivery rule, and the companion's `input_origin` enum omits it

**Location**: [design §6.10](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 647; [design §7.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 720; [design §8.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 753; [design §8.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 783; [design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 420; [agent-api §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 203, 230; [plan P4.6](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 719; [plan P4.11](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 861; [plan P8.7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 1198.

**Evidence**: design line 753: "content, attachments jsonb, revision int, input_origin human|workflow|continuation,". Line 783: "AgentTurnInput { task {task_id, attempt, session_id, input_origin human|workflow, initial_input?: reservation},". Agent-api line 203: `input_origin: "human" | "workflow";`. Design line 720: "Reserve the shared inbox input/revision or persisted continuation input". Plan line 861: "frozen initial/continuation message reservation". Plan line 1198: "a new attempt on the same logical task with the continue note". Agent-api line 230: "a continuation follows the recorded accepted-input cut instead of blindly replaying the original prompt."

**Problem**: Observation: `chat_messages.input_origin` has three values and the module input two, with continuation carried as a separate `continuation? {prior_exec_id, reason}` field; design 720, plan 861, design 647 ("its input records the interruption and continuation intent") and P8.7's "continue note" all presuppose a persisted continuation input, but §7.2 "Interruption" never mentions a row, and no document defines its role, content, writer, timing (at quiesce in stop step 3 or when the next attempt is queued), whether it is reserved under the one-input-per-attempt rule, whether it occupies `initial_input`, what text the provider receives, or how readers see it; `AgentSessionInputReservationV1` (agent-api 204) is referenced but never defined. N52 covers `continuation_pending/continuation_reason` on `tasks`, not this row. Consequence: the nudge's promise that interrupted turns "continue after the resume" (line 420) is delivered through this input, so its absence leaves that half of the contract unbuildable: P4.1 must add a CHECK for a value the module contract rejects; P4.6 must "persist continuation identity" with no row; P4.11 must hand Claude `--resume` or Codex `exec resume` a first user frame with no content rule while agent-api 230 forbids replaying the original prompt; P8.7 must assert a shape that does not exist. Divergent guesses (replay, empty frame, invented text, reuse of the delivered human row) surface as a serialization failure between API and module or as a transcript that misrepresents why an attempt restarted.

**Change**: Design §8.1 (Inbox) and agent-api §5 and §8.3: "A continuation input is a `chat_messages` row with `role=system`, `input_origin=continuation`, `delivery=queued`, `task_id` = the logical task, `attempt` = the new attempt, `content = {reason: interrupted|vm_lost|suspended, stop_reason, prior_exec_id, prior_attempt, interrupted_at}`. TaskService writes it in the transaction that settles the prior attempt and sets `continuation_pending` (§7.5). The next attempt reserves it as `initial_input`, so one ordinary input per attempt holds; `AgentTurnInputV1.continuation.prior_exec_id` equals the row's; `input.accepted` names its id. The adapter renders it as the first user frame (fixed template: the previous attempt was interrupted by <reason>; continue from where you stopped; do not repeat completed work) after provider session restore, and prepends it to the delivered-history fallback otherwise. It is never rendered or editable as a human message; readers see it as a system row." Change agent-api 203 and design 783 to `input_origin: "human" | "workflow" | "continuation"` and define `AgentSessionInputReservationV1 {reservation_id, input_id, revision, input_origin}` in agent-api §5. Plan: P4.1 item 7 the CHECK; P4.6 item 7 the writer and transaction; P4.11 items 1 and 6 the rendering and `input.accepted`; P8.7 item 5 defines "continue note" as this row. (Alternative, if no row is wanted: drop `continuation` from design 753 and plan 504, replace the three "continuation input" phrases with "the continuation record on `tasks`", and state the fixed first-frame template in agent-api §8.3.)

**Validation to add**: P4.6 api test extending item 8's interrupt/suspend/retry case: drain-interrupt a running fake turn; assert exactly one `chat_messages` row with `input_origin=continuation`, `role=system`, `task_id`=A, created in the same transaction as attempt 1's `interrupted` outcome (failure-injected commit), reserved by attempt 2 as its only reservation and named by attempt 2's single `input.accepted`; the original human row stays `delivered` and no second `input.accepted` names it. P4.11 fake-provider test: the first frame of a continuation attempt contains the reason and `prior_exec_id` and is not the original prompt; the Codex path receives it followed by EOF. P4.1 migration test: `input_origin='continuation'` inserts and a fourth value fails the CHECK. P8.7 api_rollouts.rs asserts the same row for the rollout path. All fail at HEAD.

### N123 — [P2] `runtime_identity_hash` has no producer, canonicalization or comparison rule, the GCE bootstrap credential is minted before any identity exists, and "boot-time attestation" names no retained host-side check

**Location**: [design §6.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 483, 485; [worker-host §2.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 54; [worker-host §2.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 43; [worker-host §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 109, 113; [plan P4.4a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 626, 630; [minter.rs](../backend/g5-auth/src/minter.rs) lines 248–251; [worker_hosts.rs](../backend/g5-api-routes/src/routes/worker_hosts.rs) line 521; [startup_script.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/cloud/startup_script.rs) line 134; [host_observer.rs](../backend/g5d-host/src/host_observer.rs) line 17; [attestor.rs](../backend/g5d-isolation/src/isolation/attestor.rs) line 1.

**Evidence**: design line 485: "a renewable token with claims `host_id`, `org_id`, `runtime_identity_hash`, TTL 24 h … renewal is refused when the host is draining, terminated, or its runtime identity changed". Worker-host line 43: "runtime_identity     jsonb,              -- g5d version, base image digest, libkrun, libkrunfw, kernel, providers[]". Line 113: "`refused` when the host is `draining`, `terminated`, or its runtime identity no longer matches". Design line 483: "Retained from today: … boot-time attestation". minter.rs lines 249–251: `sub: host_id.to_string(), org: org_id.to_string(), scopes: vec![Scope::WorkerHost],`. host_observer.rs line 17: "`last_attestation_result` is reported as `None` in V1: the host-boot". attestor.rs line 1: "In-guest libkrun isolation attestor."

**Problem**: Observation: the credential carries `runtime_identity_hash` and renewal is refused when the identity "no longer matches", but grep of all documents for `runtime_identity`, attestation, canonical and hash finds no producer of the hash (host or control plane), no canonical serialization of the jsonb (key order, `providers[]` order), no statement of what is compared with what at `host.hello` versus renewal, and no hello-time policy on mismatch (N39 covers only the drain after a refused renewal). The GCE credential is minted at provision time with `sub`, `org`, `scopes` only and delivered by instance metadata before the machine boots, so the first credential cannot carry an identity hash, yet worker-host 54 keeps that bring-up. The only attestor on main is the in-guest per-task check on the `g5d worker --task` path P4.9a deletes, and host_observer.rs states no host-boot attestor is wired; `runtime_identity` is self-reported in `host.hello`. Consequence: the P4.4a/P4.13 agent must invent the hash, the comparison and the bootstrap form; two canonicalizations refuse each other's renewals or never refuse; a host that upgraded g5d or libkrun keeps a valid credential whose hash no longer describes it; "boot-time attestation" has no verifier and no producer, so the security review cannot state what it protects.

**Change**: Worker-host §3.1: "The control plane computes `runtime_identity_hash = sha256(canonical JSON of runtime_identity: keys sorted, providers[] sorted, no whitespace)` from the identity presented at registration (N109) or first `host.hello`, stores identity and hash on `worker_hosts`, and puts the hash in every credential it mints for the host. On every `host.hello` it recomputes the hash from the presented identity; a hello missing a required identity field is `host.reject {invalid_identity}`; a differing hash is accepted (VMs may be running) but sets `worker_hosts.runtime_identity_changed_at`, and the next `host.credential.renew` is `refused`, which starts the §3.3 drain path. The GCE bootstrap credential of §16.1 carries `runtime_identity_hash = null`, TTL ≤ 1 h, and is valid only for `POST /api/internal/hosts/:host_id/register`; the first renewable credential is issued from that exchange." Design §6.2 line 483: replace "boot-time attestation" with "host-reported runtime identity (self-declared; no remote attestation in the MVP)". Plan P4.4a steps 1 and 3: the canonicalization, hello-time comparison and bootstrap credential; P4.13 step 1: registration stores identity and hash.

**Validation to add**: P4.4a (`cargo test -p g5-api-core -p g5d-host`): (1) two `runtime_identity` payloads differing only in key and `providers[]` order hash identically and a credential minted from one renews after a hello with the other; (2) a hello whose recomputed hash differs from the credential claim is accepted, sets `runtime_identity_changed_at`, and the following `host.credential.renew` is `refused`; (3) a bootstrap credential with null hash cannot renew and cannot open the tunnel after its TTL; (4) a `host.hello` with a required identity field removed is `host.reject`. All fail today: no hash producer, comparison or bootstrap distinction exists.

### N124 — [P2] `spec_rejected(image_not_cached)` exists only in the design, and classifying a transient pull failure as `spec_rejected` makes the control plane alert and never retry

**Location**: [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 542; [worker-host §3.2](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 97; [worker-host §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 152; [worker-host §7](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 272; [worker-host §10](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 317, 319; [plan P4.4b](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 646; [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 661–699.

**Evidence**: design line 542: "a host answers `spec_rejected(image_not_cached)` if it cannot fetch the image". Worker-host line 152: "`image.digest` matches the pulled or cached image, else `spec_rejected(image_digest_mismatch)`." Line 97: "`started` \| `already_running` \| `slot_unavailable(reason: slots \| disk \| boot_capacity \| workspace_busy)` \| `spec_rejected(reason)` \| `boot_failed(reason, log_tail)`". Line 319: "| Spec rejected | host validation | `spec_rejected(reason)` | bug: alert, do not retry the same spec |". Line 317: "| Boot fails | host | `boot_failed(reason, log_tail)` | incarnation `failed`, retry placement once, then surface to the user |".

**Problem**: Observation: the design names a refusal `image_not_cached` for "cannot fetch the image"; the companion's ordered validation list has only `image_digest_mismatch`, its result grammar lists no image-fetch variant, and grep of all documents for `image_not_cached`, image pull or timeout finds nothing else. The companion's failure matrix classifies every `spec_rejected` as "bug: alert, do not retry the same spec", while a registry outage, rate limit or pull timeout is transient; placement (line 272) never considers whether a host holds the image. Design line 5 makes the companion normative, so two normative texts disagree on the refusal's name and class; P4.4b step 1 says only "validated in the documented order with the named refusals" and P4.5 never says how `spec_rejected` or `boot_failed` results are handled. Consequence: the P4.4b agent implements the companion's list and never emits `image_not_cached`, or implements the design's and the P4.5 agent, following line 319, alerts and refuses to retry a workspace that hit a registry blip; a fetch failure folded into `image_digest_mismatch` misleads operators into suspecting tampering. Adding heartbeat image digests would contradict §13's option (a) and is not needed.

**Change**: Worker-host §4 ordered list, replace the image rule with two lines in this order: (i) "the image is cached or fetched within `image_pull_timeout_secs` (host configuration, default 300; add a row to the §7.1 limits table and design §6.2 Limits) else `boot_failed(image_unavailable, log_tail)`; the slot is freed"; (ii) "the fetched or cached bytes match `image.digest`, else `spec_rejected(image_digest_mismatch)`". Design §6.3 "Image": replace `spec_rejected(image_not_cached)` with `boot_failed(image_unavailable)` and add "a digest mismatch is `spec_rejected(image_digest_mismatch)`". Worker-host §10: add "Image unavailable | host | `boot_failed(image_unavailable, log_tail)` | retry placement once on another host in the region, then incarnation `failed` with `last_error = IMAGE_UNAVAILABLE` and the log tail surfaced to the owner; no spec-bug alert". Plan P4.4b step 1: both refusals and the pull timeout; P4.5 step 1 (`resume`): "on `boot_failed` re-place once, on `spec_rejected` mark `failed`, alert and do not retry the same spec (worker-host §10)".

**Validation to add**: P4.4b (`cargo test -p g5d-host`, fake registry): an unreachable registry yields `boot_failed(image_unavailable, log_tail)` before `image_pull_timeout_secs` elapses, the slot count returns to free and no `spec_rejected` is emitted; a correct pull whose bytes do not match `image.digest` yields `spec_rejected(image_digest_mismatch)`. P4.5 api_workspace_vms.rs: a `ScriptedHostClient` answering `boot_failed(image_unavailable)` once then `started` yields one re-placement and a `running` row; answering it twice yields `failed`, `last_error = IMAGE_UNAVAILABLE`, no spec-bug alert; answering `spec_rejected(image_digest_mismatch)` yields `failed` plus the alert and no second `vm.start`. Both fail today because neither variant nor handling exists.

### N125 — [P3] Registration, replay, identity binding and revocation have no acceptance observation anywhere: not in the P4.13/P4.4a tests, worker-host §10/§14, or the §7.1 matrix (including the row N102 proposes)

**Location**: [plan P4.13](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 913; [plan P4.4a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 629; [plan §7.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 1347–1364; [worker-host §10](worker-host-api-v9.md) lines 308–327; [worker-host §14](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 384; this review, N102 "Change"; [worker_token_service.rs](../backend/g5-api-core/src/services/worker_token_service.rs) lines 341–382.

**Evidence**: plan line 913: "5. Tests: api_worker_hosts.rs: state transitions including `unreachable` and reconnect; placement preferring the retained root, then the region, then `no_capacity`; quota; occupancy derived; the drain route sends `host.drain` and marks the host draining; operator routes." Plan line 629: "credential renewal refused while draining and the old credential still valid; `host.drain` reports `drained`." Worker-host line 384: "Tests must exercise owner-only credential selection across multiple sessions, spoofed session ids, … live same-owner key rotation, stale old-incarnation requests". Line 308: "## 10. Failure matrix".

**Problem**: Observation: P4.13's test list covers states, placement, quota, occupancy, drain and "operator routes" generically; P4.4a's covers renewal refusal only; worker-host §14 lists VM and owner boundary gates and no host-join case; the §10 matrix has no rogue-host, replayed-token or terminated-reconnect row; N102's §7.1 row observes lease, fence, stop and proxy behaviours, not registration or revocation. Grep of the plan for "one-time", "registration" and "g5w_" finds registration only as an implementation step (lines 909, 1260, 1321), never as a test, and today's `g5w_*` tokens are reusable (N109), so no retained test covers the property either. Consequence: even after N109 and N123 are specified, nothing makes them falsifiable per candidate; an implementer can satisfy every listed test with a reusable token, an unchecked hello identity and a delete that leaves the tunnel open, and the exact-head acceptance record (§7.1) would not show it. A coverage gap rather than a wrong implementation by itself, hence P3; it lands with N109 and N123.

**Change**: Plan P4.13 step 5, append: "registration: a `g5w_*` token redeemed twice yields one `worker_hosts` row and 401 on the second redemption; an expired, revoked, unknown or other-org token is refused; the minted credential's `host_id`/`org_id` claims equal the row; `DELETE …/:host` and the `terminated` transition close an open tunnel and refuse reconnect and renewal with the unexpired credential". Plan P4.4a step 4, append: "tunnel identity binding: path, claim and `host.hello.host_id` mismatch refused before `current_incarnations` is sent; a second tunnel for one `host_id` supersedes the first". Worker-host §10: rows "Registration token replayed or leaked", "Host credential stolen or host compromised", "Terminated or deleted host reconnects". Worker-host §14: "host join, identity binding and revocation" among the required gates. Plan §7.1: extend N102's "Host protocol, lease and proxies" row (or add "Host join and revocation | P4.13, P4.4a") with "a replayed registration token, a credential for another host or org, and a terminated host's `host.hello` are refused; termination closes the open tunnel and marks its VMs lost".

**Validation to add**: The unit and API tests are N109's (1)–(5) and N123's (1)–(4); this finding adds their acceptance record: the P4.13 and P4.4a tracker Notes cite the exact-head g5-ci-rust run that executed them, and the docker-ci host lane boots a real `g5d host` with a redeemed token, replays the same token from a second container and asserts 401 plus exactly one `worker_hosts` row, then `DELETE …/:host?force=true` and asserts the first container's tunnel closes and its reconnect is 401.

## Disposition of the v8 security review

This section takes each finding of the [v8 security review](lean-backend-design-v8-security-review.md) (S1 to S6 and the passt/metadata validation item), searches the seven v9 documents for the text that addresses it, and classifies the result as resolved in the contract, explicitly deferred with an owning PR, mentioned without a mechanism, or silent; the design itself states at [design §0](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 28 that "this revision does not claim that review's findings are implemented or closed", and the [decision record](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 45 repeats that "the planned production-boundary security gates remain required".

| v8 finding | v9 status | Supporting v9 text (with line refs) | This review requires |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 (P1): workspace read permissions do not isolate shared Git objects | Mentioned without a mechanism. The upload-pack contract is word for word the v8 one; the guest-cache half is stated as a rule with no serving or filtering mechanism; no PR owns the fix. V9 raises the stakes because every non-owner read now flows through internal Git ([design §0](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 19). | [design §5.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 272: "any authorized reader for upload-pack, JWT as today". [design §5.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 297: "`git-upload-pack` requires workspace read access (§5.1)." [design §10.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 922: "Read access includes authorized durable Git/metadata browsing, clone/fetch and forking". [design §5.5](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 349: "clone or reflink each member repo from the internal store (the host's bare cache is an optimization)". [design §6.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 546: "Scope private cache access by organization/repository authority; a child gets only its pinned Git content". [worker-host-api §4](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 157: "a guest never mounts a shared bare cache or obtains arbitrary other-workspace refs/objects through it" (a rule, no mechanism). [plan P4.3a](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 585: "internal git reads remain unchanged"; line 589 tests only that "invite removal revokes read/clone/fetch". [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 672 fences upload-pack by `vm_id` only.
