## Improvements to the design as a specification for coding agents

Each PR is implemented by an agent with no shared memory, so every sentence two agents can read differently is a specification defect. Recommendations are ordered by how many PRs would otherwise resolve the ambiguity independently.

### P1-1 Replace the lifecycle diagrams with (state, event) transition tables

Rationale. [design §6.1](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 467–469 draws five VM states and §5.8 lists transitions by action name, so P4.5 alone decides what a stop during `starting` or a reconciler tick over `stopping` does. [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 694–695 leaves `queued or waiting` to the reader, `waiting` has three meanings under an undefined `hold_reason` (N52), and the attempt reasons at line 665 (`timeout`, `exit_code`) do not match `AgentTurnOutcomeV1.status` at [agent-api-surface §4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 384–385 (N80).

Change. One normative table per stateful record: rows are current status (plus `hold_reason` for tasks), columns are events, cells hold the exact compare-and-swap predicate, the next status and the side effects; zero rows affected means the event is stale and dropped. `task_attempts.reason` becomes the outcome enum plus `vm_lost` and `start_unconfirmed`.

Original, [design §7.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 694–695:

> running | waiting ──continuable attempt ended──► queued or waiting (no live attempt; held)
> waiting (held) ──authorized Continue/Resume──► queued (same task, next attempt)

Rewrite:

| status / hold_reason | event | guard | next | side effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| running, waiting/response_timeout | outcome `interrupted` | continuation policy allows | waiting/drain_continuation when a still-valid drain or rollout authorization exists (N25), else waiting/owner_resume_required | `continuation_pending := true`; `continuation_reason := interrupted`; invalidate pending `provider_requests` |
| waiting/drain_continuation, waiting/owner_resume_required, waiting/owner_stop, waiting/idle_stop, waiting/vm_lost | `owner.continue` | actor is the current owner, workspace `running` | queued | same task, session, job and dependencies; `continuation_pending := false` |

`waiting` MUST carry a `hold_reason` from N52's set, `owner_stop | idle_stop | transfer | owner_resume_required | response_timeout | vm_lost | drain_continuation | publication_pending` (the only `hold_reason` enumeration in this review); every other status MUST carry NULL.

### P1-2 Define the durable Start/Resume authorization record

Rationale. "Recorded current-owner Start/Resume intent" gates `resume` ([design §6.1 transition table](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 474), the dispatch loop (line 719) and compound actions (line 438). No table holds it; §5.1 has only the negative `owner_resume_required` (line 194), and [plan P4.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) item 13 says "Persist lifecycle/start authorization" without a name, so P4.1, P4.5 and P4.6 would each invent one (N21, N22, N25, N36). The record is defined once, in N25; this section only places it in the design text.

Original, [design §6.1 transition table](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 474:

> | resume | the Workspace lifecycle service, on recorded current-owner Start/Resume intent or an authorized rollout continuation (§7.3) | ...

Rewrite (the DDL is N25's, verbatim):

> `workspace_start_authorizations(id, workspace_id, org_id, owner_user_id, kind resume|restart|compound_start|rollout_continuation, launch_task_id NULL, operation_ref NULL, lifecycle_generation bigint NOT NULL, status pending|queued_for_capacity|starting|consumed|expired|cancelled|failed, attempts int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at, expires_at, resolved_at NULL, consumed_vm_id NULL, superseded_reason stop|transfer|purpose_complete|loss NULL, error NULL)` with a partial unique index on `workspace_id` where `status IN (pending, queued_for_capacity, starting)`. Written only by `POST /workspaces/:id/{resume,restart}`, the compound Start and the P8.7 rollout orchestration after the owner check. The `resume` transition consumes one still-valid row (N25's predicate) in the same transaction as the CAS on `workspace_vms`; only that transaction clears `owner_resume_required`. Transfer commit, user stop and purpose completion cancel open rows with `superseded_reason`. `queued_for_capacity` is a status of this row, not of `workspace_vms` (N21). The dispatch loop MUST NOT call `workspace.runtime.ensure` without such a row.

### P1-3 One error-code catalogue

Rationale. `WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING`, `WORKSPACE_STOPPING` and `WORKSPACE_BUSY` have no status or payload, Save names "a stale-incarnation conflict" without a code ([design §9.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 864–866), proxies use `403 stale_incarnation`, and [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 678 mints `WORKSPACE_LEGACY_EXECUTION_ACTIVE`, absent from the design. P4.10 and the CLI cannot be written against codes minted per PR (N80).

Change. `docs/spec/error-catalog.yaml` with code, transport and status, payload schema, retryable, emitted_by and user_action, plus a stated spelling convention.

Original, [design §9.2](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 864:

> `PUT /workspaces/:id/files/*path` carries `expected_hash` and `expected_vm_id`; return `WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING` before any VM call when unattached, `WORKSPACE_STOPPING` during teardown and a stale-incarnation conflict if attachment changed.

Rewrite:

> `PUT /workspaces/:id/files/*path` MUST fail in this order, before any VM call: `403 NOT_WORKSPACE_OWNER`; `409 WORKSPACE_NOT_RUNNING {status}`; `409 WORKSPACE_STOPPING {stop_reason, stop_deadline_at}`; `409 STALE_INCARNATION {expected_vm_id, current_vm_id, epoch}`; `409 WORKSPACE_BRANCH_MISMATCH {repo, expected, actual}`; then on the VM `409 FILE_HASH_MISMATCH {path, current_hash, content}`. A route MUST NOT emit a code missing from `docs/spec/error-catalog.yaml` (checked by `scripts/check-spec.py`).

### P1-4 One timer table; resolve the lease-arming contradiction in place

Rationale. `lease_ttl_secs`, `start_deadline_secs`, `grace_secs` and `wall_clock_ms` have no default anywhere, although the lease TTL is the parameter behind N4, the slot count behind N86 and retention behind N11. [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 554 arms the host timer from the time the renewal left the host; [worker-host-api §3.3](worker-host-api-v9.md) line 112 resets it "from receipt", so P4.4b implemented from the note is unsafe.

Change. `docs/spec/timers.yaml` (name, default, unit, scope, enforced_by, data_source, change_effect, PR); line 554 says in place that it overrides the note; the dispatcher `health` report carries `last_activity_at` (line 414) and the API stores the maximum.

Original, [design §6.4](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 554:

> The host arms its timer from the *monotonic time at which the renewal request left the host* plus the TTL, not from the reply's arrival, so the host's deadline never outlives the grant.

Rewrite:

> **Lease bound (overrides worker-host-api-v9.md §3.3 `lease.renew`).** The host MUST arm its timer from the monotonic time the renewal left the host plus `lease_ttl_secs` (timers.yaml: default 300 s per N99's timing table, control-plane config, enforced by the host `LeaseTimer` and the API reconciler). Control-plane deploy downtime MUST stay below `lease_ttl_secs` (N4).

### P1-5 Interface-freeze blocks per producing PR; prompts cite only the current spec

Rationale. Prompts name routes without shapes ([plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 675), `runtime.ready` drifts between P4.2a and P4.5 (N85), the host note is stale (N108) and P4.11 calls surfaces its dependents own (N68). [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 664 sends the agent to [design v7 §0](lean-backend-design-v7.md) line 27, "Save resumes before it checks the branch", which v9 forbids at line 864.

Change. Each producing PR gets a fenced `interface-freeze` block (method and path, auth predicate, schema ids, error codes, DDL, enums); consumers cite `Freeze: P4.5#routes`; a change lists its consumers. Plan §2 item 3 adds that prompts cite only v9 and its companion notes.

Original, [plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 664:

> ... docs/agent-api-surface-v9.md §4.1, and historical design v7 §0 (docs/lean-backend-design-v7.md) item 9 (Save ordering).

Rewrite:

> ... docs/agent-api-surface-v9.md §4.1, docs/spec/state-machines.md#workspace_vms, docs/spec/error-catalog.yaml, docs/spec/timers.yaml. Save ordering is design v9 §9.2 steps 1 to 6. Do not read superseded drafts for behavior; v7 §0 item 9 let Save resume a VM, which v9 forbids.

### P2-1 Glossary and one enum source

Rationale. "Receipt" names five records ([design §5.8](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 431) and "admission" five gates. `WorkspaceRuntimeHandleV1.state` is `starting|ready|stopping|failed` with `workspace_epoch` ([agent-api-surface §4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 123) while `workspace_vms.status` is `stopped|starting|running|stopping|failed` with `epoch` (line 234); settings have two homes (lines 976, 984); stop reasons differ across four tables (N81); DDL differs from P4.1 (N74, N51); org roles are inconsistent (N78).

Change. `docs/spec/glossary.md` (term, definition, authoritative record, owner, not to be confused with) and `docs/spec/enums.yaml` generating the Rust and TypeScript enums with a CI diff check. Keep `configs`, delete `workspace_settings`.

Original, [agent-api-surface §4](agent-api-surface-v9.md) line 123:

> state: "starting" | "ready" | "stopping" | "failed";

Rewrite:

> status: VmStatus; // enums.yaml#vm_status, identical to workspace_vms.status; a handle exists only for starting|running|stopping
> epoch: number; // same name and value as workspace_vms.epoch

### P2-2 Invariants and validation as identified, testable rows

Rationale. [design §4.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) lines 169–181 lists thirteen invariants in prose and [design §15](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1063 packs about twenty scenarios into one bullet; prompts say "add the acceptance tests named in the prompt" without names (N98), and the checkpoint has no artifact to review (N95).

Change. Ids `INV-nn`, bundled invariants split, each as Given/When/Then with test ids, layer and PR; §15 becomes `docs/spec/test-matrix.md`; test names contain the id; `check-spec.py` fails an invariant without a test.

Original, [design §4.3](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 179:

> 11. **Nothing crosses the VM boundary at-most-once.** Starts, answers, events and outcomes are persisted on one side and confirmed by the other; duplicates are detected by key, never by ordering (§7.5).

Rewrite:

> **INV-11a Start is two-phase.** Given a task `running, start_confirmed=false, vm_id=V`, when the API restarts before a disposition, then dispatch re-sends `modules.invoke` with the same `exec_id` and never creates `attempt+1` while V is current. Tests T-7.5.1 (api), T-7.5.2 (docker-ci). PR P4.6.
> **INV-11b Duplicates are dropped by key.** Given `task_events(task_id, attempt, vm_id, seq)` present, when the event is replayed, then no row is inserted and no transition fires. Tests T-7.5.3 (unit), T-7.5.4 (api). PR P4.6.

### P2-3 Separate the specification from its history; use RFC 2119 keywords

Rationale. §0, §3 and §18 plus phrases like "(removed by this revision)" (line 84) require knowing eight drafts to tell current from removed behavior. "never" appears over a hundred times, and "cannot" means both a check to write (line 866) and a consequence (line 555); agents implement MUST NOT and skip consequences.

Change. Move §0, §3.1, §3.2 and §18 to `docs/decisions/` (format at [ADR 0001](decisions/0001-repository-layout.md)), keep one rationale pointer per section, add a conformance clause.

Original, [design §0](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 28:

> V8's retained contracts remain: exactly one owner; transfer stops/flushes/releases before changing owner and requires new-owner Resume; ...

Rewrite:

> Status: normative. Every sentence is a requirement unless the paragraph starts with *Note* or *Rationale*; MUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD and MAY follow RFC 2119. History lives in docs/decisions/. (§5.1:) A workspace has exactly one owner (`owner_user_id`); only the owner MUTATES it, and read access never implies write access. Rationale: ADR-0009.

### P3-1 JSON schemas for payloads described in prose

Rationale. The result payload ([design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 446) has no field names, byte bound or schema id although P4.14, P6.1 and the CLI read it (N19, N20); `AgentTurnInput` is declared twice and the design copy (lines 784–789) already lacks `schema_version` and `workspace_request_id` from [agent-api-surface §5](agent-api-surface-v9.md) lines 199–227.

Change. `docs/spec/schemas/` (JSON Schema 2020-12) named by schema id with `maxLength` and `x-max-bytes`; duplicated sketches become the id.

Original, [design §5.9](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 446:

> V9 chooses Postgres `workspace_results` as the immutable canonical result, with a versioned bounded payload containing summary, structured findings or selected explainer output, source-manifest digest, producer task/attempt and optional reviewed SHA.

Rewrite:

> `workspace_results.payload` MUST validate against `g5.workspace.result/v1` (`schema_version`, `kind review|explainer`, `summary` Markdown, `source_manifest_digest`, `findings[≤ result_max_findings]` of `g5.workspace.result-finding/v1`) and MUST NOT exceed `result_payload_max_bytes` in canonical JSON; the limits are N20's (1 MiB payload, 500 findings, 64 KiB reply, 20 candidates per attempt) and are stated once in §17.1, not here. Producer task, attempt, request id and reviewed SHA are columns, not payload fields.

## Delivery process recommendations

### Review protocol for AI reviewers

The reviewer is the other model (Codex for a Claude implementation and vice versa), works from its own worktree at the PR head sha and treats the description as claims to reproduce. Rounds are capped at five unless the program owner exempts the PR; the verdict is a `VERDICT: APPROVE|REQUEST_CHANGES @ <sha>` line and any push voids it. Each round:

1. Re-run, do not read: the compile and lint ladder, unit tests for touched crates, the acceptance greps and the status-row check. A "Validation run" line the reviewer cannot reproduce blocks approval.
2. Scope audit of `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` against the prompt's Do and Do-not lists; a silently omitted numbered item or an unrelated cleanup is a finding even when correct.
3. Run every deleted test at HEAD from a scratch copy: deleted protocol, covered at HEAD by a named test, or regression.
4. Mutation-run at least three new tests and every test cited for a [plan §7.1](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) row: an env-gated mutant removes the guarded check, one build serves control and mutant, FAILED under the mutant is required.
5. Replay the fault matrix (prompt bullets, design §15, plan §7.1) with the gate-and-cancel pattern of [branch_commit_landing.rs](../backend/g5-api/tests/branch_commit_landing.rs); rows without a test are recorded gaps or the round is REQUEST_CHANGES.
6. Extract implemented codes, enums and route tables mechanically and lay them beside the P1-1, P1-3 and P2-1 tables; every mismatch is a finding and the design wins.
7. For boundary PRs, run the conformance suite from the opposite side and paste the result. Where the prompt claims a sole writer, require a guard in [check-execution-lifecycle-writers.py](../scripts/check-execution-lifecycle-writers.py) or a sibling and show it fails when a second writer is added.
8. Ask what the restored invariant newly enables and probe it: old-owner tokens after transfer, tokens without a `vm_id` claim, retired branch-minting helpers still reachable.
9. After a rebase, verify the merge-tree proof and equal test-declaration counts before restating approval.

The track owner reads the final summary, coverage matrix and gap list, confirms both lanes green on the exact head, and hands to the program owner. Nobody merges on "CI green" or "review complete" alone. A red lane outside the diff is excusable only for a documented flake re-run to green (N61).

### Drift and status controls

1. Scope manifest: the description carries the prompt's authorized path globs; a Platform-owned `scripts/check-pr-scope.py` step in both lanes fails on any path outside them unless named under "Blocked / deviations" with a capability owner.
2. Prompt pin: the sha256 of the §6 prompt block at branch time; a docs PR editing a prompt lists in-flight PRs, which re-pin.
3. Five-state ladder as a mandatory section with fixed wording: Implemented, Locally validated (commands and exit codes), Exact-head CI validated (build ids on the sha), Deployed ("no", plan §2 item 11), Observed (who, where, when). A body lint rejects "verified", "works" and "done" outside it unless followed by a command.
4. Coverage matrix, acceptance gaps, removed-test table and mutation table as mandatory sections; reviewer outcomes are appended so the merged description is the audit record; tracker Notes repeat the open gap and marker counts.
5. Tracker semantics in CI: the status-file diff touches only the row matching the title prefix, that row's Branch is empty on `origin/main`, Depends-on is unchanged unless §5.1 changes, no row says in progress; every `LEAN-TODO(P<id>)` names a §5.1 id whose Branch is empty.
6. Design-wins rule: a prompt disagreeing with the design is corrected in a one-line docs PR referenced from the deviation; a design change needs a `docs/decisions/` entry approved before the code PR.
7. Codify all of this in `docs/delivery-process.md`; today the round cap, the mutation and merge-tree recipes and "never merge before approve on the current head" live in one operator's memory. Multi-session PRs commit one numbered Do item at a time with the item number in the subject.
8. Weekly snapshot: per open PR the head sha, lane conclusions on that sha, latest VERDICT sha and open findings; a head moved since its verdict is "needs re-review".

### Gating the first runtime PRs across three owners without stubs

The boundary is control plane (P4.5, P4.13) to host `ctl` (P4.4a, P4.4b) to dispatcher `rpc` (P4.2a, P4.2b), three people, no stand-ins ([plan §5.3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 308). N70 shows API lifecycle tests have no host-protocol peer, N43 that no PR builds the CI VM image, N69 that the new crates run in no lane before P8.6, N72 that P4.12 needs a provider turn it does not depend on. The sentence the plan allows, "test harnesses that drive a real component from its tests are not stand-ins", becomes these artifacts.

1. Recorded protocol fixtures as the shared contract, landed by the first PR that defines each frame, mirroring [record.sh](../tools/fake-provider-cli/record.sh) and `tests/fixtures/provider-frames/`: `tests/fixtures/runtime-protocol/rpc/` from P4.2a and `.../ctl/` from P4.4a, each with its version constant and a golden JSON per frame. A fixture is data produced by the real implementation.
2. One conformance crate (`backend/g5-runtime-protocol-tests`, Platform-owned, created by P4.2a, extended by P4.4a): a fixture client speaking recorded frames to a real server and a fixture server answering a real client. The same code runs on both sides, so divergence is caught by whichever PR lands second.
3. Harness levels a prompt names and a reviewer enforces: L0 in-process; L1 `g5d dispatch` as a child process over a loopback mux (P4.2a, P4.2b, P4.3a, P4.5, published as a testkit dev-dependency); L2 a libkrun-booted VM in docker-ci Phase 4 (P4.4b, P4.12, P5.x), with the CI runtime image built by P4.4b (N43); L3 the e2e stack with fake providers (P4.6 onward). A claim proven below the named level is refused.
4. Merge order: P4.1a, then P4.2a and P4.4a in parallel, then P4.4b as the first PR running both real processes, then P4.13, then P4.5. P4.5 branches only when P4.4b's isolation-lane boot test and both conformance suites are green on `origin/main`. New crates join the rust lane in the PR that creates them (N69); P4.9a retargets the e2e lane in the same PR (N7).
5. Contract changes: CODEOWNERS on `backend/g5-internal-api`, the dispatcher method table and the fixtures require a reviewer from the other side; a change bumps the version, re-records the fixture, updates the suite and lists dependent in-flight PRs. Fakes stay at real boundaries (exact-pin provider binaries, an HTTP fake upstream for P4.12); no development mock of dispatcher, host or facade, the default the meeting left standing ([v9-meeting-decisions](v9-meeting-decisions.md) line 41).
6. Integration receipt: when P4.5 merges, the program owner runs the Runtime milestone check of [plan §7](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) on `origin/main` and records it in P4.5's Notes; nothing downstream starts before that.

### The grouping checkpoint

[plan §5.3](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) line 320 and [design §13](lean-backend-design-v9.md) line 1003 defer consolidation and the mock decision to a checkpoint nothing defines (N95).

Who: the program owner chairs and merges; the three track owners decide; the Git and governance assignee joins once named; one agent prepares the packet and writes the docs PR.

When: checkpoint 1 when P4.1, P4.2a and P4.4a are merged, before P4.5 branches; checkpoint 2 when P4.5 and P4.11 are merged, before P4.6, P4.7 and P4.8; checkpoint 3 when P4.10 merges, before P4.14 and P4.9a/b; then only on a trigger: a PR past three sessions, a boundary version bump, or a lane red on `main` for more than a working day. One hour, prepared packet.

Inputs: per merged PR the changed lines, sessions, rounds, findings by severity and time from dependencies-merged to merge; per open PR the same plus head, VERDICT sha and open findings; the critical path recomputed from §5.1 with actual durations, ranked by longest remaining path to an MVP exit node (N104); the drift ledger of deviations, stale pins and design-wins corrections; conformance status and fixture versions; carried gaps and markers by target PR; the split proposals below; the mock question with default answer no.

Outputs, one docs PR titled with the checkpoint id and merged the same day: updated §5.1 rows, sizes and tracker Depends-on cells for every split (a/b ids, never reused); an updated §5.3 table with the new critical chain written out; `docs/decisions/<date>-checkpoint-N.md` listing each decision, its evidence line and what it does not decide; the explicit mock decision; prompt corrections; the next wave's start gate as observable facts.

Rules: split when a prompt exceeds about 900 words or eight numbered items, or a PR has consumed three sessions without an openable state, and only at a seam where the first half is real merged behavior; consolidate only within a track when neither half can be validated at its harness level alone; never decide by line count.

### PR sizing

Word counts are of the §6 prompt blocks; P4.5's is about 2,800 words with items 0 to 16 ([plan P4.5](lean-implementation-plan-v9.md) lines 661–699).

| PR | Issue | Proposal |
|---|---|---|
| P4.1 (M) | One migration of about fifteen tables with backfills is the single root of the MVP graph (N103); ownership backfill needs an operator mapping (N58); the dual-write window has no reconciliation marker (N67) | Per N103: P4.1a, P4.1b, P4.1c, P4.1d as N103 assigns them, with P4.4a and P4.13 depending on P4.1b only; the N67 marker rides with P4.1d |
| P4.5 (XL) | Lifecycle, fence, facade, transfer, sole fork service with recovery, mapping cutover, purpose hooks and a Playwright dialog in one PR (N62); the transfer guard keys on "active" legacy execution and omits the frozen resolver (N59) | Per N62: P4.5a fork service, mapping cutover and the shared Create/Fork dialog; P4.5b lifecycle, fence, owner authorization and facade; P4.5c transfer (with the N59 resolver freeze in its steps), legacy-work blocker and purpose-child hooks. P4.6 depends on P4.5b |

P8.9 is unblocked now and conflicts with later frontend PRs, so it goes first in its column (N105) |

## Strengths

- Invariant 12 (§4 line 180) and §5.1 line 251 state the write seat precisely: `owner_user_id` alone is the authority for human writes, `created_by` is immutable provenance, and read access never implies write access; the plan repeats it in P4.1, P4.5 and P6.4 with an explicit "no interim writer-role or creator bypass" instruction.
- Reader forks are contractually isolated from the source VM: §0 principle 4 (line 22) and §5.8 line 392 require a pinned durable Git snapshot and forbid asking the source VM to flush or quiesce, and the §15 Sharing bullet (line 1080) makes "readers see only durable Git repository data regardless of source VM state" a boundary test. This closes the v8 reader-preview escape cleanly.
- The §5.3 receive-pack table enumerates identities × ref namespaces including the `refs/g5/forks/<fork id>` and `refs/g5/merges/<merge op id>` namespaces (lines 286–287), and the rewrite rule at line 295 with P4.3a step 7 forbids zero-old-OID creation for network identities, which makes the fork service the only branch creator in a testable way.
- Immutable per-flush refs plus an API-verified receipt (§5.4 step 4, line 329: `git for-each-ref` on each mirror before `complete_at`) and the rule that `last_flush_id` moves only at release (§5.8 step 7, line 432) close the v7 mutable-snapshot bypass and give restore a single authority.
- Observing the internal head with `git ls-remote` instead of `git fetch` (§5.4 step 1, line 316) preserves the agent's `--force-with-lease` lease semantics; the design correctly states that the server cannot prove lease safety and assigns it to the profile as a client obligation (line 295, §14 line 1050).
- Warm roots are always materialized from the receipt with no path/HEAD equality shortcut (§5.5 line 355: steps 3 and 4 are idempotent and untracked non-ignored paths absent from the snapshot are removed), addressing v7 review R5; the P4.3a same-second same-size test targets the exact failure mode.
- `vm.stop` fenced by ownership only, returning `not_found` and never `stale` (§6.2 line 503), resolves v7 R4 and is consistent with the deadline/idempotency-key rule for every VM-scoped command (line 494), so the release-before-stop race cannot recur.
- The lease bound is stated with the correct monotonic-departure rule (§6.4 line 554: the host arms its timer from the monotonic time the renewal left the host, not the reply's arrival), repeated in the §14 risk table (line 1025) and the §18 register (line 1183); the v7 review's V7-3 is genuinely addressed in the text, and N4 above concerns only the unspecified TTL value.
- R1 of the v7 review is closed consistently: `delivered` follows the adapter's journaled `input.accepted`, never the module `started` disposition, in §8.1 (line 768), the §14 risk table (line 1021) and the §18 register (line 1200), with plan P4.6 step 7 and P4.11 step 6 repeating it.
- Execution identity and at-least-once semantics are coherent: `exec_id = (task_id, attempt, vm_id)` (§6.6 line 607, §7.5 line 732), insert-ignore on `(task_id, attempt, vm_id, seq)` with cumulative acknowledgements and ordered replay (§7.1 line 672, §7.5 line 735) give an implementer a precise, testable contract for start/cancel/replay races.
- The value loop's core safety property is stated in §5.9 line 450: `result submit` acknowledges only a durably captured, immutable candidate in `workspace_result_submissions` keyed by `(request_id, task_id, attempt, idempotency_key)` (§5.1 line 225), never final publication, so an implementer cannot read a tool call as completing the request.
- Loss is always made visible and never silent: deadline-bound teardowns that proceed without a receipt set `unflushed_loss_at` (§5.4 line 338) and release records either `last_flush_id` or `unflushed_loss_at` in one update (§5.8 step 7, line 432), which is the right operability posture for an accepted-loss design.

## Review limits

No runtime exists for anything from design §5.4 onward, so no penetration test was run, no fixture was executed and no CI lane was exercised against v9 behavior; every finding rests on the text of the eight documents, the retained code they cite and probes in isolated scratch repositories. The review considered 185 candidate findings from twelve reviewers, merged into 151 distinct candidates, of which 36 were refuted on evidence, 7 were judged immaterial and 0 could not be verified. The refuted candidates were mostly claims that the text is silent or contradictory where a cited line settles the point (ownership of the publication barrier, the `vm.stop` retry semantics, the reader-cutover instructions, the projection loop's cost, the transfer audit key), claims that mischaracterized retained code (the guest-side `llm_proxy.rs`, the retained receive-pack scope), and PR-sizing or scheduling opinions without a concrete defect. The immaterial ones were vocabulary drift, stale change-log phrasing inside normative sections and duplicated item numbers that no implementing agent could misread. A second round examined host registration and identity, the repository-selected image and runtime directory, inbound provider mirroring, and the nudge and its notices; it added N109 to N125 and rejected candidates of three classes: inherit-or-re-resolve questions that the cited image and preview rules already answer for a review child; host-credential revocation, identity-carrier equality and GCE bootstrap-delivery items that describe the same host-join boundary as N109 and N123 without a distinct implementer decision, and are covered by N125's coverage rows; and conflict outcomes for later additions to `protected_branches` that N116's rule (5) settles. Closing any finding requires an edit to the design or plan plus the named regression on the eventual implementation; this document itself validates nothing.
